# Chang Mou — Portfolio 2026

## Overview
Product designer portfolio. Static HTML/CSS/JS site (no framework). Dark mode, minimal, trendy 2026 aesthetic.

## Structure
- `index.html` — Home (hero + work grid, 6 projects, 1344px content width). Work cards can link to internal case studies (coffee/pollen/nova/homewise) or external live projects (StoryBloom → storybloom.tech/app, Credit Analysis Agent → credit-analysis-agent.vercel.app, target="_blank"). **CURRENT ORDER as of 2026-08-16 (supersedes the row-by-row history below AND the 2026-07-28 order): Homewise · PollenNav / Countersign · StoryBloom / Coffee Chat · NOVA / Mercor · New Craft Society** — Coffee Chat ↔ Countersign swapped 2026-08-16 per the user, plain HTML block moves with no rationale attached. **This is the THIRD time this exact pair has been swapped** (done 2026-06-18, reverted 2026-06-23, and explicitly listed on 2026-07-28 among the swaps "considered and deliberately NOT made" as churn) — so the pair is evidently NOT settled, and the 2026-07-28 do-not-redo note is void. Consequences: (a) the `--plate-*` vars are inline on each `<a class="project-card">`, so the mint #CDE3DA and lavender #D0D1FD plates travel with their cards automatically — verified computed after the swap, nothing to hand-edit; (b) `project-card--desktop-only` travels with Countersign too, so the desktop grid still shows 8 and mobile still shows 7; (c) **the mobile stack DOES change, contrary to the 2026-06-18 note that called this pair a desktop-only reorder** — Coffee Chat moves from 3rd to 4th on mobile (now Homewise · PollenNav · StoryBloom · Coffee Chat · NOVA · Mercor · New Craft Society), because hiding Countersign removes a slot ahead of Coffee Chat rather than beside it. Verified at 375: 7 cards, correct order, zero horizontal overflow. HTML-only, no CSS or version bump. Two swaps that day, both per the user, both plain HTML block moves with no rationale attached: **Coffee Chat ↔ StoryBloom** (row 2) and **NOVA ↔ Mercor** (rows 3-4 boundary). Consequences worth knowing: (a) row 2's visual rhythm flipped — StoryBloom is the full-bleed cover and Coffee Chat is a contained screenshot, so row 1's two contained plates now continue one card further before the full-bleed break; (b) **both swaps change the MOBILE stack too** (unlike the 2026-06-18 Coffee↔Countersign swap, which was desktop-only because Countersign is `--desktop-only`) — the phone order is now Homewise · PollenNav · Coffee Chat · StoryBloom · NOVA · Mercor · New Craft Society; (c) NOVA and Mercor are BOTH the documented flat-`#FFFFFF`-plate exception, so they simply traded slots and neither needed a plate change — verified both still render `rgb(255,255,255)` with no `.project-img--lit`. **The Mercor "promote only after its cover and destination are fixed" note below still stands** — this swap moved it 6→7, which is away from the grid opening, so it is not the promotion that note guards against. The only change that day: **Mercor moved 8 → 6**, pushing NOVA 6→7 and New Craft 7→8. Rationale: Mercor is the most role-relevant credential on the site (frontier AI lab, RLHF, 2026) and was sitting dead last, 7 of 7 on mobile. It went to **6, not row 1, on purpose** — its cover is a bare wordmark on a flat white plate, which would read as a logo slide interrupting the opening row next to Homewise's dashboard screenshot. Position 6 puts it mid-grid (5 of 7 on mobile) without asking a weak cover to carry row 1. **Promote it further only after its cover and destination are fixed** (it still links to mercor.com, a third party's page with none of Chang's work — the documented unsolved issue). **Two swaps were considered and deliberately NOT made**, because both are settled decisions and re-doing them is churn: Coffee Chat ↔ Countersign (done 2026-06-18, reverted 2026-06-23) and Homewise ↔ StoryBloom (settled 2026-06-15). The organizing principle is a **quality gradient, not chronology**: rows 1-2 are the most complete stories (shipped / award-winning), row 3 is supporting work, row 4 is the in-progress product. Grid order is curated, not chronological (2026-06-11 audit): row 1 = Homewise + PollenNav, then StoryBloom + Coffee Chat (**Homewise ↔ StoryBloom swapped 2026-06-15** per the user — Homewise now leads the top-left "10-second scan" spot and the award-winning StoryBloom moved to row 2; the earlier rationale had StoryBloom + PollenNav up top for their award badges, so revisit if the badge placement matters more than leading with Homewise), then Countersign + NOVA in the last row (Countersign is the credit-analysis agent, added 2026-06-12 and retitled from "Credit Analysis Agent" to the repo's real name same day; card copy mirrors the repo thesis "the agent analyzes, a human countersigns"; mint plate #DCEFE8/#B9D6CB/#CDE3DA from its UI's teal accents; repo: github.com/chang627627/credit-analysis-agent). **Coffee Chat ↔ Countersign swapped 2026-06-18 (per user), then swapped BACK 2026-06-23 (per user):** the home grid's row 2 is now **StoryBloom + Coffee Chat** and row 3 is **Countersign + NOVA** (the 2026-06-18 change had put Countersign in row 2 / Coffee Chat in row 3; 2026-06-23 reverted it). Since Countersign is mobile-hidden, this is a desktop-only reorder — the visible mobile stack is unchanged. HTML-only, no CSS/version bump. **Countersign is also hidden on mobile** via a new `.project-card--desktop-only` class on its card + a `@media (max-width:768px){ display:none }` rule in style.css (mirrors play.html's `.play-card--desktop-only`), so the mobile stack is Homewise · PollenNav · StoryBloom · Coffee Chat · NOVA (5 cards; desktop keeps all 6). **TickerPulse was removed from the home grid 2026-06-14** and its 5 final-design wireframes moved to the Play page (carousel) — this dropped the grid from 7 cards to 6 (clean 3×2) and paired NOVA (previously alone in the last row) with Countersign; ticker.html still exists but is no longer linked from home (see its bullet). **Homewise's card gained a `Shipped` status chip 2026-06-14** (`Home Services · Dashboard · Shipped`) — user's explicit call; note it sits in tension with the case study's deliberate "prototype, not product" framing, so keep the chip unless the user revisits it. **Card image conventions:** thumbnails export at 1920px wide (dimension-matching, PNG lossless / JPEG 100 — NOT full-res exports; an 8MB thumbnail killed first-load until resized). Row-1 card images load eager; all below-fold card images get `loading="lazy" decoding="async"`. **New Craft Society added as the 7th card 2026-07-22 (per user)** — sits alone in row 4 after Countersign + NOVA, external link → newcraftsociety.com (target="_blank"), chips `Freelance · Design Community · Web App · Building` (the `Building` status chip is the user's call — the product is in active development; **type chip `Personal` → `Freelance` 2026-07-31 per user**, the first use of the Freelance label). Cover `images/newcraft-card.png` (1920px PNG) is a **full-bleed screenshot of the logged-in app home** (watercolor bg + wordmark) — the user pasted it in chat from clipboard; it was recovered from the session transcript JSONL (`~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session>.jsonl` stores pasted images as base64 — useful trick when a paste has no file). The public logged-out site shows a dark typing intro instead, so do NOT try to re-capture this cover headlessly. Card visible on mobile (mobile stack is now 6: … · NOVA · New Craft Society). Copy grounded in the site's own thesis ("captures your process as it happens", "the case study writes itself"); `api/chat.js` PORTFOLIO list got a matching entry (labeled newest, in active development). **Card outcome pass 2026-07-26 (improvement-research P1, PARTIAL per user):** Homewise's description became "AI command center for homeowners. Prototyped in code, shipped by the dev team, managing repairs from one workspace" (the prototype→product beat surfaced from the case study's Collaboration section; consistent with the honesty framing — Chang built the prototype, the dev team shipped the product) and NOVA's gained ", tested with 20+ parents and kids" (from the resume). **The suggested StoryBloom `Live`-for-`Award Winner` chip swap and Countersign `Live Demo` chip were DECLINED by the user ("do not touch others") — do not re-suggest.** Chang is also **one of the product engineers on New Craft Society** (2026-07-26, per user) — recorded in `api/chat.js` + `llms.txt`, deliberately NOT on the card (a third builds-in-code claim in one viewport reads as overclaiming; hero + Homewise card already carry it). **Mercor added as the 8th card 2026-07-23 (per user)** — pairs with New Craft Society in row 4 (desktop grid back to a clean 4×2; mobile stack 7). External link → **www.mercor.com/mission/** (target="_blank"; link iterated homepage → /mission/ → /experts/ (2026-07-23) → **back to /mission/ 2026-07-26, user's call after reconsidering**. The reasoning that won: /experts/ opens on "$119/hr average rate" + role listings + Apply buttons, which reframes the role as hourly gig work for a design-hiring reader, while /mission/ opens on the company thesis ("organizing human intelligence to power the AI economy") that makes the card copy land. The earlier "mission is too abstract" objection is real but secondary — the question is which page makes the reader think better of Chang, not which is more concrete. **Known unsolved issue (Mobbin landing benchmark move #4):** BOTH destinations send the strongest frontier-AI credential to a *third party's* marketing page containing none of Chang's work. The proper fix is pointing the card at his own content (About experience section, or a future write-up of the eval work); /mission/ is just the better of two versions of that problem), NOVA-style **flat white plate** + `images/mercor-card.png` at 55% width: the user-pasted Mercor lockup, extracted from the session transcript, trimmed, and **white keyed to transparent alpha** (the paste was JPEG; an opaque white rectangle gets outlined by the `.project-img > img` drop-shadow filter against the white plate — same reason NOVA's plate needs a transparent PNG). Copy from the resume's Mercor role (Product Design Expert, May 2026–Present, kept lab-anonymous like the resume), currently "Expert UX evaluation for a frontier AI lab. Reviewing AI generated interfaces and turning design critique into feedback that teaches models product taste" (copy iterated 2026-07-23: resume-length → shortened per user → **lengthened again per user**, landing on this mid-length version; the middle clause names the day-to-day work the short cut left implicit, and it still fits 2 lines like all 8 cards. The interim "a leading frontier AI lab" was trimmed — user flagged "leading frontier" as redundant, since "frontier" already means the leading edge); chips `Professional · Frontier AI · RLHF · UX Evaluation` (**RLHF chip added 2026-07-23 per user** — user first floated saying "reinforcement learning by human loop" in the copy; corrected to RLHF, and the signal went into the chip row instead of the sentence so the description keeps its plain design language while the chips carry the technical category. Defensible because Mercor's own resources frame expert evaluation as the human-preference input RLHF runs on — mercor.com/resources/experts/what-is-rlhf). The chat prompt's resume section already lists the Mercor role (no chat.js change).
- **Boxless work grid + component-scale stills (2026-09-02, per user "do 1,2,7 first" from the homepage-aesthetics list; PUSHED TO MAIN 2026-09-02 as ea1feff together with the font self-hosting and the hero light source; index.html only, style.css untouched except the @font-face block, index bumped to style.css?v=250 while the other pages stay at v=249 on purpose).** (1) The `.project-card` TILE IS GONE on the home grid: an inline override block at the end of index.html's `<style>` (the documented inline-block precedent) sets the card to `background: transparent; border: 0; overflow: visible`, moves the radius and the oklch hue glow onto `.project-img` (a card with `overflow: hidden` clips its child's box-shadow, so the glow could never spill past the card edge), retires the cursor-following `::before` white haze (the plate light already answers the cursor), hangs `.project-info` at `padding: 18px 0 0` flush with the plate's left edge, and opens the grid to `column-gap: 32px; row-gap: 56px` (40px on mobile). The base `.project-card` rules in style.css still exist and are simply overridden. Reason: eight identical dark tiles were the loudest template tell on the page. Historical note of the deletion follows: THE "SELECTED WORK" HEADER WAS GONE from the home page (the `.section-meta` markup was deleted; the rule-wipe JS is null-guarded and simply does nothing now; `.section-meta`/`.section-rule` CSS in style.css is orphaned on this page) and replaced by an ARRIVAL: row one uses `@keyframes card-arrive` (opacity 0 / translateY(56px) scale(0.96) to rest) on its existing view() timeline over `entry 0% entry 70%`, while rows 2-4 keep the standard card-rise; `.work { padding-top: 148px }` (104px mobile) reproduces the vertical space the header used to occupy so row one still starts below a 900px fold. TALL-SCREEN TRAP HANDLED: a card already inside the viewport at load would sit faded at its start keyframe, so `armArrival()` (after fonts.ready, next to the rule-wipe code) adds `.arrive-now` to any row-one card whose top is above `innerHeight`, which swaps it to a timed 0.9s entrance delayed 1.25s to follow the hero reveal (`animation-timeline: auto` + a real duration; the auto-duration trap does not apply to the timed variant). Verified: 1440x800 scrubs 0 to 0.51 to 1.0 across 700px of scroll, 1440x1080 and 375 use the timed path, zero errors, no overflow. Verified desktop + 375 (no overflow, plate and title both at the page inset, loops arm only on desktop).
- **Neue Montreal is SELF-HOSTED as of 2026-09-02 (fonts/NeueMontreal-Regular.woff2 + Medium.woff2, `@font-face` at the top of style.css ahead of the body rule, `font-display: swap`; index.html preloads the Regular file and no longer links Fontshare).** Why: Fontshare DELISTED the family (its CSS endpoint returns a 1-byte empty stylesheet; fontshare.com/fonts/neue-montreal says "Font family not found"), so from some point before 2026-09-01 every visitor rendered the whole site in the fallback stack (SF Pro on Apple, Arial/Roboto elsewhere), invisible on Chang's Mac because the face is installed locally (`~/Library/Fonts/NeueMontreal-*.otf`, Pangram Pangram v1.000, fsType 8 = editable embedding). The WOFF2s were converted from those OTFs with fontTools (`/usr/bin/python3 -m pip install --user fonttools brotli`; 23-25 KB each). Verified: `document.fonts` reports "Neue Montreal 400 loaded" from the web font. LEFT AS FOLLOW-UPS, deliberately, because the user asked for home-page-only changes: the other 15 HTML files still carry the dead Fontshare `<link>` (harmless, one wasted render-blocking request each) and still reference `style.css?v=249` (they still get the new @font-face because Vercel serves style.css with max-age=0 and revalidates); the four self-contained decks do not load style.css and still render in the fallback until they get their own @font-face (they use weight 500 too, which is why the Medium file exists). LICENSE: the user was told to confirm the Pangram Pangram license covers web embedding on a personal site and said "do"; treat that as his call. Probe gotcha: `curl` treats the `[]` in the Fontshare URL as a glob, use `curl -g`. ALSO FOUND 2026-09-01, still open: the production `deck-comments` store for `coffeeslide` holds six of Chang's own working notes ("speaker note, do not look at the slide for 2 min.", "too simple." etc.), public to anyone who presses C; deleting them is a production data change and awaits his OK.

- **Row scroll effect, v2 (2026-09-02, per user "do 1 and 2. also image left text right" from the scroll-effect suggestions; index.html only, PUSHED TO MAIN 2026-09-02 as b48fbd5).** (a) LAYOUT, FINAL AS OF 2026-09-02 EVENING (PUSHED TO MAIN as 3e2a474): IMAGE LEFT, TEXT RIGHT AGAIN (per user "i think we need to go back to left image right text"), i.e. the b48fbd5 form restored line for line: `.feature-card` is `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 840px) minmax(280px, 1fr); column-gap: 48px; align-items: center` across the full 1344 container: the plate in column 1 is CAPPED AT 840px (473 tall, the still 546 wide; per user "image can be smaller" minutes after the restore, down from the b48fbd5 1016px) and the caption takes the rest of the row (456px at 1440, so "Litespace Coffee Chat" fits one line and descriptions run two; the 280px minimum keeps the caption readable on narrow laptops, where the plate shrinks instead). **"SHEET AND DATUM" CARD REDESIGN (2026-09-02, per user "the whole card need to redesign to look more premium... you can use mobbin mcp" then "do"; index.html only, style.css untouched, NO version bump).** From a 9-agent Mobbin-evidenced workflow (4 directions, 1 adversary each, xhigh judge; references: Freshman, Kinfolk, MOUTHWASH, Telepathic Instruments, Slash, Norma, Dovetail, plus Linear/Vercel/Deel/Raw Materials/Daniel Sun from the earlier pull). THE MEASURED DIAGNOSIS, worth keeping because it names why a big card can read cheap: (a) the capture had NO EDGE, one 28px `drop-shadow` with square corners, which is the literal default output of every deck template and reads as placed rather than photographed; (b) the caption was CENTRED IN A VOID by `align-items: center`, so each title's y was an average of its own text height and no two rows shared a line; (c) THE TYPE SHRANK WHEN THE ARTIFACT GREW, 27px title on a 656px grid card became 26px on an 840px plate and the description silently dropped 16 to 15. **THE THUMBNAIL HALF WAS REVERTED WITHIN THE HOUR (per user "revert all the change on the thumbnail"), SO ONLY THE CAPTION HALF SHIPPED.** The stills keep style.css's own floating `drop-shadow`, square corners, 65% width and the `scale(1.04)` hover zoom, exactly as before. **THIS IS THE FOURTH REJECTION OF A THUMBNAIL CHANGE (component crops, the 84% size-up, and now the sheet treatment which changed only the SHADOW and corner radius, not the size or framing): treat the card stills as frozen. Do not propose any change to them again, including shadows, edges, corner radius, framing, size, or hover transform.** What was built, for the record: (1) SHEET, `.card-sheet` on the three OPAQUE captures plus the two opaque loops (Homewise, Countersign, Coffee): `filter: none`, `border-radius: 6px`, and the single blur split into `0 0 0 1px` edge + `0 1px 2px` contact + `var(--plate-sx) 10px 24px -6px` ambient (so it still leans away from the cursor). **THE OPT-IN IS LOAD-BEARING: PollenNav and NOVA are transparent PNGs and a box-shadow ignores alpha, so it would paint a hard rectangle around empty sky, which is the documented NOVA halo bug. A sheet gets an edge, an object keeps its cast shadow.** The `opacity` entry in the sheet's transition list is also load-bearing: `.card-loop` is (0,1,0) and the sheet rule is (0,2,1), so dropping it turns the loop crossfade into a cut. (2) DATUM, `align-items: start` + a `rgba(255,255,255,0.12)` hairline across the caption column with 20px padding, so every caption hangs off its plate's top edge. (3) TYPE, title `clamp(28px, 2.64vw, 38px)` (38 at 1440, deliberately NOT 44: at 44 "Litespace Coffee Chat" measures ~430px in a 456px column) and description back to 16px. (4) LIP, `inset 0 0 0 1px rgba(12,18,22,0.08)` on every plate, which also finally gives StoryBloom and New Craft a defined edge against the black. **HOVER, THE TRAVELLING RULE (2026-09-02, per user "study case card's hover you can see the line get light up. How to make this effect more interactive? Should it look the same as the link's effect?" then "do"):** the caption's hairline no longer brightens uniformly. It keeps its 0.12 resting line untouched and a `::before` segment at 0.34 travels along it left to right on `scaleX(0)->1`, `transform-origin: left`, over `--dur-base`/`--ease-out`, i.e. the EXACT duration and curve the nav / footer / play / about link underlines use (that recipe appears 4x in style.css). **THE ANSWER TO "should it match the link effect" IS NO, AND THE REASON MATTERS: a link underline is an AFFORDANCE (absent at rest, appears on hover, means clickable), while the caption rule is STRUCTURE (present at rest, the datum every caption in the column hangs from). Growing it from zero would make the page's structure blink in and out with the pointer. So it borrows the link language's VERB, a 1px line travelling left to right, without becoming a link.** `scaleX` not `width`, which is pixel-identical here and cheaper, and is the fix the animation audit listed as open for the four `width`-animated underlines. The title's whitening gained `transition-delay: 60ms` so the hover reads left to right instead of as four simultaneous state changes. Verified with a real pointer: rest scaleX(0) / title #f0f0f0, mid-sweep 0.95, settled scaleX(1) / #fff, released back to 0. NOT gated on reduced-motion, deliberately, because the link underlines it matches are not gated either. KNOWN TRADEOFF: the caption rule is ~456px against a ~40px nav link, so at the shared 160ms it travels roughly 11x faster in px/ms and can read as a flash rather than a sweep; the dial is that one duration, and slowing it would break the exact family match that is the point. **STILL OPEN, the larger coherence gap: the plate leans its light toward the cursor and the caption ignores the pointer entirely, so the two halves of one card behave differently under the same hand.** HOVER: the sheet's 2px-lift replacement for `scale(1.04)` went with the revert, but **the oklch hue glow the row build had silently lost is restored** (its rule is scoped `.project-grid .project-card:hover`, which stopped matching when the builder moved cards into `.feature`, while line 201 killed the base glow, so desktop rows had NO hover glow at all for days). SHIPPED STATE after the revert: the DATUM (align-items start + caption hairline + 20px padding), the TYPE (title clamp to 38, description back to 16, chip padding 6/10 and gap 8) and the PLATE LIP, plus the restored hue glow. (b0) **SCROLL PASS 2026-09-02 EVENING (per user "home page's card's aesthetics and scroll effect, nothing else"), two fixes that had been researched hours earlier and never built:** (i) the sweep is now an EVENT, `animation-range: cover 18% cover 82%` with the keyframes widened to +/-26% (was `cover 0% cover 100%` at +/-18%). Measured, the old form moved 170px of light across ~1370px of scroll, about a tenth of a pixel of light per pixel of scroll, and the row was always mid-travel while you were reading it, which reads as a gradient being scrolled rather than light passing over something. The new range holds the light at each end and spends the whole travel while the row is on screen. Do not go past ~28% total with the 12% cursor lean also in play, or the pools slide off-plate and the EDGES move less, not more. (ii) **THE SCREENSHOT PARALLAX WAS SILENTLY DEAD AND IS NOW REVIVED ON THE ROWS.** `card-parallax` (the +/-8px drift of the still against its plate, built and approved 2026-07-26) is scoped `.project-grid .project-img... > img` in style.css, and the feature-row builder MOVES the cards out of `.project-grid` into `.feature`, so from the moment the rows shipped the still never moved on desktop. A feature-row copy re-points the same keyframes at the row's own `--row` timeline, excluding `.project-img--cover` (the two full-bleed covers have no plate margin to move within) and `.card-loop--cover` (NOVA's full-bleed loop). Verified: five contained stills drift -7.2 to +7.2px across their crossing, StoryBloom correctly stays put, phones keep the untouched grid rule. **LESSON, and it has now bitten twice on this page: any style.css rule scoped to `.project-grid` dies when the row builder runs. The hue glow was the other casualty. Grep `.project-grid` in style.css before assuming a card effect still works on desktop.** (b) THE LIGHT PASSES OVER EACH PLATE: a registered `--plate-sy` (percentage, inherits) is animated by `@keyframes plate-sweep` (-18% → +18%) on `.feature-card` over `cover 0% cover 100%` of its own view() timeline, and a feature-row COPY of the `.project-img--lit` recipe adds `+ var(--plate-sy)` to both pools' y (style.css untouched). The cursor lean keeps writing `--plate-dx/dy` inline from JS; the two never share a variable, which is what keeps them from fighting (an animation would otherwise override the inline value). (c) ASSEMBLY STAGGER: the row no longer animates as a block. `view-timeline-name: --row` on the row; the plate runs `card-arrive` on `--row` over `entry 0% entry 55%`, then `.project-info-top` (`entry 15%-70%`), `p` (`25%-80%`) and `.card-meta` (`35%-90%`), selected as `.project-info-left > *` (NOT `.project-info > *`: the pieces sit inside a `.project-info-left` wrapper, and the first build matched the wrapper so the stagger silently did nothing), run `piece-arrive` (18px rise + fade) on the same row timeline, so the stagger is against the ROW's entry rather than each tiny piece's own (a piece's own view() entry spans only its ~30px height and would pop). The `arrive-now` timed fallback now targets the pieces (plate 0.9s at 1.25s, then 1.45 / 1.6 / 1.75s). Verified headless: sweep value moves with scroll, plate lands before the caption pieces, cursor lean still writes its own vars, zero errors.

A more persistent variant keyed to `chat_opened` (so it returned on every visit until the visitor actually used the chat) shipped for one round and was rejected the same day — user: "each time open it will jump the bubble which is too much. I just need the first time." The only surviving change from that variant is the hold, 9s instead of 6. **EDGE CASE THAT COST A ROUND, worth remembering for any once-per-browser pattern: the scroll listener is registered at LOAD, so it must RE-READ the flag when it fires, not just at registration — otherwise a visitor who opens the chat before scrolling still gets the bubble popping at them on their first scroll.** The toggle's click handler also writes the flag, so engaging with the chat first suppresses it permanently. Verified across five loads in one profile plus a fresh profile plus that edge case. Everything else is the original: the bubble fires on FIRST SCROLL rather than a timer so it can never overlap the hero headline, and `.chat-teaser` is `display: none` below 768px. All the CSS was still in style.css (orphaned since the removal), so this was markup plus script only, no stylesheet change and no version bump. KNOWN, PRE-EXISTING: the bubble is `position: fixed; bottom: 280px; right: 40px`, so at some scroll offsets it lands over a row's chip line for its 9 seconds; that geometry shipped for months before the removal and moving it would break the tail's alignment with the toggle. The localStorage reads and writes are wrapped in try/catch (private windows throw). Historical note of the removal follows: THE CHAT TEASER WAS REMOVED from index.html (markup, the first-scroll trigger, the `chat_teaser_seen` localStorage flag, and the toggle handler's reference); the button stays as a silent mark. The teaser never existed on about/play, and the case studies keep their pasted widget blocks untouched per the user's do-not-touch-case-studies instruction, so a first visit that lands on a case study can still see one teaser. The desktop `sizes` values are deliberately LARGER than the real slots (428 / 658 px) so a 2x desktop still resolves to the untouched 1920 file and the home grid stays byte-identical on laptops; phones get the 960 (2x) or 1280 (3x cover) files. - **Countersign card VISIBLE ON MOBILE as of 2026-09-02 (per user, from the audit's verified finding: the strongest 2026 agent piece was hidden from every phone reader while the Mercor wordmark showed). SUPERSEDES every "Countersign is `project-card--desktop-only` / the mobile stack is 7" note below.** The class was removed from the card; the mobile stack is now all EIGHT cards in grid order (Homewise · PollenNav · Countersign · StoryBloom · Coffee Chat · NOVA · Mercor · New Craft Society). The original reason for hiding it (the card once linked straight to a desktop-only live demo) stopped applying on 2026-08-21 when the card was pointed at countersign.html, which was verified mobile-clean. On phones the card shows its static still only (the loop observer's ≤768 early return is unchanged). The `.project-card--desktop-only` CSS rule in style.css is now unused on the home page (kept, harmless; play.html still uses its own variant). Verified at 375: 8 cards, correct order, no overflow.
- **Work-card motion (added 2026-08-16, per user "I want to make them more dynamics with motions"; index.html only).** Four of the eight cards now move; the CSS + loader are INLINE in index.html on purpose (`.card-loop`/`.sb-phone` exist nowhere else, so shipping them in style.css would force a version bump across nine pages — same reasoning as homewise.html's inline flow-chart block). **Triggered by viewport entry, NOT hover** (per user "play no need hover"): one IntersectionObserver (rootMargin 100px, threshold 0.35) adds `is-playing`, sets the raster loop's `src` from `data-loop`, and on exit removes the class AND the `src` so the decode is dropped. A card showing `loaded:false` while off-screen is that behaviour working, not a broken image. `prefers-reduced-motion` disables everything, and so does **`max-width: 768px` (per user 2026-08-17 'my portfolio's mobile version. the thumbnail please use the static images for each')** — phones keep the approved stills. ONE early return in the observer does all of it: `is-playing` is never added, so no loop `src` is ever set (verified 0 of 4 fetched at 375, saving ~1.5MB), StoryBloom's CSS entrance never arms and renders at rest, and NOVA's `.card-still--swap` never fades. `.card-loop { display: none }` on mobile is belt-and-braces. **The assets were generated, not recorded by hand** — the reusable recipe is: clone the product repo, patch a deep-link/bypass into it if a screen sits behind a gate, `npm run build`, serve `dist/` with python http.server, then drive it with **puppeteer-core** (installed in the scratch dir; it reuses the installed Chrome via `executablePath`, no Chromium download) taking `page.screenshot()` on a fixed interval, and assemble the PNG sequence into an animated WebP with PIL. **Capture at the EXACT pixel dimensions of the card's existing still** (Countersign 1920×1147, Homewise 1920×1144) so the app lays out identically and NO cropping is needed — a first pass captured at 1280 wide and then cropped to aspect, which laid the app out tighter and clipped its edges (user: "the interface get cut off"). Per card: **Countersign** (429KB) is the real agent loop running to the approval gate, captured from github.com/chang627627/credit-analysis-agent (mocked backend, so it is deterministic); **Homewise** (476KB) is a slow pan down the normalized-scope table, because its compare screen is a *document* not a process — a straight capture had only 7 of 53 frames changing, and the pan is the honest way to give a static screen motion; **New Craft Society** (220KB) is the live site's watercolor drift, captured after waiting 26s for the black intro to run out (per user "maybe just record the green background as loop. no black") and built as a **ping-pong loop** (forward then reversed) because ambient drift is not periodic and a straight loop visibly jumps; **StoryBloom** is **pure CSS, no raster at all** — see below. **StoryBloom's FAN-IN ENTRANCE WAS DELETED 2026-09-02 (per user "storybloom's entry animation is too much. maybe make it just like pollennav is enough"): the three phones now render at rest and the card's only entrance is the standard `card-arrive` on its plate, exactly like PollenNav. The `sbPhoneIn`/`InL`/`InR` keyframes and the `.is-playing .sb-phone` rules are gone, and the loop observer no longer watches `.sb-phone--1` (it now collects only `.card-loop`). THE LAYERED COMPOSITION STAYS** — the card is still a reconstructed wash plus three transparent phone PNGs, because that is what gives the phones their live CSS drop shadow instead of a baked one, and the masking work below is still why they sit cleanly on the wash. The build notes that follow are therefore about the ASSET, not about an animation. **StoryBloom's asset treatment is the one worth copying**: the flat cover was split into a reconstructed wash (`storybloom-bg.jpg`, 38KB) plus three transparent phone layers, so the phones can fan in separately (outer two rotate ±6° from their own side, centre rises straight, 130ms stagger, overshoot curve, plays ONCE on entry per user "do not make it like loop"). **The phones MUST be masked to their true rounded-rect silhouette (radius 70 on the 1920px cover), never a feathered rectangle** — a rectangular crop carries a patch of its original background that does not match the reconstructed wash and reads as a glowing box (user: "why it has the weird ass background" / "white glow"). The wash is rebuilt by interpolating each row horizontally across a padded box (PAD 46 to swallow the drop shadow) then blurring; verified pixel-identical either side of each phone. Phone geometry is expressed in percentages of the 1920×1080 cover so the resting composition matches the original exactly. **NOVA now rotates (2026-08-16, SUPERSEDING the earlier note that it must stay static): the user generated a Midjourney image-to-video turntable of the glasses themselves and supplied the mp4.** `images/nova-loop.webp` (413KB, 60 frames, 816×480) is that clip used RAW — no keying, no colour clamping, no cropping (the user's explicit call: "this video is perfect. just use it"). It is **full-bleed** (`.card-loop--cover`), so the clip's own white background becomes the card's plate and the still crossfades out beneath it. **Three CSS traps hit on the way, all the same root cause: `.project-img > img` in style.css is (0,1,1) and owns BOTH the 65% sizing and the floating-screenshot `drop-shadow`, so any full-bleed clip must out-specify it** (`.project-img > img.card-loop--cover`) or it renders at 65% with a shadow drawn around its rectangle — the same trap the Mercor note describes. Dead ends worth not repeating: (a) the first mp4 had a pure-black background AND black glasses, so luminance keying is impossible — **connectivity keying works though** (`ImageDraw.floodfill` from the four corners, thresh ~30-52, then erode 1px INTO the object or mpeg mosquito noise leaves a grey fringe on white); (b) keying to real alpha lets the still show through the gaps, which reads as TWO pairs of glasses — hence `.card-still--swap`; (c) alpha tripled the file (349KB → 1077KB). **Mercor stays static permanently** (wordmark under NDA). A turntable rendered from Chang's own Rhino/SolidWorks model remains the higher-fidelity option if the capstone file resurfaces; the shipped clip is Midjourney's interpretation of the glasses, not the original industrial design. **The loop is a PING-PONG** (forward then reversed, sharing no end frames), because the clip does not return to its start angle and a straight loop jumped visibly on restart (first-vs-last mean diff 25.1 -> **2.91**). Same fix as the New Craft watercolour. It reads as rotate-and-return rather than a one-way spin, which was judged the better trade at card size: a jump draws the eye more than a reversal does. **Build it by halving the frame rate, not by doubling the frames** - a naive ping-pong of all 60 frames was 791KB; sampling every other frame and playing at 172ms gives identical motion and duration at 389KB, i.e. smaller than the straight loop it replaced. **Framing lesson, learned the hard way and worth reusing for any turntable: centre the SWEPT ENVELOPE once with a single constant offset, never each frame on its own bbox.** Per-frame bbox centring was tried and the user immediately read it as the object "floating" — correctly, because an asymmetric object's bbox legitimately moves as it turns (the temples swing out toward profile), so pinning it applies a continuous counter-motion that fights the rotation. The shipped build takes the union of every frame's bbox, centres that once (offset 30,17 on a 1.10x white canvas), and leaves the spin untouched: envelope imbalance 1px, while individual frames stay asymmetric by design and the object reads 50-59% of card width through the cycle. The final source is the THIRD Midjourney clip the user generated ("make it more centered"), which is inherently steadier than the second (37px per-frame drift vs 58px). PollenNav was assessed and skipped (three phones plus four award badges is already the busiest card). **Stale note corrected:** New Craft's public site is no longer "a dark typing intro" that resists headless capture — it is a full marketing page whose opening sequence captures fine.
- `about.html` — About (vertical layout: hero → photo deck → background → experience → education → awards → skills). The photo deck is an infinite drag-to-rotate carousel on desktop, a static 4×2 grid on mobile (see **About photo deck** below). **Polish pass (2026-06-20):** Experience, Education, and Recognition all share one aligned 3-column format via the `.experience-list` CSS **subgrid** (`grid-template-columns: auto minmax(0,1fr) auto`; each `<li>` is `grid-template-columns: subgrid`, `.exp-row` is `display:contents`, so the primary/secondary/date columns align *across* rows; each list sizes its primary column to its own longest entry; mobile reflows to primary+date on the top line, secondary below). **Recognition was converted** from a bulleted `.recognition-list` text list into this same `.experience-list` format (per user "same format like experience and education") — award name white, status grey (Winner / Silver Winner / Featured / Exhibited at Ferry Building / **Recipient** for the scholarships), date grey-right; the interim `.recognition-list li`/`.rec-detail` comma styling was removed. **Skills + Tools now sit side by side** on desktop via a `.about-cols` 2-col grid wrapper (stacks on mobile). **Books + Podcasts kept full-width on purpose** — side-by-side shrinks the covers below legible (~78px), so they keep the full width. Chips (`.skill-list`) were left as-is (already clean bordered pills). style.css bumped **v=174→v=178** across all 8 pages. **Reworked 2026-06-20 (this session — SUPERSEDES the Polish-pass "Skills + Tools side by side" and "aligned 3-column subgrid" notes above):** (1) **Skills + Tools sections DELETED** entirely (per user); the `.about-cols` 2-col wrapper + `.skill-list` chip CSS are now **unused** (left in style.css, harmless). Do NOT re-add Skills/Tools unless asked. (2) **Experience / Education / Recognition converted from the 3-col `.experience-list` subgrid to an inline "name · detail · date" row (the "B1" layout).** *Why:* the three lists were *separate* subgrids, each sizing its first column to its own longest entry, so the middle column started at a different x per section (268 / 346 / 403px) — a cross-section zigzag the user flagged as "messy". Do NOT re-add it to the bio.** The diagnosis is the reusable part: the complaint was READABILITY, and the cause was the effect itself, since dim-until-scrolled-past means any paragraph is partly unlit *while you are reading it*. That is unfixable by colour, which the session proved the expensive way — `--text-soft` → `#f0f0f0` ("too bright") → `#c8c8c8` ("too bright") → `#888` (matched case studies exactly, but inverted the page's own hierarchy: prose dimmer than the `.ai-stage-desc` card copy nested below it) → **back to `--text-soft` #a0a0a0, i.e. the original value, which reads fine now that the animation is gone.** Two durable lessons: (a) **when a user reports text as hard to read, check for an animation on it before touching the token** — five colour values were burned before the real cause was named; (b) **there is no single site body colour to "match"** — case-study prose and `.impact-card` copy are `--text-muted` #888 (5.6:1) while long-read text (About bio, `.testimonial-quote`) is `--text-soft` #a0a0a0 (7.5:1), and that split is the deliberate 2026-06-20 decision, which STANDS. The one surviving colour change from the session: **`.ai-stage-desc` #888 → #a0a0a0**, because it sits on a `--surface` #1a1a1a card where #888 falls to 4.6:1, barely over the AA floor at 14px — compare card copy to card copy (`.testimonial-quote`), never card copy to page prose. A `--text-read: #c8c8c8` fourth tier was created and deleted the same day; the ladder stays three tiers. **Testimonial vantage labels were also built and DELETED the same day (per user "delete something like: 'The founder I designed for'")** — a `.testimonial-window` line above each quote naming the relationship (founder I designed for / designer next to me / PM across from me), from a "three windows onto the person, not three ratings of the work" reframe; do not re-propose. **What was KEPT from that reframe: a white `<span style="color: var(--text)">` highlight on one behavioral clause inside each of the three quotes** (Zeeshan "conceptualize and implement complex design solutions", Dylan "supported his colleagues", Isabel "mentoring junior designers and elevating the people around him") — no endorser's words were changed, the highlight just lands the eye on a behaviour rather than a rating. style.css **v=235→v=239** across **10** pages — note storybloom.html was added since the old "nine pages" note, so **grep for the version rather than trusting the count**. The one sanctioned future extension, NOT built: each case study's opening paragraph — a separate user decision. Mechanics: an about.html IIFE splits the paragraphs into 122 `.bio-word` spans (spaces stay bare text nodes so wrapping behaves as plain text — hero precedent; an element-children guard skips any paragraph that later gains a link/highlight span rather than mangling it), and style.css animates `.bio-word` only — **inert on the other 8 pages since nothing else ever creates the class**; JS-fails → static soft text, same graceful direction as card-rise. Each span carries its own **anonymous `view()` timeline** (inline-block so the spans generate real boxes; same-line words share identical progress, so the sweep reads line-by-line with zero delay bookkeeping; verified no ancestor of `.about-block p` is a scroll container, so the `--card-view` trap does not apply). Longhands + `animation-duration: auto` per the card-rise trap; gated `@supports (animation-timeline: view())` + `prefers-reduced-motion` with mirrored JS guards. **Range is `cover 18% cover 45%`, tuned by measurement — a `cover 0%` start shipped first and was INVISIBLE:** it spread each word's ramp over ~460px of scroll, blurring the whole bio into one near-flat gradient (the documented "too subliminal to earn its place" failure mode); at 18% the ramp ≈ the bio block's own height and the sweep is real (measured 192/185/168/160 front-to-back mid-sweep, byte-identical on the reverse pass). **NEW probe gotcha, cost a false negative: scroll-driven animation styles do NOT update synchronously after `scrollTo()` — a same-task `getComputedStyle` reads stale values and the effect looks dead; probes must wait a double rAF between scroll and read** (same family as rAF-not-while-hidden). Consequence, accepted: on this surface `--text-soft` now means "not yet read" — text rests white once passed, which deliberately softens the 2026-06-20 mid-tier-grey decision here. Clean URL `/coffeeslide` via a `vercel.json` rewrite (added alongside `/nova`, `/homewise`). **Fixed 1920×1080 presentation slides** (user explicitly asked for this size): a `.stage` (1920×1080) is scaled to fit the viewport (letterboxed) via a JS `fit()` that sets `--scale = min(vw/1920, vh/1080)`; **all inner sizing is fixed px** (NOT vw/vh/clamp) so the whole stage scales uniformly and looks identical everywhere. **19 slides** following coffee.html's section order: Title → Overview → Impact (63%/200+/$4.5M) → Problem → Research (4 tool tiles + insight) → Design Goals → Constraints → Exploration 1 (pairing card, split layout) → Exploration 2 (before/after flow tag-cards, NOT the full SVG — deck digest) → Iteration 1 → Iteration 2 → **Final Design (5 slides, one screen per case-study deliverable in order: Seamless scheduling · Handling declines · Flexible scheduling · Review proposed times · Propose your own availability, labeled "Final Design · N of 5")** → Design System → Reflection → Closing (recap + CTAs to coffee.html / index / mailto). **Final Design expanded from 2 condensed slides → the full 5 (2026-07-17, per user "a final design omits a lot of things, refer to the case study order"): the first cut collapsed the 5 deliverable screens into scheduling + a merged reschedule/declines slide and reordered them; now each screen is its own slide in coffee.html's exact order (coffeefinal1 · handling-declines · Flexible1 · Flexible2 · Flexible3). Em dashes in body copy were also cleaned to periods/commas per [[feedback]] (page-title em dashes kept, matching the site convention).** **Self-contained** — inline `<style>`+`<script>`, does NOT load style.css (so no version bump), but mirrors the site tokens (#0a0a0a/#1a1a1a/Neue Montreal, warm→blue title+closing gradients like hero g2, `#D0D1FD` lavender app-screen plates from coffee.html). Nav: `.deck-track` translateX(-index*1920px) inside the scaled stage; **keyboard** (←/→/space/PageUp-Down/Home/End), **click** (prev/next circle btns), **swipe** (touch), and **`#N` hash deep-linking** (1-based, e.g. `/coffeeslide#8`; syncs via `history.replaceState`). Chrome (top progress bar in the warm→blue gradient, brand top-left→index.html, `NN / 16` counter, hint) is viewport-fixed (unscaled). **Images are EAGER (no `loading="lazy"`)** — deliberate: `loading="lazy"` computes intersection against the *scroll* viewport, but slides move via `transform`, so off-screen lazy images never reliably load and showed **blank plates mid-deck** (hit on the Iteration-1 "After" plate during build). Slide 1 is text+gradient only so first paint stays instant while the ~13 screenshots (incl. coffeefinal1 3MB, coffeedesignsystem 2MB) stream in behind it. `prefers-reduced-motion` drops the slide/reveal transitions; `<noscript>` points to coffee.html. **NOT linked from the home grid or coffee.html** — standalone at `/coffeeslide` (reachable by URL), like ticker.html. Per-slide content is designed to fit within 1080px (verified in preview at 16:9 — no clipping on any slide, incl. the tall image/split/before-after slides). **Design/spacing pass 2026-07-17 (per user "check the spacing consistency, let slide use site's design system, make it more visual"):** (1) **Spacing scale** — replaced ad-hoc margins with one rhythm adapted from style.css and scaled for the 1920 canvas: label →20px→ title →24px→ body →48px→ media block; grid gaps 24px (matches `.project-grid`). (2) **Site design system** — `.s-label` now carries the **`.section-meta` hairline-rule** treatment (`LABEL ————`, flex + `::after` 1px rule) like the home-page section headers; radius 14→12px. (3) **More visual** — single-image slides (all 5 Final Design + Design System) now let the plate FILL the space below a top-anchored header via `.slide-inner > .plate:last-child { flex:1; min-height:0 }` + `img{max-height:100%}` (bigger, dominant images; also structurally prevents title-clipping since the header is pinned to the top, not vertically centered). Card/prose slides stay vertically centered (`.slide-inner{justify-content:center}`) — a `:has()` rule to top-anchor ALL slides Keynote-style was tried and reverted because it left sparse slides like Impact bottom-empty; centered balances short content better while the plate-fill naturally top-anchors image slides. All CSS-only (self-contained page, no style.css/version bump). **Closing slide DELETED 2026-07-17 (per user "feels repetitive with slide 3"):** the old slide 19 recapped the same 63%/200+/$4.5M stats as Impact plus exit CTAs; the whole section was removed (deck now **18 slides**, ends on Reflection; `.slide--end`/`.end-stats`/`.cta-row` CSS left unused in the inline style block, harmless). Do NOT re-add a stats-recap close; a minimal stat-free sign-off is the only acceptable form if a closing returns. **Figma-style COMMENT MODE added 2026-07-17 (per user "add the comment mode to the slide like figma"):** a speech-bubble `.navbtn--comment` (right:150px, next to prev/next, count badge) or the `C` key toggles `body.commenting`; in that mode each slide's `.pin-layer` (absolutely-inset div appended by JS into every `.slide`, `position:relative` added to `.slide` for it) captures clicks — click anywhere drops a draft popover (name + note, name remembered via localStorage `coffeeslide_commenter`), Post renders a numbered **teardrop pin** (`border-radius:17px 17px 17px 3px`, Figma-style tail at the anchor point). Pins/popovers live INSIDE the scaled stage so they ride the track transform, are anchored via a 0×0 `.pin-anchor` at %-coords of the 1920×1080 canvas, and **inverse-scale** (`transform:scale(calc(1/var(--scale)))` about the anchor corner) so they render constant screen size at any viewport. Pins are visible ONLY in comment mode (like Figma). Clicking a pin opens a view popover (avatar initial, name, date, Delete). **Comments are SHARED as of 2026-08-11 (per user "I want everyone can comment") — SUPERSEDES the per-browser-localStorage note that used to live here.** `api/comments.js` (GET/POST/DELETE, origin-allowlist + 60/5min rate limit, deck whitelist coffeeslide/homewiseslide) over the **PRIVATE `deck-comments` Vercel Blob store** (**ONE IMMUTABLE BLOB PER COMMENT at `comments/<deck>/<id>.json` — v2, same day: the v1 one-JSON-array-per-deck design was replaced because Blob's overwrite cache/consistency window served the FUNCTION's reads stale for minutes cross-region, resurfacing deleted pins and hiding fresh posts even with `cacheControlMaxAge: 0` + `?ts=` busting; immutable objects sidestep that layer entirely and kill the concurrent-post race, since posts create objects, deletes remove them, and nothing ever overwrites**), **provisioned via CLI with `npx vercel blob create-store deck-comments --access private --yes`** — no dashboard needed; BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN landed in project env automatically. **This added the repo's first root `package.json`** (@vercel/blob dependency; no build script, so Vercel still serves the root statically — verified all pages 200 post-deploy). Both decks' pin engines fetch the shared store on load and on entering comment mode, POST/DELETE through it, and **lift any localStorage-era pins into the store once** (`<deck>slide_comments_v1_migrated` flag); if the API is unreachable (LOCAL PREVIEW — /api/* 404s there) the engine falls back to the original per-browser behavior, verified identical. **Deletion is OPEN as of 2026-08-11 (per user: trusted audience, "let everyone delete each other's comment") — the Delete button renders on every pin and DELETE checks nothing but deck+id.** The per-browser author key + GET's computed `own` flag still exist (harmless, kept for a future tightening); the original owner-only enforcement was built and verified first, then relaxed the same day. The mailto pill stays. **Blob gotchas, all hit: the private-store PUT access header is `x-vercel-blob-access` (raw REST is version-pinned pain — use the SDK), and NEVER build overwrite-based storage on Blob (Vercel's own docs say it is not a database; local same-region reads look deterministically fresh while deployed function reads are not, so the failure only shows in production). If a real datastore is ever wanted, `npx vercel install upstash/upstash-kv` gets to the finish line but requires the ACCOUNT OWNER to accept Upstash's terms in a browser — an agent cannot complete it.** Deploys of api/ functions take ~2-5 min to fully serve; a verification loop right after push needs generous retries or it tests the OLD function (this cost two false alarms). The top .cmt-bar pill carries a **"Send N notes to Chang ↗" mailto** that packages all notes to changmou627627@gmail.com. Guards: the deck's keydown handler early-returns for input/textarea targets (typing space/arrows must not drive slides), Esc closes popover then exits mode, swipe-nav is disabled while commenting, and the whole comment chrome is hidden ≤640px (pins would be untappable at phone scale). Custom SVG comment cursor via data-URI on the layer. **Fable-5 polish pass 2026-07-17 (per user "make the slides more polished by fable 5" + "use portfolio-audit as checker"; SUPERSEDES the 18-slide count and parts of the earlier spacing-pass notes):** ran a 5-agent workflow (4 audit lenses over screenshots of every slide + a high-effort judge, all `model:'fable'`) against `~/.claude/skills/portfolio-audit/SKILL.md`; the judge returned 12 prioritized changes and rejected 10 findings (incl. per its own check: no entrance-animation rework, no wholesale retitling, no chrome-type normalization). The eyebrows are fixed (they mirror the side-nav), so the **h2s became claims**: "Explain the match, and people accept it", "Remove the messaging, remove the drop-off", "Building the atomic foundations of the rebrand". **Lesson: after renaming a section label, diff it against its own h2** — a rename that matches the heading creates redundancy rather than clarity. **Hello slide moved BACK to position 2 on coffeeslide 2026-08-03 (per user "self intro at the beginning") — this REVERSES the 2026-07-28 mentor-framework move that sent it to the penultimate slot.** The recorded rationale for putting it late ("in an interview you have already been introduced, so it spends the first minute before any stakes") was raised once and overruled; treat slide 2 as the settled position for this deck and do not move it again unprompted. Spine is now Title · Hello · The bet · … · Reflection · Sign-off. **Homewiseslide followed 2026-08-03 (per user "move the self intro to the beginning"), so BOTH decks now open Title · Hello · The bet and the asymmetry is resolved.** Homewise's Hello moved 18 → 2, everything between shifted down one (Reflection 17→18, Sign-off 18→19; still 19 slides), and the clip slides moved with it (Final design 10-13→11-14, Design system 15→16) — the JS keys off `slides.length` and DOM position, so nothing needed updating, but **its `homewiseslide_comments_v1` pins are keyed by slide INDEX and now point one slide earlier than intended.** Verified after the move: folio 02/19 on Hello, no overflow, and the Design-system clip still auto-plays. **User-flow diagrams RESTORED, deck is now 20 slides 2026-08-03 (per user "why this slide do not have the diagram?"):** the original build made this beat a "deck digest" — it kept the ✕/✓ tags from coffee.html's two flow charts and dropped the SVGs, leaving the sparsest slide in the deck (title + body + two bullet lists, no artifact) under a heading that asserts "remove the messaging, remove the drop-off" without showing it. **Slide 8 was split into two: 8 "The user flow — before" (Existing tools, 6 boxes) and 9 "The user flow — after" (Our Coffee Chat flow, 9 boxes incl. the decline/reschedule fallbacks)**, each carrying its own chart at full width; everything downstream shifted by one (Sign-off is now 20) and the slide-number comments were renumbered. `const total = slides.length` and the JS-injected folio pick the new count up automatically — **there is no hardcoded total to update**. The two `<svg class="flow-svg">` blocks were copied verbatim from coffee.html and their `.flow-svg`/`.flow-box` CSS inlined in the deck (which does not load style.css); the `arrow1`/`arrow2` marker ids do not collide. **Sizing is the interesting part and the reason it works:** `.flow-wrap` is `flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0` and the SVG is `height: 100%`, so **height is the constraint and width follows the ratio via preserveAspectRatio** — that one rule fits both the 960×260 and the 960×460 chart with no per-slide sizing. Two moves bought the taller chart its legibility: the viewBoxes were tightened to their real content bboxes (`0 0 960 460` → `10 30 940 420`, measured with `getBBox()`, which showed 40px of dead space at top), and both slides were converted to the deck's own 2-col `.media-head` (title left, annotation right) — the pattern slides 12-17 already use precisely to stop a paragraph eating the artifact's height. Result: the before chart draws at 1.81× (13px viewBox text renders ~23px on canvas) and the after chart at 1.44× (~19px), up from 1.15×/~15px before the fix. **Do NOT re-collapse these into one slide** — a side-by-side pair was measured at ~0.86×, i.e. ~9px text on a laptop, which is unreadable in a room. **Consequence for comment mode:** `coffeeslide_comments_v1` keys notes by slide INDEX, so any pin previously left on slides 9-19 now points one slide earlier than intended. **Competitive-analysis screenshots RESTORED + pairing-card corners fixed 2026-08-03 (per user):** (a) the four tool screenshots deleted in the reskin are back on slide 5, reusing the existing `images/tool-{donut,watercooler,gather,focusmate}.jpg` that coffee.html's table already uses (no new assets — the user's pasted images were byte-identical to these). New `.col-shot` rule frames them uniformly: `aspect-ratio: 3/2; object-fit: cover; object-position: top left` + hairline border + 8px radius, because the four source captures are different shapes (327-530px wide at 320 tall) and only a shared frame makes the row align. **Donut's description was lengthened to "Matching is automated, but it feels robotic and repetitive if not personalized."** — at one line it sat 15px higher than its three two-line siblings and broke the screenshot row; the added clause comes from the case study's own pros column ("Fully automated matching"), so it adds information rather than padding. (b) **`coffee-pairing-card-slide.jpg` → `.png`**: the card's rounded corners had pure-black `(0,0,0)` pixels outside the 24px radius, which read as black notches on the lavender plate. Rebuilt with transparent corners — **PNG is both lossless AND smaller here (623KB vs 776KB)** because it is a flat light UI screenshot, and transparency survives any future plate-colour change. **GOTCHA: cutting at the true 24px radius leaves a dark antialiasing rim** (the JPEG's black-to-border blend); measured combinations and settled on **radius 28 + 2px erode**, the smallest adjustment that yields zero sub-120-luminance pixels within 80px of every corner. Counter fallback 19→20; all later `#N` deep links shifted by one. **Faux-bold titles fixed 2026-07-17 (per user "font weight too much for title, refer to the website"):** `.title-h1` and `.s-title` never set `font-weight`, so UA-default h1/h2 bold (700) applied — and since only the 400 face of Neue Montreal is loaded, the browser **synthesized a fake bold**, making deck titles chunkier than the site's regular-weight headings. Fix: explicit `font-weight: 400` on both. **Gotcha for any new page:** the site is single-weight 400 by design, so every h1–h4 rule MUST set `font-weight: 400` explicitly (style.css does; a fresh page's UA defaults will silently synthesize bold).

Built with Vite to a static bundle at **`/coffee-agent/`** (~500KB, `base:'./'`; keeps the export's `noindex` meta, NOT in sitemap — standalone URL works and the slide links it as "open it full size"). **Patched source is checked in at `coffee-agent-src/`** (minimal package.json: react/react-dom/motion/tw-animate-css — the export's versioned-alias deps belong to the unused shadcn `ui/` folder; also patched: `p-8` removed from App.tsx so the 500×700 window fills the iframe exactly, Inter webfont added to its index.html since the export's fonts.css was EMPTY and the app rendered serif, and 12 em dashes in Luna's strings swept to house punctuation). Rebuild: `cd coffee-agent-src && npm i && npm run build && rm -rf ../coffee-agent && mkdir ../coffee-agent && cp -R dist/ ../coffee-agent/`. **Three integration facts that will bite:** (1) **`scrollIntoView` inside a same-origin iframe scrolls the HOST page's ancestors — including `overflow:hidden` boxes** — the app's message autoscroll dragged the whole deck track sideways (~half a slide, copy column off-screen); fixed at source by scrolling the messages container itself (`el.scrollTo({top: el.scrollHeight})`); any future live embed must obey the same rule. (2) The iframe is upscaled `transform: scale(1.08)` (500×700 app → 540×756 canvas) so its 14px UI type stays legible at deck scale; clicks resolve correctly through the nested stage+iframe transforms (verified: book → reflection → history loop all work in-slide). (3) **Lazy `data-src` load wired into `render()`** — the bundle loads exactly on arrival (`index === demoAt`), so first paint never pays for React and Luna's opening script plays as the viewer lands; verified `End` jumping past the slide does NOT load it. Consequences: sign-off is slide 21; `coffeeslide_comments_v1` pins previously on slide 20 (old sign-off) now point at the new slide. **Probe gotchas recorded:** a `navigate` to a hash-only URL is same-document and does NOT reload (stale-bundle false positives — bust with a query param), and synthetic `keydown` dispatched on `document` throws in the deck's handlers (`e.target.closest` — document has no `.closest`); dispatch on `body`. **Brought onto the page 2026-08-06 (per user "bring this experience into the study case"): coffee.html now has an 11th section `#rebuild`** ("What I'd do differently" eyebrow, claim h2 "This time, the agent goes first" — a deliberate callback to the Problem card "Nobody wants to go first") after Reflection. **The settled 10-section spine was consciously extended by the user here** — the two prior 11th-section rejections (named-principles list, HITL section) were about restating arguments already on the page; this is new material with a live artifact, and it honors the case-study-format memory's "end on the outcome" rule (a clickable rebuild is the 2026 outcome artifact). Prose is framed explicitly as a **personal exploration** so no reader thinks Litespace shipped Luna. Structure: prose + `.deliverable` caption ("Try it. This is the working build, not a video") + `.agent-embed` (lavender #D0D1FD plate, iframe at natural 500×700, IO-deferred via the same data-src pattern, rootMargin 200px one-shot) + **`.agent-restart`** (40px ink-on-lavender circle, bottom-right 16px of the plate, per user; reloads the iframe's contentWindow; **lucide rotate-cw glyph — the first closed-ring icon was rejected per user "so closed, make it a little open"**; carries a scoped dark `:focus-visible` outline-color override because the global near-white ring vanishes on the light plate; messages-area `pb-0` → uniform `p-[21px]` fixed the same day per user "no margin at the bottom"). **The whole section is desktop-only per user** — `.project-section--desktop-only` (display:none ≤768, Countersign precedent); bonus: the hidden element has no box, so the IO never fires and **phones never download the ~500KB bundle** (verified at 375: section gone, iframe unloaded even at page bottom, zero overflow, Reflection closes the mobile page as before). App also made responsive in the same pass (`max-w-full h-[min(700px,100svh)]` + `overscroll-contain`) so the standalone /coffee-agent/ URL works on phones — verified at 375. llms.txt + api/chat.js both gained the Luna-rebuild mention (chat.js notes desktop-only). style.css **v=228→v=229** across all 9 pages. **Same-day follow-ups (all per user): (1) deck slide 20 got the restart too** (`#demoRestart`, floated `right:-64px` off the frame's bottom corner — the deck frame has no plate margin to host it; ink-on-cream styling; wired in the engine next to demo/demoAt) **and its `.demo-note` line was deleted** (the orphan `.demo-note` CSS rules stay in the deck's inline styles — an edit removing them was explicitly rejected mid-flight, leave them). "what you would rather do" now lands ONCE in page copy (the caption; both surfaces' prose tails are "and the rest is a conversation."); two demo-honesty fixes (dropped "works as well as any button" — typing breaks at the reflection prompt;
