restart wired BEFORE the loader's no-IO fallback return. **Deliberately NOT actioned:** the demo supports typed in-person/online switching, which Iteration 2 says A/B testing removed from the 2023 flow — left as-is because the capability has no visible UI (it surfaces only if asked), which is the remove-friction-keep-capability resolution; revisit only if a reader probes it. **Privacy-transparency intent added 2026-08-06 (per user, out of the "does slide 19 need privacy consent?" discussion — the answer given: the FEATURE needs a consent story, the demo does not need a consent screen; consent's home is onboarding, which the demo deliberately skips, and the 63% opt-in stat is the real feature's consent artifact):** typed "what do you know about me" / "privacy" / "my data" etc. (intent kind `privacy`, checked before `about`, no pattern overlap) gets a two-message transparency answer — the sources Luna reads are exactly what the reason cards cite; reflections stay private from the match; the match sees the same kind of source-cited reasons; opt-in with exit via settings (worded to promise NO typed action the demo cannot handle — the overclaim class the adversarial pass caught). Same surfaces-when-asked pattern as the mode switch, zero visible UI; the handler re-prompts `currentPrompt` so buttons survive the digression. Verified live end to end. **Luna "2026 pass" 2026-08-06 (per user "how to make this feature more 2026?" → research workflow (3 agents: shipped-agent-product patterns, design essays, code-architecture map) → user approved all 4 recommendations):** (1) **Real model behind the free-text path — `api/luna.js`**, a clone of api/chat.js's hardened shape (origin allowlist, 20/5min `globalThis.__lunaRate`, 500-char message cap, 8-turn sanitized history, Haiku `max_tokens: 200`); its system prompt carries Luna's persona + the ENTIRE fictional world serialized from matchData.tsx (**mirror rule: if matchData changes, update the prompt**) + a no-prompt-leak rule. ONLY the unknown-intent default branch calls it (`handleGenerated`); all 11 scripted intents stay client-side and free. **Failure of any kind (404/timeout-10s/empty) falls back to the old honest boundary line — locally /api/* 404s, so the local demo exercises exactly this fallback and still works on stage.** Generated replies carry a muted "· generated" tag (ChatMessage, shows after reveal completes) so scripted vs live is visibly distinct. (2) **Trace step kind** — small check-icon activity lines via `runSteps`: "Scanned calendars · org graph · shared channels" before every proposal (sources ⊂ the privacy answer's list on purpose), **"Pinged {name}'s assistant · confirmed on their side" before "Booked."** (the agent-to-agent beat), "Memory updated · saved in this browser" after reflections, "Memory loaded · your reflections carried over" on return visits. (3) **Cross-visit memory** — `luna_memory_v1` in localStorage (history/seenMatchIds/meetingPreference; lazy state init + persist effect); a visitor who ACTED last time (met someone new or reflected — merely watching doesn't count) gets "Welcome back, Kevin. Last time you met {last}…" and the next unseen match via `proposeMatch(..., preSteps)`; all-seen cycles by resetting seenMatchIds. **Consequence: the deck/page restart button no longer fully resets — memory survives reload BY DESIGN (restart now demonstrates memory); a true first-run needs site-data cleared, which the privacy answer's new third message tells the user.** api/chat.js PORTFOLIO entry + llms.txt note the live-model fact. Production test required post-deploy (endpoint 404s locally). **A 2-agent verify (security + honesty) caught 9 real findings before push, all fixed — the HIGH is a lesson: the first system prompt's world model was written partly from memory and invented wrong Priya facts (Natarajan/New York vs the real Nair/Seoul) and wrong slots; the prompt is now transcribed from matchData.tsx verbatim, and the client sends a sanitized live-state `context` block (met count, past chats, current proposal, preference) that the prompt tells the model to trust over its static text — so memory/proposal drift can never contradict the screen.** Other fixes: privacy source list expanded to cover what the reason cards actually cite (shared docs, directory, shared meetings — both surfaces, handlePrivacy + luna.js rule 4); reflection notes softened to promise only what happens, and option 2 ('Useful, a bit formal') now REALLY sets meetingPreference to in-person; welcome-back requires an actual reflection (not just a second match viewed), never re-proposes the person just met on a cycle reset, and says "next week"; the memory receipt trace is gated on a localStorage capability probe; the deadline banner's impossible date fixed (July 19 is a Saturday in the demo's own Mon-Jul-21 grid → "Sunday, July 20", banner + prompt); '· generated' carries an explanatory tooltip; **and api/chat.js's error path leaked upstream Anthropic error bodies via a `detail` field — closed.** Post-deploy: Haiku slipped an em dash past the prompt rule on the second production reply, so luna.js now strips em/en dashes from replies mechanically (commas, per house punctuation). Production verified end to end: ground-truth Priya answer, live-state count answer, and the '· generated' marker + tooltip rendering in the real UI (a probe reading innerText mid-reveal misses the marker — its gate opens only when the typewriter finishes). **Ops lesson: the preview pane is SHARED — the "phantom" demo interactions chased during verification were the user click-driving the embed in real time** (instrumented no-touch runs were spotless, `isTrusted` logging is the discriminator); unexplained state changes in an interactive embed are probably the user. **Deck Reflection moved to slide 20, AFTER Luna, 2026-08-06 (per user "what I learned at the end"; a first reading of their "18 and 19 should switch" as a DS↔Reflection swap was built then corrected same turn):** closing order is now Final Design ×5 → **Design System 18 → What I'd do differently 19 → Reflection 20 (ink) → Sign-off 21** — the deck ends on What I learned, with the two ink slides back-to-back as a closing dark sequence. NOTE this diverges from the page on purpose: coffee.html runs Reflection → Luna (the rebuild as coda), the deck runs Luna → Reflection (a narrated deck closes on the lesson). Luna's deep-link is now `#19` and `demoAt` self-computes; comment-mode pins on old slides 18-20 shift meaning again (known index-keying consequence). **Deck audit pass 2026-08-06: EXECUTED THEN FULLY REVERTED the same day (user: "revert to the version before i ask") — the deck is back at the pre-audit tree (8974592) except one typo fix. Do NOT re-apply any of it unprompted; the deck's current form is settled.** What was built and discarded: a 6-agent audit (4 lenses + web research + xhigh judge) and 4-agent crop workflow shipped a 10-item plan (claim h2s on 4/11/12/20, honesty recuts on slide 3, scaled triads/numerals on 5/7, a single-row flow SVG for 9, state-detail crops for Final Design 15-17, scale-parity crops for the testing pairs, a palette-half crop for 18, and an in-place ember rebuild of iteration2-before.png's orange box); then an "immersive" pass (animated gradient bookends + a slide-4 pan/zoom) was rejected first ("look ugly as fuck"), after which the user reverted the whole audit too. The findings live in the session transcript if ever wanted, including two open data gaps (the 63% denominator; whether an acceptance/attendance rate exists) and the judge's kill list. **The one survivor, re-applied as its own commit: "User A receive→receives" in BOTH files' flow-chart SVGs — an objective grammar error that was live on production.** **Slides 5/7's empty middles are SETTLED AS-IS (2026-08-06): the user asked about them ("feels empty in the middle. do we need some illustration?"), illustration was advised against (the deck has no illustration language; it would be a template-tell), the scale fix was rebuilt and shown as renders, and the user said "revert" — do not re-propose either scale or illustration for those slides.** Probe lessons kept: the pane's document timeline FREEZES while not compositing (getAnimations() reports `running` with currentTime pinned at 0); headless `--virtual-time-budget` double-capture pixel-diffs prove main-thread (custom-property) animations but NOT compositor (transform/opacity) ones, which snapshot at their start state — for those, force a pane composite with a `computer screenshot`, then read the computed transform matrix.
- **homewiseslide.html gained slides 25-26, the "One more thing" V2 coda, 2026-08-29 (per user "add it to the homewise slides?"), deck is now 27 slides**: slide 25 mirrors the page's three-card triad as the deck's rule-topped `.cols` (01 The finding / 02 The cause / 03 The answer) under the same claim h2; slide 26 "One more thing · One take" is the 80s V2 recording as a media slide — **same click-to-play markup as the shipped-build slide (controls + preload=none + poster + NO `data-clip`), which is exactly what keeps the loop engine off it**; plain `.clip` (no `--play` override) because the recording is 1920×1204, the default aspect pin. Annotation carries the deck's inline-anchor idiom → homewise-v2.vercel.app. Sign-off is 27; index-keyed comment pins shift again (known consequence). Probe notes: this deck's engine DOES navigate on mid-session `location.hash` writes (like novaslide), while a synthetic ArrowRight keydown on body did NOT advance it; and the active slide can transiently show the documented +18px reveal-state scrollHeight artifact.
- **homewiseslide.html gained slide 23 "Collaboration · The handoff" 2026-08-20 (per user "we need the slidedeck get update as well"), deck is now 25 slides** (was 24): same Figma-handoff beat as homewise.html, in the deck's own grammar (`.s-label` header row, 2-col `.media-head`, JS-injected folio which picked up `23 / 25` on its own since the engine reads `slides.length`). Deck copy is deliberately SHORTER than the page's — a deck is narrated, so it carries the claim plus the artifact and the presenter says the rest. New `.shot` class is the still-image sibling of `.clip`, centring the `<img>` inside a flex box so the hairline hugs the artwork with no letterbox gap, and it carries the same load-bearing `flex-basis: 0` that `.clip`'s own comment warns about. Reflection 23→24, Sign-off 24→25; index-keyed comment pins shift again (known consequence). **MEASUREMENT GOTCHA that nearly sent a non-bug chase: the deck stage is CSS-scaled to fit the viewport, so `getBoundingClientRect()` returns SCREEN px while `clientHeight`/`offsetHeight` return CANVAS px.** Mixing them made the artifact look like 854×351 with 339px of "unused" space; in real canvas units it is 1490×612 on the 1920×1080 slide, i.e. fine. **On any deck, measure with offsetWidth/offsetHeight, never bounding rects.** Deck is self-contained, so no style.css change and no version bump.
- `homewiseslide.html` — **Slide-deck version of the Homewise case study** (added 2026-07-28, per user "make the homewiseslide as well? Use study case color, do not use site's gradient"). Clean URL `/homewiseslide` via a `vercel.json` rewrite. Same deck engine as coffeeslide.html (fixed 1920×1080 `.stage` scaled by a JS `fit()`, `.deck-track` translateX nav, keyboard/click/swipe/`#N` hash deep-linking, Figma-style comment mode with its own localStorage keys `homewiseslide_comments_v1`/`homewiseslide_commenter`), but **a completely different skin**. **The color decision is the point: the deck wears HEARTH, Homewise's own design system, and is LIGHT.** The user's "do not use site's gradient" was read as pushing away from the portfolio's identity toward the project's, so the warm→blue hero radials and the `#D0D1FD` lavender plates are gone. Tokens were lifted verbatim from the live `/designsystem` page (homewise-rust.vercel.app/designsystem, read in-browser because the page has no CSS custom properties to scrape and the copy lists every hex): canvas `#F6F4EF` / canvas-soft `#FAF8F3` / card `#FFFFFF` / canvas-deep `#EEEAE1` / ink-900 `#1A1A17`, ink 700-100 `#3C3C38 #6B6B65 #A8A8A1 #CFCEC7 #E7E5DE`, **sage** (trust) 50-700 `#F1F4EF #E2E8DC #C6D2BB #9AAE8C #738A63 #4F6942 #3D5333 #2F4027`, **ember** (caution) 50-500 `#FBF3EA #F5E4CE #EBC89B #D9A461 #B98132 #8E601E`, **sky2026** (info) 50-700 `#EEF3F5 #D9E4E9 #8FAAB3 #4E6F7A #2E4850`. Hearth's own rules are honored, not just its hexes: **shadows are banned** (depth comes from the 5-tier surface ladder + hairlines, so there is not one `box-shadow` on slide content), the **rule-line eyebrow** (`.s-label` uses a 24px `::before` rule + 0.20em caps, matching Hearth's "SM · W-6" eyebrow pair, NOT coffeeslide's full-width trailing rule), and Hearth's voice rules (sentence-case headings, no em dashes) which already match the site's. **Title + sign-off are the ink-900 bookends** with the `.dot-grid` brand texture (18px radial dots) — deliberately dot-grid and NOT Hearth's other texture `.grain-dark`, because [[the no-grain rule]] in Design Decisions records the user rejecting SVG-noise over dark backgrounds as reading like glitch/static; a crisp geometric pattern sidesteps that entirely. A quiet sage/ember radial wash keeps the dark slides from reading as generic black. Progress bar is the tri-role ramp sage→ember→sky, not the site's 4-color gradient. **19 slides** — **restructured 2026-07-28 to Chang's mentor's case-study storytelling framework** (high stakes upfront backed by UXR then narrowed to a scoped design statement · explain why the problem is HARD so the reader does not just see "a page" · design principles that follow from the complexity so the solution feels like the only correct answer · 10-15 min, explainable in under five). It was 21 slides in homewise.html's chronological order; the rebuild imposes an argument on the same material. New spine: Title → **The bet** ("People will hand the hire to an agent they can trust" + the 70/41/33 market stats, so the PMF question is stated as a bet before anything else) → Overview **+ my role merged** (the 4 "What I did" bullets as a `.note-list` beside the cover) → **Evidence · Eight interviews** (the 3 pain cards) → Insight ($250→$7,000 spread) → **Why this is hard** (a user problem / a data problem / one connected system) → **Design statement** (scope before shopping + the three named principles: structured not conversational · verified not scraped · the human signs off) → The workflow (Scope/Match/Compare/Track) → Process → **Final design 1-4** (was 5: "One scope, every contractor" folded into the scope slide's caption, since it is a consequence of that clip rather than its own beat) → Outcome → Design system → **Collaboration + shipped build merged** (the skipped-Figma story narrates over the proof video) → Reflection → **Hello** (the personal intro moved from slide 2 to here — in an interview you have already been introduced, so it costs the first minute before any stakes; as the penultimate slide it lands as "who made this") → Sign-off. The clip count drops 7→6 with the contractors slide. **The case study page was NOT changed to match 1:1** — see the homewise.html bullet; a deck is narrated live, a page is skimmed, and the judge in that audit explicitly killed giving the page a "The bet" section. **Four things that will bite a future edit:** (1) **The clips are 1920×1204, NOT 16:9** (and `homewise-live.mp4` is 1920×994) — an `aspect-ratio: 16/9` frame silently crops ~35px off the top and bottom of every clip. The frame is pinned to `1920 / 1204` with a `.clip--play` override at `1920 / 994`, so `object-fit` has nothing to crop and nothing to letterbox. `mdls` returns null for these mp4s; read `videoWidth`/`videoHeight` off a `<video>` in the browser instead. (2) **The clip's `flex-basis` MUST be 0, not `auto`** — with `auto` the aspect-ratio's own content size (width 100% → 1680 → height 945) feeds the basis and survives shrinking, so the frame overran the slide by 18px and got clipped by `overflow:hidden`. `flex: 1 1 0` + `min-height: 0` makes the height purely leftover free space. (3) **`fit()` reserves a 160px chrome lane, not coffeeslide's 76px** — the stage is CREAM here, so the light brand/counter/nav-circle chrome must stay fully inside the ink-900 letterbox or it lands on the slide and disappears. 160 guarantees ≥80px top and bottom at every viewport (verified 1440×900 and 1024×768: zero chrome/stage overlap). **Clip posters + two-slide warming 2026-08-03 (per user "design system video can not play in the actual live site"):** the video was never broken — live serves 200/`video/mp4` with **Range 206** (Safari's requirement), the `moov` atom is at the front (faststart), and the codec is H.264/`avc1`, not HEVC. The failure was PERCEPTUAL: the five looping clips were `preload="none"` **with no `poster`**, so arriving at a slide showed a flat `--canvas-deep` rectangle until enough of a 6-24MB file arrived (hearth alone is 24MB; on a 10 Mbps line that is ~20s of empty plate, worse on a cold `x-vercel-cache: MISS`). **TWO REAL PLAYBACK BUGS were found and fixed the same day, and the posters alone would NOT have been enough:** (i) **the clips were not actually playing on arrival** — `syncMedia()` called `v.play()` while the element was still at `readyState 0` (which is exactly where `preload="none"` leaves it), that play is aborted by the element's own resource selection, and the old `.catch(() => {})` swallowed it, so the slide sat on its poster looking frozen. Replaced with a `playClip()` helper that flips `preload='auto'` FIRST, calls play, and **retries once on `canplay`** (bailing if the deck moved on). Verify with a pixel diff — draw the video to a canvas twice and compare — because `paused`/`readyState` alone read as healthy while nothing moves. (ii) **preload was flipped to 'auto' on the way past and never flipped back**, so by the late slides every clip (~119MB) was still pulling in the background and starving whatever you were trying to watch; this is what made slide 16 crawl. Now a **sliding window**: current slide + next two are `auto`, everything else is released to `none` (verified: 3 warm at slide 10, 2 at 13, 0 by 16). Fixed by (a) **frame-0 posters for all five clips** (`images/homewise-{scope,match,compare,track,hearth}-poster.jpg`, 1400px/~110-136KB each) and (b) **warming the next TWO slides** instead of one. **Poster extraction technique, since this machine has NO ffmpeg:** `qlmanage -t` picks a *representative middle* frame, which makes the poster jump when playback starts at 0 — instead compile a ~15-line Swift `AVAssetImageGenerator` (`requestedTimeToleranceBefore/After = .zero`, `appliesPreferredTrackTransform = true`) with `swiftc`, which gives exact frame 0 so poster→playback is seamless. **Re-encoding was TRIED AND REJECTED:** `avconvert` exposes no bitrate control, only resolution presets, and `Preset1280x720` turned the 24MB hearth clip into 20MB at 1280x804 — a third of the resolution for a 17% saving. Not worth it; revisit only if ffmpeg is ever installed (CRF would halve size at full resolution). Gotcha: `avconvert` silently writes NO output file if you pass `--disableSubtitles`, and still exits 0.
- (4) **Videos use `preload="none"` + play-on-arrival**, because the six clips total ~100MB; `render()` plays the active slide's clip (which is what triggers its load), pauses and rewinds the others, and warms the NEXT slide's clip to `preload="auto"`. An IntersectionObserver would be wrong for the same reason `loading="lazy"` is wrong in a deck: slides move by `transform`, so they never leave the scroll viewport. Also note `.slide--media` trims only `padding-bottom` (84→56px) to hand the artifact more room — never `padding-top`, which would break the one-canvas-y header anchor. **NOT linked from the home grid or homewise.html** and **not in `sitemap.xml`** — standalone at `/homewiseslide`, exactly like coffeeslide. Self-contained (inline `<style>`+`<script>`, does NOT load style.css, so no version bump), loads Neue Montreal at **400,500** (500 for the editorial display ladder) and sets `font-weight` explicitly on every heading per the single-weight gotcha. **deck.gallery editorial reskin 2026-07-31 (per user "current designs are too ugly", referencing deck.gallery — SUPERSEDES this bullet's boxed-card/tri-ramp/eyebrow details; same 19 slides, same copy, same engine/comment mode):** the layout grammar moved from Hearth-tiled cards to editorial print, patterns lifted from three deck.gallery references (Geist*Studio Impact Report: stat rows + in-slide document chrome; Reece Evans Portfolio: cover with tiny metadata columns top + giant wordmark bottom; Lefty: persistent brand footer). What changed: (1) **every slide is framed by in-slide chrome** — `.s-label` became a full-width header row (label left, static "Homewise · Case study" via its `::after`, hairline under; the 24px `::before` dash is gone) and a `.page-foot` folio (rule + "A case study by Chang Mou" + "NN / 19") is **injected by JS at init** into each `.slide`, so markup stays clean and render() never touches it; the fixed `.deck-counter` element was **removed** (folio carries the number; letterbox chrome is now brand + nav only). (2) **No boxed cards anywhere**: stats are full-width hairline **rows** (`.rows`/`.row`, 112px numerals left, annotation pinned to a right 620px column — the Geist table); triads/stages are rule-topped **columns** (`.cols`/`.col` with tiny sage index, no backgrounds); the $250→$7,000 spread lost its inset well and its figures grew to 128px ember/ink. (3) **Bottom-anchored composition**: artifact blocks carry `.push` (`margin-top:auto; padding-top:44px`) so slides read title-zone-top / artifact-bottom instead of content floating in the top half with a cream void below (the user's core complaint); `.slide-inner > .split` gets `flex:1 1 0; min-height:0` so Overview/Hello center between header and folio. (4) **Title slide is Reece-style**: 4 tiny metadata columns top (Role/Team/Timeline/Built with), `margin-top:auto`, then sage kicker + **280px nowrap wordmark** + description bottom-right (`max-width:430px` — at 300px/520px it clipped off the right edge, the one real overflow hit during the build; keep h1 size and sub width paired). Sign-off mirrors it ("See it in full" at 170px). (5) **Outcome is a mid-deck ink statement slide** (`.slide--ink`: flat ink-900 + sage wash, NO dot grid — the dots stay reserved for the two bookends; 96px white title, CTAs bottom). (6) Media slides use a 2-col `.media-head` (46px title left, 19px annotation right) so the clip keeps its height. (7) Progress bar is **solid sage-400** (the tri-role sage→ember→sky ramp read decorative, replaced). (8) Reflection's closing highlight became a bottom-anchored 46px `.pull` line. **Probe gotcha recorded during verification:** inactive slides' reveal state (`translateY(18px)` on `.slide-inner > *`) inflates scrollHeight/bounding-rect probes by exactly 18px — a uniform +18 "overflow" across non-active slides is the entrance animation, not a layout bug.

The `.intro-grid` CSS + the 4 life photos are unused in this deck (left harmless).** Structural changes this session: (1) **the title slide now carries the product cover** — a `.title-cover` (flex-fill, `aspect-ratio: 2400/1065`, no crop, `rgba(255,255,255,0.16)` hairline) fills the band between the meta row and the wordmark (mentor: product must be visible before The bet; a standalone full-bleed cover slide 3 was built first, then merged into the title per user "merge slide 1 and 3"); Overview's split image became `homewise-track-poster.jpg` so the cover doesn't repeat. The conversation is where I make them", and a WORKFLOW LOOP SVG filling the middle (Describe -> agent argues tradeoffs -> I decide -> it builds live, right-angle loop-back "the next exploration, until it is right", dashed exit box "The canvas · when the team wants it" bridging to the handoff slide; drawn in the Process charts flow-box/sage grammar) ("In Figma · the canvas / I compose what I have decided" vs "In Claude Code · the conversation / I decide out loud: the agent argues each exploration's tradeoffs back") — written for a presentation TO the author of a UXMatters AI-innovation article (the "Stingray Model"), so it deliberately arrives at his problem-and-solution-shaped-together claim in Chang's OWN pre-existing vocabulary, zero words borrowed; **the beat was drafted onto the Process slide first and moved per user ("do not talk figma and claude code here") — Process stays tool-free**, and the Figma column stays positive per the standing rule ("exactly what a handoff needs"). The old order argued the tradeoff before the audience had seen the design. **DOM-order trap that broke this for one edit: ALL the deck's clip-geometry rules are `:last-child`-scoped, so the `.fd-lockup` must sit BEFORE `.clip` inside `.slide-inner`** (it is absolutely positioned, so DOM order is free) — lockup-after-clip strips the flex/aspect/margin rules and the clip renders unstyled; verified all five clips at exactly 1722×1080 canvas with the clip as last child. Probe note: a deep-linked clip can sit paused at ~0.8s in the pane (occlusion pauses muted video after the engine's canplay retry detaches) — manual play() advances fine, and real navigation re-triggers playClip; not a bug.** ORIGINAL FORM, now historical: **Final Design was FIVE decision/proof pairs** (labels "N of 5"; "One scope, every contractor" restored per user — the 2026-07-28 fold-into-scope is reversed): each decision slide = claim h2 + body + "The tradeoff" block (`.trade-k` eyebrow, `.trade-cols` rule-topped columns, `.tick--bad/--good`, `.trade-shot` evidence images, receipts at 18px); each proof slide is chromeless `.slide--full` (clip at full height 1722×1080, 99px ink mats, folio hidden via CSS, **the `margin: 0` reset on its clip is load-bearing** or the mats go asymmetric). New poster `homewise-contractors-poster.jpg` (frame-0 via the documented Swift recipe). Visuals are pulled from the framer reference site at `aware-acknowledge-592065.framer.app/nova` and stored as `images/nova-*.png`. **Cover optimized 2026-06-20 (load-speed pass):** the cover is now `images/nova-hero-unigirl.jpg` (1920px, JPEG q90, 349KB) — it was `nova-hero-unigirl.png` at **10448px / 28MB(!)** but displays at ≤960px, so it was downscaled to ~1920px (= 2× the display size) and converted PNG→JPEG via `sips -Z 1920 -s format jpeg -s formatOptions 90`; the 28MB PNG was deleted and the `<img>` src updated. **Full-page image optimization 2026-06-21 (load-speed pass, per user "images majorly large, loading too long"):** the whole page's image payload went **~154.7MB → 14.6MB (−90.5%)**. Audit found ~40 images, most 3840–7680px wide (one 10180px / 16.5MB!) but displaying at ≤960px, most as PNGs of photographic AR/app screenshots. Applied a real-transparency check via PIL (`alpha.getextrema()[0] < 250` — `sips -g hasAlpha` only reports the *channel*, not whether any pixel is actually transparent; most were opaque RGBA), then: **opaque PNGs → JPEG @ ≤1920px q90** (27 files, refs in nova.html updated `.png`→`.jpg`, old PNGs deleted — e.g. `nova-companion-dashboard` 4.6MB/7680×4320 → 0.17MB); **genuinely-transparent → kept PNG, downscaled** (2 partner logos to 220px tall, 3 `nova-c2-*` phone screens, `nova-mascot-exploration` 16.5MB→0.76MB on its #E5E5E5 panel, 3 `nova-research-*`); **recompressed in place** the two heavy comps (`nova-comp-meta-orion.webp`, `nova-comp-apple-roadmap.jpeg`, ~2MB→~0.2MB each). Verified in preview: all 41 imgs load, 0 broken, logos/HUD-text/mascot all visually clean. **Cache caveat:** the 27 renamed JPGs get fresh URLs, but the in-place recompressed files (logos, c2, comps, mascot-exploration, research) keep their filenames, so returning visitors may serve stale cached big versions until cache expires — add `?v=` if that matters. **`og-image.jpg` kept** (it's the share card). **The "still oversized elsewhere" items were RESOLVED 2026-07-25:** `coffeecover.png` (20896px/3.9MB — so large PIL's decompression-bomb guard refuses it; opened with `Image.MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS = None` for the alpha check, converted via sips) → `coffeecover.jpg` 1920px q90 **0.13MB** (ref updated in coffee.html), and `pollen-cover.jpg` downscaled in place (see the pollen.html bullet). nova.html + coffee.html also got the below-fold `loading="lazy" decoding="async"` pass the same day (covers stay eager), and index.html's Homewise card img dropped its incorrect `loading="lazy"` (row-1 = eager per the card-image convention). Downscaling to ~1920px (= 2× the ≤960px display) is compatible with the [[feedback_image_quality]] JPEG-100/PNG-lossless rule — at 2× display size the quality loss is invisible; it only drops resolution the screen physically can't show.

Final section order: Problem · Insight · Solution · **Process** · Final Design (ends on the outcome + live CTA) · Design System · Collaboration · Reflection. **Process section (`#process` + side-nav link, added 2026-06-19, sits after Solution):** the AI-era build-first story. Heading "I designed it by building it" + **one** paragraph (merged from two per user): the traditional discovery→mockups→handoff→build flow is dead, so Chang flipped the order — align with the PM, build, iterate on real feedback from team + users, with Claude Code as a thinking partner weighing each design exploration's pros/cons ("Less time making mockups, more time on the calls only a person can make"). Visualized by **two `.flow-chart` SVG blocks reusing Coffee Chat's `.flow-chart`/`.flow-svg`/`.flow-box`/`.flow-chart-tags` pattern (NO new CSS):** "The traditional process" (linear Discovery→Research→Mockups→Handoff→Build, ✕ tags) vs "How I work now" (Align with PM → Build → Feedback → Iterate, with a **right-angle, NOT curved** loop-back arrow Iterate→Build labeled "repeat until it is right", ✓ tags incl "Claude Code weighs each option"; the Build box is inline-highlighted brighter). **"chat with PM" was rejected as too casual → "Align with PM" (user) — keep it.** Closes with an inline credit to **Jenny Wen** (Head of Design for Claude at Anthropic), linked to her Lenny's Podcast episode "The design process is dead" (`lennysnewsletter.com/p/the-design-process-is-dead`); the inline `<a>` carries an inline `style` (color `var(--text)` + a 0.35-white underline) because case-study prose has no default inline-link affordance. **Mobile fix (applied 2026-06-19, supersedes the earlier "NOT yet fixed" caveat):** the SVG flow-charts scale to page width, so box labels dropped to ~3.3px on phones (same limitation as Coffee Chat's flow-charts). Fixed by wrapping each chart's `<svg>` in a `.flow-scroll` div and adding an inline `<style>` in homewise.html's `<head>` that, **on mobile (≤768px) only**, gives `#process ~ .flow-chart .flow-scroll` `overflow-x:auto` + `overflow-y:hidden` (the explicit `hidden` dodges the overflow-x-forces-overflow-y-auto gotcha) + a thin styled scrollbar, and holds `.flow-svg` at `min-width:800px`. Result: labels render ~9.2px and each chart scrolls horizontally **within its own strip** — the page itself does NOT overflow (verified), and the cut-off right edge + scrollbar thumb are the swipe affordance. **Desktop is untouched** (rules are media-gated, so `.flow-scroll` is a transparent passthrough div there and the SVG still fills the full card). **Homewise-scoped on purpose** (inline in homewise.html, NOT style.css), so Coffee Chat's shared `.flow-chart` is unaffected and still has the tiny-label issue. HTML-only — no style.css change, no version bump. **Process paragraph trimmed same day (per user "too long"): 144→102 words** — cut the prose that re-narrated the two charts' steps (the charts already show Discovery→…→Build and Align with PM→…→Iterate); kept the reframe, the "more time on the calls only a person can make" punchline, and a shortened Jenny Wen credit (dropped "at Anthropic"). Reflection carries two beats: a project insight ("Trust was the hard part, not the automation") + the Claude-Code-first-build/handoff takeaway. Content grounded in the repo's real VOC research (github.com/chang627627/homewise, `docs/` synthesis PDFs). **Clips complete (2026-06-12):** all 4 flow clips are live — `images/homewise-scope.mp4` ("Define the scope"), `images/homewise-contractors.mp4` ("One scope, every contractor"), `images/homewise-match.mp4` ("A match you can trust" — the 3-contractors-side-by-side matching screen with Verified/FLAGGED badges), `images/homewise-compare.mp4` ("Compare apples to apples, then you sign off" — the "Quotes are in" 3-bid same-scope comparison); no `.img-placeholder` boxes remain. A 5th `hw-clip`, `images/homewise-hearth.mp4`, sits in the Design System section (walkthrough of the live /designsystem page). Each live clip is wrapped in `<div class="hw-clip project-img-full">` containing the `<video loop muted playsinline preload="metadata">` plus a `.hw-clip-progress > .hw-clip-progress-bar` **playback progress bar** (thin 4px bar pinned to the video's bottom edge so viewers see start/end and position; the wrapper's `overflow:hidden` + radius clips it to the rounded corners). The inline script before the chat widget does two things per clip: drives the progress bar via rAF (tied to play/pause so it's smooth and resets cleanly on loop) and runs play.html's IntersectionObserver to defer play to the viewport. The `project-img-full` class on the **wrapper** gives the clip the same 24px caption→artifact / 96px artifact→next spacing as the case-study images (the `.img-placeholder` adjacency rules were added to style.css for the same reason). All 5 clips (4 flow + the Hearth walkthrough) use this exact `hw-clip` wrapper. **Clip conversion (no ffmpeg on this machine):** screen-recordings are H.264 Retina .mov (~3456px, ~40MB); transcode with Apple's `avconvert -s in.mov -o images/<name>.mp4 -p Preset1920x1080 --replace` (H.264, NOT the HEVC presets — poor browser support; → ~1920px, ~15MB, fast-start). Videos live in `images/`. **The home card links here** (`href="homewise.html"`) as of 2026-06-12, once all 5 clips landed (it previously pointed at the live demo). Homewise-specific CSS is the `hw-` block at the end of style.css; page chrome stays portfolio-dark while artifacts carry Hearth's warm palette. style.css bumped to v=115 on this page. **Enriched from the Demo Day pitch deck (2026-06-17, source `homewise-pitch.vercel.app` — the PM Accelerator AI Bootcamp deck for the same product):** added, without breaking the lean format — (a) a `.hw-stats` market-stat band in the Problem after `.voc-quotes` (label "Beyond our eight interviews"; 70% fear an unreliable contractor / 41% deceived by a provider / 33% delay repairs; source "Leaf Home / Morning Consult, 2025. HomeServe, 2025." — a dark panel, sibling of `.hw-spread`; the warm `#B98132` top-accent bars on each stat were **removed 2026-06-17** as meaningless decoration per the user, so the numbers stand alone; the `.hw-stats + .project-section { margin-top:96px }` rule re-asserts the Problem→Insight gap that `.voc-quotes + .project-section` used to give); (b) the trust split "14% trust review platforms, 61% word of mouth" woven into the Problem intro; (c) **[added then removed same day, 2026-06-17, per user — do NOT re-add]** a `.hw-flow` map (Photo → Scope → Match → Quotes → Booked) briefly opened Final Design above the first clip; the user deleted it (the four clips already walk the flow) and its CSS was removed; (d) verification specifics in the "match you can trust" caption (license, insurance, real permit history); (e) a Collaboration sentence naming what the prototype became (Claude vision, Shovels.ai permits, Twilio); (f) a Reflection beat "trust is a data problem as much as an AI one"; (g) sidebar engineers 5→7 (it was a 13-person team: 3 PM / 3 design / 7 eng — user kept the "Lead Product Designer" framing). **Deliberately skipped** to protect the lean format: the 3 pitch personas (David/Maya/Ray) and the full architecture diagram. The Insight copy was left as-is (already lands the symptom/cause point). style.css bumped v=160→v=161. **Problem + Solution rework + two videos (2026-06-17 — SUPERSEDES the stat-band "numbers stand alone", the 14%/61% "woven into the Problem intro", the `.hw-flow`, and the "All 5 clips" notes above):** (1) **Problem VOC → scannable pain-point cards** via a homewise-only modifier `.voc-quotes--pain` (so TickerPulse's shared `.voc-quotes` is untouched): each card leads with a white pain **headline** ("No way to know who to trust" / "No benchmark for a fair price" / "Comparing quotes is exhausting") and demotes the real interview quote below it to muted italic evidence (single-weight font, so the hierarchy is color+size, not bold). The other ~10 headings were judged already-scannable and left as-is — they're insight-statements, not generic labels, so skimming the h2 stack alone tells the whole story. **(6) Process — PM/PRD collaboration beat added, then re-tightened 2026-06-27 (per user; SUPERSEDES item (4)'s quoted sentence and the 144→102-word note for the *current* Process paragraph text):** the user asked what real PM↔designer collaboration looks like (the PRD as the shared artifact) so it could be written into Process. The prototype exposes gaps the document cannot, which feed back into the PRD."* The bidirectional loop (prototype surfaces gaps → back into the PRD) is the deliberate sophistication, and it counters the traditional-chart's own "spec thrown over the wall" critique. (b) applying several recommendations independently put **"ratings can be gamed" on the page four times** (Problem setup + principle + table cell + Match caption) — the payoff now lands once, in the Match caption, at the screen that resolves it. (c) the audit's rec-2 **named principles block was built then DELETED by the user** ("feel too wordy"): the three rules (structured/verified/recommended, not X) now live folded into the Solution paragraph as prose — "Every step stays checkable: the scope is editable before it goes out, the trust signals are public records rather than ratings, and the agent recommends but never books." **Do NOT re-add a stated-principles list to this page**; the deck keeps its Design-statement slide, and that divergence is intended. **Hero sub reworked 2026-07-28 (audit rec 7, per user):** now "AI command center for homeowners. So a compressed mechanic was kept. **The four meta/og/twitter descriptions were deliberately NOT changed** — they are search and social-preview copy, where a bare stat without context is worse than a plain description of the product. **General lesson: an audit recommendation's rationale can be invalidated by a later edit in the same session; re-check the premise before applying a queued recommendation.** **HITL naming pass 2026-07-31 (per user, from a Substack HITL-design essay):** the page and deck now NAME the pattern the product already implements — "human-in-the-loop" appears exactly twice on the site: in the Solution's white-highlighted sentence (recorded frozen in the audit kills above; **amended with explicit user approval** to "structured, human-in-the-loop workflow") and in the deck's The-human-signs-off card ("Human in the loop, never an autopilot default"). the story shot pins `Math.random=()=>0` for the "Goodnight, moon garden" variant and sets lav/rain/tangled/crane per the deck's worked example), serve the clone with python http.server, capture each shot with headless Chrome `--window-size=620,1100 --force-device-scale-factor=2 --default-background-color=00000000 --virtual-time-budget=9000`, then PIL-composite the fixed-geometry crops (bezel at (193,220)-(1047,1980) @2x) onto a recreated app-wash gradient (#fff7ea→#f1e6ff→#ffe0cf). **Plate decision, iterated three times 2026-08-16 and now SETTLED per the user's pasted reference (their own card-cover style): phones sit on a BARELY-THERE pale backdrop (vertical (242,236,250)→(253,248,238)), captures take `.phone-bezel { filter: none !important }` so the app's purple drop-shadow glow is gone at the source, the harness ALSO freezes every in-phone animation at its base position (`.phone, .phone * { animation: none !important; transition: none !important; }` — without this, virtual-time capture parks the app's floating decorative blobs mid-drift, including ON TOP of CTAs, which the user read as 'an extra layer on the phone... button color is not consistent'; base positions are the designed layout, matching the app's own exports; note the CTA gradient #b8c2ff→#8f9eff is the DESIGNED button, not a defect), and the mask is the capture's OWN ALPHA SILHOUETTE (threshold 128 → MinFilter(3) erode → GaussianBlur 1.2), which follows the bezel's true corner radius — a geometric bezel+24px mask leaked a ring of ambient tone that read as light around the dark sleep phone's corners. NOTHING renders outside the bezel, verified by pixel-sampling just off the phone edges and corners (within JPEG noise of the far corner). Two rejected versions, do not revive: (1) the app's own three-stop wash #fff7ea→#f1e6ff→#ffe0cf ('too much light... do not have other gradient layer'), (2) flat #0a0a0a page-matching plates, where the bezel drop-shadow's rgba(180,130,220) layer read as a light halo ('outside of the screen there are some gradient. do not need these'). The original gotcha still applies in spirit: the raw crop carries the app's ambient background blobs at low alpha, and only the feathered mask keeps them out of the plate. **THE WASH THE USER KEPT CATCHING ('extra layer on the phone', 'button too bright', 'delete these blue and cream glow') was REAL and took four wrong suspects to find: the app's `.bg-deco::before/::after` page-ambience radials (blue #cbb8ff top-left, cream #ffd1aa bottom-right, blur 60, opacity .55) are positioned pseudo-elements on BODY that paint in DOM order AFTER the `.stage` — i.e. OVER THE PHONE, hazing its corners and CTAs (cream at ~20% over the periwinkle button pinned its blue channel at 238 vs the designed 255). Every standard probe missed them because pseudo-elements appear in neither `elementsFromPoint` (which also skips `pointer-events:none`) nor ancestor-chain walks — falsely-cleared suspects on the way: harness patch, headless flags (bg/profile/budget — a minimal color-swatch page round-tripped EXACTLY), decorative `.blob`s (a hide-and-pixel-diff proved they never touch the button), the frame PNG interior, and the status-bar/home-indicator PNGs (all fully transparent where it matters). The harness now `display:none`s `.bg-deco::before/::after` + `.sky-dots`, and the captured button matches the design gradient to within 2 RGB. LESSON for any 'phantom overlay' hunt: replicate the suspect element in a minimal page first (instant pipeline exoneration), then bisect with hide-rules + `ImageChops.difference` — and remember pseudo-elements are invisible to every DOM hit-testing probe. COMPANION FIX: `animation: none` leaves ANY `opacity:0 + animation-forwards` reveal permanently invisible — the story screen's `.line` spans (lineIn) shipped one commit blank; the harness forces `.line { opacity:1; transform:none }`. Audit `grep forwards` when freezing animations. PRODUCT-REPO FIX SHIPPED 2026-08-16, THEN AMENDED SAME DAY: first the `.bg-deco::before/::after` glows were DELETED per user (c6bb32b), but that left the app near-white (user: 'why background is white?') because the two radials WERE most of the page's color — the body's own wash is faint. Final state (edad693 on github.com/chang627627/StoryBloom): **glows RESTORED with `.stage { z-index: 1 }`**, so the ambience paints BEHIND the phone — color back everywhere, and the hazing bug is structurally fixed (verified: welcome CTA blue channel 255, corners tinted). `.sky-dots` untouched throughout. The case-study captures still hide the glows entirely via the harness, which remains correct for flat artifact plates.** Verified desktop + 375 (no overflow, cover insets to 327px, side-nav/back-to-top/footer-big hidden), scroll-spy active, zero console errors, all images serve. **Full-screen-coverage pass same day (per user "please include all the phone screens in these slides", with the two award-deck PDFs from ~/Downloads as the reference):** the page now shows EVERY phone screen from the repo's App Walkthrough deck (the Case Study deck has no phone screens, it is diagrammatic). Page total: 8 images. **PDF-reading note for this machine: no poppler and no PyObjC, so the Read tool cannot render PDFs — compile a ~40-line swiftc PDFKit renderer (`pdfrender <pdf> <outdir> <scale> [first] [last]`, pattern lives in the session transcript) and Read the PNGs; the harness ALSO misreports PDF page counts wildly (claimed 645/178, real 8/7), so trust PDFKit's pageCount, not the system-reminder.**

Every changed asset carries `?v=3` across countersign.html, countersignslide.html and index.html. **PRODUCT-REPO BUG FOUND AND NOT PUSHED: `design-preview.html`'s Typography board `<code>` labels are stale at HEAD** (they still read 20/700, 18/700, 17/700, 14/700, 10.5/600, mono 10, 11.5, 10/700) while that same file's own `.t-*` CSS already renders the new tokens — the "sync design-preview to the shipped tokens" commit updated the CSS and missed the labels. The labels were corrected **locally in the scratch clone** so the captured board is truthful, and the fix is NOT in the repo; the same file's "Shape, line & depth" section is stale too (says 4 / 6 / 10 px against tokens 6 / 8 / 12) but is not inside any portfolio crop. Offer to push the one-file fix so the live preview page and the portfolio boards agree. **SAME-DAY FOLLOW-UP (user: "current version did not screenshot correctly") — the re-sync captures had a real framing defect, all re-shot and re-derived:** the capture harness framed by scrolling the gate to a FIXED viewport y (`frame('.gate', 445)`), which after the part-2 spacing changes left ELEMENTS SLICED MID-CARD directly under the sticky plan bar — the gate shot had flag banners cut in half, the rework shot had the restructure tiles' label row chopped so the numbers floated context-free, and the cover + home-card still inherited it since they are composed from the gate capture. A top-slice under a sticky bar reads as a broken screenshot; a bottom-edge slice reads as page continuation and is fine. **The durable recipe (now in scratch lib.mjs as `alignUnder`/`alignUnderText`): never frame at a fixed y — scroll so a chosen element's TOP sits ~12-14px below the sticky `.plan` bar's bottom, anchoring on a real boundary:** the last `.step__title` for gate/note/autonomy, the LAST `.art__head` containing "Proposed restructure" for rev-2 shots (index 0 is an earlier tool artifact — anchor by TEXT, not position), and `.whatif` for the stress panel (its align hits scroll-max, which happens to leave the gate's amend row + verbs complete above it — acceptable). Light/dark theme pairs framed by the same anchor come out scroll-identical, which the theme 2-up and flip.webp require. Then verify the target's rect fits: rev-2 gate is 699px tall (thread + restructure line), so anchoring its step title overflows the frame bottom by 27px and cuts the verbs — the tiles-head anchor is the one that fits. All 8 app captures + loop tail + cover + card still + theme/flip re-derived; deck object-positions retuned AGAIN per the documented gotcha (gate 86→71, note 86→74, rework 55→65, whatif-flip 85→74, autonomy 40→69, signature 23→58); changed assets bumped `?v=4`. **THEN a THIRD full re-screen the same session (user caught two more defects: "why is it purple" on plan-review, "why two borders" on the gate; then "re-screen every image") — root cause, and the biggest harness lesson of the day: BOTH the plan-review card and the gate call `ref.focus()` on mount, and puppeteer's SYNTHETIC clicks don't register as mouse modality, so Chrome matched `:focus-visible` and baked focus rings into the screenshots — the purple UA-default ring on `.planreview` (which defines no focus style) and the gate's own teal `outline-offset: 3px` ring stacking on its border as a double ring. Verified with a REAL trusted click in the browser pane: no rings — real users never see them, so blurring is honest. The harness now has `deFocus(p)` (blur activeElement + park the mouse at 4,4 + 250ms) called before EVERY screenshot — treat it as mandatory for ANY future capture of this app, and check any capture of any app that programmatically focuses on mount for the same artifact.** All 11 screens + all 5 t-*-new crops + loop + cover + card still + theme/flip re-derived at identical anchors (rects byte-matched the previous pass, so the deck object-positions did NOT need a third retune); everything bumped `?v=5`. Also on record: the product's PlanReview section has NO custom focus style, so a real KEYBOARD user gets the purple UA ring there instead of the app's teal focus language — a genuine product a11y-styling gap (the gate solved it with the `.gate:focus`/`.gate:focus-visible` pair); worth offering as a product-repo fix alongside the stale design-preview labels. **Deck is 39 slides as of the same day: the problem beat is TWO triad slides between The bet and Overview (per user, iterated within the hour: first "one slide talk about user problem and business problem" shipped as a 2-col slide, then "maybe two slides. each user problem and business split into three cards" replaced it — the 2-col variant with its inline 32px/21px scale-up is GONE).** Slide 3 "The user problem · The work is mechanical. The accountability is not" (cards: Days before judgment / A signature, not a submit / Checking costs as much as doing) and slide 4 "The business problem · The fast memo or the defensible record, never both" (cards: Slow answers lose borrowers / Black box speed is a liability / The record is the product), with paired s-bodies ("Read from the analyst's seat…" / "Read from the lender's ledger…"). All card claims ground in the already-verified FDIC/Wolters Kluwer facts or the case study's own thesis — no new stats. **Dedupe from the first iteration STANDS: slide 2 (The bet) no longer describes the mechanical memo work and keeps only the bet + the stat rows — the problem slides argue, the bet's rows prove.** All slide comments renumbered; deep links and index-keyed pins from 3 on shift by two vs the 37-slide deck (comment store verified empty, so nothing actually moved). **Deck is 40 slides as of 2026-08-31: homewise slide 9 ("I flipped the process and designed by building", the two flow charts) PORTED as slide 11, directly after "Behavior cannot be mocked" (per user "add homewise slide's page 9 to countersign because I may present them separately" — the same present-one-deck-alone motive as the method block, which now means BOTH the flipped-process slide and the method slides travel with every deck).** Solo adaptations, same discipline as the method-block port: "Align with PM" box → "Set the intent" and the body's PM/PRD beat → "a written intent, the repo's own backlog" (Countersign has no PM — an unedited port would hand an interviewer a false box); ✓ tag "Feedback from team and users" → "Feedback from every real run" (no user testing is claimed anywhere in this case study, fabrication ban); sage arrows/Build-box tint → teal (#0F7D8C arrows, box #E4EEF0/#9FC4CB/#0A4A54) per the sage-is-only-✓✕ accent rule; marker ids csArrowOldP/csArrowNewP. Ordering argument, deliberate: slide 10 is WHY building was necessary (behavior/pacing), slide 11 is HOW the build-first loop runs — the body avoids restating the mocked-backend beat. All `.flow*` CSS already existed in the deck from the transplant, so the insert is markup-only. If homewise's process slide copy changes, mirror here (adaptations aside).
- `countersignslide.html` — **Slide-deck version of the Countersign case study (added 2026-08-30, per user "use the repo to make presentation slides… check previous version on github to discuss tradeoff like you did for homewise"). FOURTH deck, transplanted from homewiseslide.html** (not coffeeslide — homewise is the donor with the tradeoff grammar: `.trade-cols`, `.shot`/`.shot--crop`, `.media-head`, `.crumb`, `.derive`, ink slides), shared light system per the one-system rule. **Per-deck accent = the app's OWN teal both engines: `--accent #0F7D8C` (light) / `--accent-dark #2BB8CC` (on ink surfaces)** — sage/ember stay ONLY as the shared ✓/✕ flag colors; the sage-accent usages (progress bar, col-idx, kicker, bookend washes, comment-chrome tints) were repointed to the teal pair. **26 slides:** Title (the ink bookend's field is the DARK-ENGINE rev-2 gate capture `countersign-dark.jpg` under a two-ended scrim — the novaslide scrim pattern; `.tm-k/.tm-v` overridden to on-dark) · The bet ("An answer nobody can inspect is not an answer anyone can sign" + the page's verified 1 day/10 days/28.4% rows) · Overview + role (mini-grid) · Insight ("The signature is the product" + the decide() one-liner as a `.cmd` card) · the rule as a TREE ("Two questions, three exits, one gate", flow-box SVG, teal arrows, the app's own status-dot colors) — **rebuilt 2026-08-30 (per user "what is the difference between decline and escalate"): the leaves now carry MEANINGS, not categories ("recommend signing the deal" / "recommend killing the deal" / "hand it to human judgment"), each with its real sample deal (Meridian clean · Cobalt breach+risk 81 · Atlas breach+risk 57), plus an s-body stating the rule: breach blocks approval, risk >= 75 declines, below it escalates to a person; the page's cs-flow tree got FULL parity (per user "did you update the site for this slide's content as well"): the same three sublabels PLUS a third leaf line citing each sample deal (leaves 58 to 82px tall, gate shifted +24, viewBox 392 to 416), the risk-75 explanation appended to the cs-rule-note, and the Solution table's Decide cell corrected (a breach flips to escalate, OR to decline when risk runs high — the old cell claimed escalate only). Decline = the agent's no; escalate = the agent's not-mine-to-decide. The old "a recommendation / a handoff" sublabels did not explain the split even to the deck's owner — leaf labels on a decision tree must say what the exit MEANS, not what kind of artifact it is** · Why this is hard ×3 · The loop (5 `.derive` rows, stage ← reason) · Process ("Behavior cannot be mocked, so I built it") · **five Final-Design TRIPLETS (2026-08-30 per user "each final design need to have three page structure like homewise did. two slide are two crowded" — SUPERSEDES the original pairs; deck now 33 slides, DS slides 28-31, Reflection 32, Sign-off 33, index-keyed pins shift): (a) CLAIM slide (76px title full width + s-body + the .shot--crop preview window), (b) FULL-SCREEN slide (.slide--full with a STILL — new CSS `.slide--full .slide-inner > .shot:last-child { margin: 0 }` + border/radius reset gives images the homewise film-frame treatment, 1806×1080 between 57px ink mats), (c) TRADEOFF slide (unchanged). Crumb `Plan · Gate · Rework · What-if · Autonomy`. NOTE the full slides read +18px scrollHeight while inactive, the documented reveal-state probe artifact** **PLUS the homewise method block, PORTED as slides 32-34 (2026-08-30 per user "add 28,29,30 of homewise into countersign slides too, because sometimes I just present one study case" — the method slides travel with EVERY deck now; deck is 36 slides, Reflection 35, Sign-off 36): Canvas-and-conversation (label "Process ·" not "Collaboration ·" since Countersign is solo; teal arrows, marker id csArrowConv; the five-versions line adapted to "each won one rebuild at a time" since Countersign's old versions SHIPPED first), The harness (col 03 is Countersign's REAL gate — tsc --noEmit strict + the typed AgentEvent contract, NOT a design linter it does not have; the .term is a real npm run build at HEAD: 36 source files, 0 errors, vite 669ms — re-run before restating), and The ecosystem (the three homewise-eco-*.jpg tiles are SHARED assets, Chang's tooling + the industry, deck-agnostic; col 02 notes Countersign's DESIGN.md is literally in the awesome-design-md format). If homewise's method slides change, mirror here. **DESIGN-SYSTEM SECTION REBUILT 2026-08-31 (deck 40 to 41 slides) after the USER deployed the system themselves** (product-repo commit `dfb21a0 feat: deploy the design system at /designsystem, organized to the reference convention`, on a FORCE-PUSHED history, every SHA changed, so a stale clone must `git reset --hard origin/main`; my own staged public/ move was discarded as redundant). **The live board is `https://credit-analysis-agent.vercel.app/designsystem`** (Vercel serves `public/designsystem/index.html` as a clean `/designsystem`, exactly like homewise, no vercel.json needed), and it grew 6 to 13 sections: Overview, Colors, Typography, Layout, Elevation and Depth, Shapes, Components, Component specimens, Motion, Dos and Donts, Responsive Behavior, Iteration Guide, Known Gaps. Consequences applied: (a) **new opening slide 32, Graphite and Teal, four ideas and a token for each**, a `.cols--4` triad-plus-one lifted from the live page's own Overview (Atmosphere / One accent, one meaning / The record is monospaced / Depth by surface), so the section now OPENS on the argument and hands evidence to the boards, matching every other beat's claim-then-artifact shape; it carries BOTH links (live and DESIGN.md) and the colour slide's duplicate spec link was removed. (b) The three boards were **re-cropped from the LIVE page** (`countersign-ds-{color,type,components}.jpg`, `?v=6`; Component specimens replaced the old Components crop and now includes both signature blocks, the human hand and the auto policy). (c) **The two TALL boards moved to `shot--crop` full-width windows** (`object-position: 50% 0`): a contained `.shot` is HEIGHT-constrained, so a 2240x1564 board rendered about 850px wide inside a 1700px column with big empty margins, the documented floating-in-whitespace failure; full-width shows about 854 of 1564 rows legibly and the live link covers the remainder. The type board stays contained (wider and shorter, it fits). (d) countersign.html: prose now says the system is deployed beside the app, captions trued, and a new first CTA, Explore Graphite and Teal live, sits ahead of Read the design spec. **Rule going forward: the boards are captures of a LIVE page, so re-crop from the URL, not from a repo file.**** · One memo two signatures (the ink-flourish signature block) · Portfolio · Audit · Outcome (ink, "refuse to sign", live + repo CTAs) · Design system (light half of the token board, `countersign-system-light.jpg`) · Theming (the 2-up) · Reflection (pull line) · Sign-off. **The tradeoff column headers are "The version that shipped first" ✕ / "Why it changed" ✓ — deliberately NOT homewise's "The version I didn't ship", because Countersign's old state WAS shipped** (live on Vercel + in the case study for 2.5 months); evidence pairs are matched-aspect crops `countersign-t-{plan,gate,rework,whatif,auto}-{old,new}.jpg`, old side captured from a `git worktree` build of 40c3b7d (the documented old-commit pipeline), and the first tradeoff slide carries the provenance line in a `.src`. The five beats: instant-start → intent gate; two-buttons-no-note → four verbs + note on the record; reject-only → send-back + Structuring-Engine cure through the SAME decide rule; blank tracks → flip zones + headroom + attach; every-run-gated → auto-clean policy with visible countdown (✕ = Meridian's spotless approve still demanding the click). **FD media slides use `shot--crop` full-width windows with per-image `object-position`** (gate/autonomy 50% 74%, rework 50% 33%, whatif 50% 85%, signature 50% 23%) — the contained `.shot` rendered ~840px wide floating in cream, the exact whitespace complaint the homewise FD stills resolved. Engine untouched (works off `slides.length`; no videos, the clip machinery is inert); comment keys `countersignslide_comments_v1`/`countersignslide_commenter`; `/countersignslide` rewrite added to vercel.json and **'countersignslide' added to api/comments.js DECKS (shared comments need the deploy; localStorage fallback meanwhile)**. Standalone like the other decks: not linked from the grid or countersign.html, not in sitemap. **Design system expanded to FOUR slides 2026-08-30 (per user "add design system into the slides too"; deck now 28 slides — Reflection 27, Sign-off 28, index-keyed comment pins shift, known consequence):** slides 23-26 = Color (annotation carries the teal-means-live/active/yours + never-mix laws and the deck's link idiom to the DESIGN.md blob) · Type ("Inter is the voice, Geist Mono is the record") · Components (specimens, dashed-marks-sandbox, the reserved gate glow) · Theming (**`countersign-flip.webp` animated in the deck, `<picture>` reduced-motion fallback to the static 2-up; `.shot picture { display: contents }` is the guard — without it the picture wrapper breaks the shot flex sizing**). The boards are the product repo's own design-preview.html captured at 2x (the same `countersign-ds-*.jpg` files the page pager uses, 2240px wide); `countersign-system-light.jpg` is now deck-orphaned. Verified: 28 folios, zero slide overflow, zero broken images, mobile chrome hidden. **Deck is 37 slides as of the 2026-08-30 repo re-sync (was 36): a fourth slide inside the Gate stage, "The note it drafts, and never signs" (media slide, crumb keeps Gate bolded, so the 5-stage crumb is unchanged), sits after the Gate tradeoff.** The numbered `<!-- N · -->` slide comments were renumbered in document order; index-keyed comment pins shift again (known consequence). **RETUNING GOTCHA, and it applies to every deck built on this engine: a `shot--crop` `object-position` is a constant tuned to ONE capture's scroll position, not a property of the screen — re-capturing the same screen invalidates it.** After the re-sync, gate went 74% → **86%**, autonomy 74% → **40%**, rework 33% → **55%**; at the old values the gate slide cut its own three verbs off and the autonomy slide cut the auto-countersign countdown, i.e. each slide hid the exact thing its title claims. Retune by measurement, not by eye: `scale = winW / naturalW`, `over = naturalH*scale - winH`, visible source band = `[over*p/100/scale, that + winH/scale]`, then read the target element's y-range straight off the capture (a colour scan for the gate's teal ring works well). Also note the deck HTML itself is never cache-busted, so a `?cb=` query is required before measuring, or the probe reads the OLD object-position values and every number is wrong.

- `404.html` — **Custom 404 page (added 2026-08-01, Mobbin interaction/visual research pass; Vercel serves `/404.html` automatically on static deployments).** Site-grammar error page: standard nav, `.hero` + `.hero-orbs--g2` gradient, plain-text h1 "Lost in the fog." (callback to the old © line; NO per-glyph machinery — a bare h1 inherits the hero type styles fine), sub "This page doesn't exist. The work does.", two `.status-tag` pills (Back to the work → /index.html, Say hello → mailto) in a page-local `.notfound-tags` flex row, and the same gated Lightfall+tilt script as home minus the type machinery. **All asset/nav hrefs are ABSOLUTE ("/style.css?v=224", "/index.html") on purpose — a 404 renders at arbitrary broken URLs, so relative paths would 404 too.** `noindex`, NOT in sitemap.xml, deliberately NO chat widget and NO footer block (single-viewport utility page). GA included per convention.
- `style.css` — Shared styles across all pages
- `api/chat.js` — Vercel serverless function behind the chat widget (Claude Haiku 4.5). Its `SYSTEM_PROMPT` embeds the resume + a PORTFOLIO PROJECTS list. **When the resume, experience, awards, or project lineup change, update this prompt too** — it silently served year-old facts until the 2026-06-11 refresh. Not runnable on the local python preview (`/api/chat` 404s locally); test against production after deploy. **The 2026-06-15 TickerPulse open thread was CLOSED 2026-07-26** (improvement-research P2): its PORTFOLIO entry now says the wireframes are in a Play-page carousel and explicitly tells the model not to direct visitors to a case-study page. **Endpoint hardened the same day:** CORS wildcard replaced with an origin allowlist (www.chang-mou.com / chang-mou.com / chang-mou-portfolio.vercel.app; requests with a foreign Origin get 403), message capped at 2000 chars, history sanitized (user/assistant roles only, 2000-char cap per item — blocks role injection), and a best-effort in-memory rate limit (20 req / 5 min per IP per warm instance, `globalThis.__chatRate`).

## Design Decisions
- **Font:** Neue Montreal (via Fontshare CDN) — single font, weight 400 (regular) everywhere
- **Color:** Dark mode (#0a0a0a background, #1a1a1a surface, #f0f0f0 text, #888 muted). **Accent blue:** `#0094FF` (matches the blue in NOVA's AR UI — sampled from `images/nova-hud-text-example.png`). Used for legend circles, the HUD Text "Selected" image border, and Solution Evaluation Matrix highlighted-row accent + tint. When adding new blue accents, reuse `#0094FF` (or `rgba(0, 148, 255, ...)` for transparent variants) so the page stays unified.
- **Blue scope:** `#0094FF` is the **NOVA case study's** accent (it matches the AR UI). It should NOT bleed into page chrome that lives across the site — e.g. the side-nav active-state indicator uses `var(--text)` (white) instead of the blue, so the chrome stays neutral while the case-study content keeps its own brand voice.
- **Motion tokens (added 2026-07-26, UI-craft review P8):** `--ease-out: cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1)` (the curve the site already used on its best transitions), `--dur-fast: 100ms`, `--dur-base: 160ms`, `--dur-slow: 400ms`. **Use these for any new transition.** The governing idea is the **acknowledgment/scenery split**: a hover must ANSWER the pointer in ~160ms while decorative travel stays slow. Applied to `.project-card` — border-color + box-shadow dropped **400ms → 160ms** while `transform`/`opacity` stay 400ms and `.project-img img` keeps its 800ms glide (verified computed: `0.4s, 0.16s, 0.16s, 0.4s`). **Do NOT re-unify the card's durations** — the asymmetry is the point. Also converted: nav/footer/about/play link underlines, `.play-card`, `.carousel-arrow`, `.hamburger`, carousel dots (~21 token usages). **Deliberately NOT swept across the ~40 remaining `0.3s/0.2s ease` transitions** — at those durations the curve swap is imperceptible and would touch the chat panel, lightbox and mobile overlay for no visible gain; convert them opportunistically when editing those components. `.impact-link::after` was left on plain `ease` because it is case-study-only (out of the review's scope).
- **Radius:** 8px globally via `--radius` CSS variable
- **Slide decks are LIGHT and share ONE system (2026-08-03, per user "i think we need to slides light mode" → chose "Both decks, one shared light system"). This SUPERSEDES the palette-is-per-project rule below — do not re-split the palettes.** Coffee Chat was converted from the site's dark canvas to **Hearth's ramp, which homewiseslide already used**, so the two decks are now literally value-identical: canvas `#F6F4EF`, card `#FFFFFF`, deep well `#EEEAE1`, ink `#1A1A17` / `#6B6B65` / `#A8A8A1`, hairlines `#E7E5DE` / `#CFCEC7` / `#A8A8A1`, and shared flag colours (good = sage `#4F6942` on `#E2E8DC`, bad = ember `#8E601E` on `#F5E4CE` — Homewise's `.tick--*` convention, now Coffee's `.flow-ic--*`/`.tag--*` too). **The ONLY per-deck difference is `--accent`** (Coffee `#2456D6`, a deepened Litespace blue that holds contrast on cream; Homewise sage) plus Coffee's lavender `--plate: #d0d1fd`. Coffee kept its old token NAMES (`--bg`/`--text`/`--line`) and just repointed the values, which is why the diff is small; Homewise needed no change at all. **Four structural rules that make a light deck work, all learned the hard way here:** (1) **the letterbox must stay ink** — a new `--letterbox: #1A1A17` backs `html/body/.deck` while `.slide` is cream, or the light fixed chrome (brand, nav circles) vanishes into a cream frame. (2) **The two bookends stay INK** — `.slide--title, .slide--end { background: var(--letterbox) }`; the warm→blue gradient is a *wash on the dark base*, and without that base the slide inherits cream and the white wordmark washes out completely (this shipped broken for one render and is the most visible failure mode). (3) **The mid-deck peak inverts**: Coffee's Reflection went from `.slide--lav` (light-on-dark-deck) to `.slide--ink` (dark-on-light-deck), matching homewiseslide's Outcome slide — a light deck gets one dark peak. (4) **Flow-chart SVGs carry INLINE white** (`stroke="rgba(255,255,255,0.4)"` on 18 arrows/connectors plus white marker fills) which is invisible on cream — those attributes had to be recoloured in the markup, not just the CSS. **(5) EVERY fixed chrome element lives in the ink letterbox, so it must use `--on-dark*`, never `--text`/`--text-muted`** — `.deck-brand`, `.deck-hint` (+ its `kbd`), `.navbtn` (all three circles), `.navbtn--comment.on`, `.cmt-count` and `.cmt-bar`. This one is invisible in a contact sheet: the nav circles still drew their light borders, so they looked present, but the chevron/bubble SVGs are `stroke: currentColor` and inherited ink — **three empty rings on a dark letterbox**, which is exactly how the user reported it. **Method note: the first leftover-scan only grepped for WHITE literals and therefore missed the inverse class of bug entirely — dark surfaces that now need to be light.** A second scan for dark literals found `.cmt-pop` (`rgba(20,20,20,0.97)`) and `.pin` (`#1c1c1c` with `color: var(--text)`), i.e. ink text on an ink popover and an invisible pin number. **Scan BOTH directions after any palette flip.** Also easy to miss: `.cta` used `var(--text)` but only ever renders on the ink sign-off (needs `--on-dark`), and `.cmt-ava` was a white tint inside a popover that is now white. Verified end to end: comment mode opens a white card with ink text and cream inputs, and all three nav icons read.
- **Slide-deck visual grammar (established 2026-07-31, user-approved on homewiseslide's deck.gallery reskin — the LAYOUT grammar still applies to any future deck; the palette-is-per-project clause is superseded by the shared light system above):** editorial print, not boxed cards. Source patterns: deck.gallery's Geist*Studio Impact Report (stat rows + in-slide document chrome), Reece Evans Portfolio (metadata-columns cover + giant wordmark), Lefty (persistent brand footer). The PALETTE is per-project (homewiseslide wears Hearth; another deck would wear its own case study's tokens) — the grammar below is what transfers. (1) **Every slide is a framed page**: in-slide header row (section label left, static "<Project> · Case study" right via `.s-label::after`, full-width hairline under) + JS-injected folio footer (hairline, "A case study by Chang Mou" left, "NN / NN" right). No separate letterbox counter — the folio carries the number. (2) **Top/bottom composition**: title zone anchored top, artifact block pushed to the bottom via `.push { margin-top:auto; padding-top:44px }`, folio closing the frame. Never leave content floating in the top half with a void below — that was the exact "ugly" the reskin fixed. Full-height splits get `flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0` so they center between header and folio. (3) **No boxed cards**: stats are full-width hairline ROWS (oversized numeral ~112px left, annotation pinned to a right ~620px column); triads/stages are rule-topped COLUMNS with a tiny accent-color index (01/02/03), no background fills; hero figures (the money numbers) run 96–128px. (4) **Extreme type contrast**: ~13.5px/0.2em caps labels against 76px titles (54px compact variant for artifact-heavy slides; 46px in media slides' 2-col `.media-head` where the annotation sits right so the artifact keeps its height), ~96px ink statement slides, 170–300px display on the bookends. Single-weight rule applies as everywhere (400/500 only, explicit `font-weight` on every heading). (5) **Cover + sign-off are Reece-style dark bookends**: tiny metadata columns top (Role/Team/Timeline/Built with), giant `white-space:nowrap` wordmark bottom-left + small description bottom-right — SIZE THE PAIR TOGETHER (wordmark px + sub max-width; 300px/520px overflowed, 280px/430px fits), brand texture (dot grid) reserved for the two bookends only. (6) **One mid-deck ink statement slide** at the payoff beat (flat dark + quiet accent wash, no texture) for rhythm. (7) **One accent color** for chrome (solid progress bar; no decorative multi-color ramps). (8) Radius only on media frames (~8px) and pills — layout structure is drawn with hairlines, never rounded boxes.
- **Premium-research quick-win pass (2026-07-31, IN WORKTREE FOR USER JUDGMENT — not yet pushed; from a 5-agent research workflow over craftwork.design's AI category + Linear/Vercel/Stripe/Anthropic/Raycast benchmarks + a code audit + 2026 trend criticism):** style.css **v=221→v=222** across all 8 pages. (1) **Mono metadata tier — TRIED AND REJECTED (2026-08-01, user: "I do not like the monospace change"; do NOT re-propose a second typeface).** A `--font-mono` system-stack tier on chips/eyebrows/hero-meta shipped to the worktree for one day and was fully reverted — the site stays single-face Neue Montreal everywhere. - **Interaction six-pack (2026-08-01, per user "do rest" from the interaction-quality audit — a 3-agent workflow: site inventory vs Linear/Vercel/Raycast/Stripe/Resend micro-interaction benchmarks; verdict "pointer-and-scroll layer at or above the bar, gaps concentrated in app-like/async moments"):** style.css **v=223→v=224** across all 8 pages. (1) **`.chat-panel` opens/closes like the lightbox** — motion-gated longhand `transition-property: opacity, transform, display` + `transition-behavior: allow-discrete` + `@starting-style`, enter 240ms/exit 160ms on the tokens; reduced motion keeps the instant swap. (2) **Chat typing indicator is three staggered blinking dots** (`chatBlink` 1.2s, delays 0/0.2/0.4s, static under reduced motion) — **the widget JS line `typing.textContent = '...'` became `typing.innerHTML = '<span></span>×3'` in ALL 8 pages' pasted widget blocks, so the documented `dc03dc69…` md5 for the coffee/homewise/ticker/nova block is STALE** (those four are still byte-identical to each other; pollen is still the odd one out). (3) **Global `::selection`**: `rgba(240,240,240,0.92)` on `#0a0a0a` — the white-inversion form of chrome-stays-neutral; NEVER #0094FF. (4) **Carousel**: entering `.carousel-slide.active` fades in via motion-gated `@starting-style` (exit stays an instant cut under the fade), and `@media (hover:hover) .carousel-dot:hover { opacity: 0.6 }` finally drives the transition the dots declared.
