- **Animation audit pass (2026-08-02, per user, from Emil Kowalski's `improve-animations` skill installed via `npx skills add`; 4 read-only category agents produced 44 findings, each verified at its cited line before action; user picked findings 1-5):** style.css **v=224 to v=225** across all 8 pages **+ 404.html** (which the v=224 six-pack had missed - **remember 404.html when bumping; it is the 9th page**). (1) **The "Say hello" sweep was fighting its own stylesheet on all 8 pages** - `.footer-big-text .letter` declares `transition: opacity 0.3s ease` (style.css:1742) while the rAF loop writes that same property every frame, so the cursor spotlight rendered a ~150ms-lagged smear, and `textShadow` (NOT in the transition list) ran ahead of the glow it is paired with. Fixed with `letters.forEach(l => l.style.transition = 'none')` at the top of the sweep block - **the identical fix, with a comment explaining the identical reason, already existed 140 lines above on the scroll-CTA** (index.html:464); the sweep simply never got it. Verified: all 9 letters now change per frame and sit in the documented 0.37-0.53 idle band. **This edits the 8-page pasted sweep block, so its md5 changed again.** (2) **Reduced motion now suppresses smooth scrolling**: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { html { scroll-behavior: auto } }`, plus a `scrollBehavior()` helper in all 5 case studies because their 10 JS `window.scrollTo({behavior:'smooth'})` call sites **override the CSS property** and would otherwise still fling a motion-sensitive reader through thousands of pixels. (3) **The About photo-deck rAF is now IntersectionObserver-gated** (about.html) - it was the ONLY ungated loop on the site, running 60fps with 24 `getBoundingClientRect()` calls while the visitor read 4000px below. **GOTCHA worth keeping: the callback must read `es[es.length - 1]`, not `es[0]`** - a fast scroll batches several entries into one callback and `es[0]` is the OLDEST, which left the loop stopped while the deck was on screen (cost a real debugging cycle; the footer-sweep observer uses `es[0]` and works only because human scrolling rarely batches). (4) **Four `:hover` moves gated behind `@media (hover: hover)`** - `.logo span`, `.footer-links .arrow`, `.play-link .arrow`, and `.status-tag .arrow`; **the last one was a regression introduced by this session's own six-pack**, which added the nudge without the gate its `.project-card .project-arrow` twin has. (5) **`.project-card::before` glow 300ms bare `ease` to `--dur-base`/`--ease-out`**, so one hover no longer resolves on two clocks (border/shadow answered in 160ms while the glow took 300). **Findings deliberately NOT taken:** the decks' 620ms slide travel (an agent called it a HIGH "no animation on keyboard actions" violation - rejected, slide transitions ARE the medium in presentation software; the narrower real issue is the reveal stagger adding ~550ms and Home/End sweeping past blank slides), the "Say hello" hide-on-scroll-up (documented deliberate), the blanket 0.3s/0.2s-to-token sweep (documented as imperceptible, convert opportunistically), and `bounceDown` (orphan CSS, zero markup references). **Still open from this audit, ranked:** footer glow writes `height` from a raw scroll listener on 8 pages (index.html:697); the chat toggle's 8s conic border is an ungated paint animation on a fixed always-visible element; five underlines animate `width` where `scaleX` is pixel-identical; the mobile menu is the one overlay without the lightbox/chat `@starting-style` treatment; the hero entrance changes easing family mid-sequence (h1 on `--ease-out`, sub+pill on bare `ease`); `--ease-out` is re-typed as a literal 4x inside the file that declares it; and the chat reply arrives as a hard cut after the typing dots.
- **Tablet-band side rail (fixed 2026-07-26, UI-craft review P1):** `.about-vertical` / `.testimonials` / `.media-section` now carry `padding: 0 24px` **unconditionally** with `max-width: calc(var(--max-width) + 48px)`. The 24px rail used to live only inside the `@media (max-width:768px)` block, so from **769px to ~1007px** — exactly iPad portrait (810/820/834) — the viewport was too wide for the mobile padding and too narrow for the 960px cap to leave any margin, and every element (headings, bio, testimonials, media grid) computed `left:0 right:<viewport>`, i.e. text touching both screen edges. The `+48px` on max-width keeps the desktop content measure at exactly 960px, so ≥1008px renders byte-identically (verified: content 240→1200 and all 17 `.exp-date` cells still terminate at x=1200). **Any new full-width About-style section needs the same pair.** Companion fix: `@media (max-width:1135px) { .chat-toggle{bottom:96px} .chat-panel{bottom:156px} }` — below 1136px the content column reaches into the fixed chat button's lane (button owns the rightmost 88px; content right edge = viewport/2+480), so at `bottom:220px` it sat mid-screen and clipped the Experience date column. **96px, NOT 24px:** the footer links row occupies the bottom 32–54px and at 24px the button covered the "X" link completely (`elementFromPoint` returned the button — the link was unclickable); 96px clears it by ~42px and only overlaps the `pointer-events:none` watermark.
- **Max-width:** 960px for case study content sections (coffee/pollen/ticker). Home work grid and Play grid are 1344px for a wider workshop feel.
- **Case-study cover + hero spacing (redesigned 2026-06-20):** homewise/pollen/nova/coffee now open with a **contained, rounded cover** via the shared **`.case-cover`** class in style.css (`display:block; width:100%; max-width:var(--max-width)` = 960px; `margin:147px auto 0`; `border-radius:var(--radius)`). It sits **below the floating nav (not under it)** with a **symmetric 72px gap** above (nav→image) and below (image→heading): the `147px` top margin = nav bottom (~75px) + 72px, and 72px is `.project-hero`'s `padding-top` (image→heading). The cover `<img>` is still a direct `<body>` child ABOVE `.project-hero`, so the 72px hero `padding-top` still works (the cover supplies the top content; if a case study ever ships WITHOUT a cover, give the heading ~160px nav-clearance instead). **Mobile (≤768px):** `.case-cover` insets to `width:calc(100% - 48px)` so it lines up with the text — the cover is a body-level element *outside* `.project-content`/`.project-hero`, which get `padding:0 24px` on mobile, so `calc(100% - 48px)` reproduces that same 24px side inset (without it the cover bled edge-to-edge while the text was inset — the bug that prompted this). **TickerPulse is the lone exception** — still the original full-bleed cover (`width:100%`, edge-to-edge, sharp corners), as the unlinked page it was not converted. **History (2026-06-20):** covers were originally full-bleed with the nav floating OVER them; iterated constrain-to-960px → image-below-intro → swap-with-meta → settled on **image-at-top, below the nav, symmetric gaps**. The per-page inline cover styles were consolidated into `.case-cover` in the same pass. style.css bumped **v=171→v=172** across all 8 pages.
- **Nav wordmark + back affordance — considered and DECLINED 2026-08-01 (per user):** two ideas were weighed and dropped in one conversation: (a) a "← Chang Mou" / back-arrow logo on case-study pages (the decks' `.deck-brand` precedent does NOT transfer — decks have no other nav, so an arrow can only mean exit; in the full nav pill it conflates action with identity and is redundant with the labeled Work link), and (b) distinguishing the wordmark (period, monogram, weight) after the user asked if plain "Chang Mou" looks too generic — verdict: plain-name wordmarks are the premium personal-site convention and chrome stays deliberately neutral; the one sanctioned upgrade if ever revisited is the house-voice terminal period ("Chang Mou."), nothing bigger. User closed with "current is good, no need to change" — do not re-propose header/wordmark changes unprompted.
- **Nav link contrast (fixed 2026-07-26, UI-craft review P7):** `.nav-links` colour moved `--text-muted` (#888) → **`--text-soft` (#a0a0a0)**. At 13px over the animated hero gradient the muted grey measured **3.44:1 worst-case** across the irisFlow cycle (sampled 6 frames × 3 links from rendered pixels) — below WCAG AA's 4.5:1 for normal text. #a0a0a0 measures **4.67:1** there and ~7.6:1 on the dark pages. Deliberately not brighter (0.72 white would hit 7.13:1) so the inactive links stay clearly secondary to the white `.nav-active` link. Note this is shared chrome, so case-study navs get the same strictly-better contrast. **The audit's companion claim — that `.footer-links`/`.footer-copy` fail AA on the footer's amber band (3.40:1 / 2.99:1) — did NOT reproduce and was NOT acted on:** it reasoned from the CSS gradient stops (amber `rgba(255,190,35,0.64)` at 88%/100%) but the links actually render over the *red-orange* part of the gradient, sampled `rgb(160,62–67,38)` at every viewport tested (1440×700/900/1200/1600, 1024×768), giving **5.61–5.81:1 for links and 4.64–4.78:1 for the © line — both pass**. The proposed dark scrim on `.footer-bottom` would have dimmed the footer for nothing. **Lesson: compute contrast from rendered pixels, not from stacked CSS colour values.**
- **Nav:** Floating pill, 700px wide (narrowed from 760px 2026-06-20 per user — they wanted the removed scroll-condense's "after-shrink" 700px size kept static, with the lighter "before-shrink" glass opacity `rgba(18,18,20,0.72)`), centered. Links: Work | Play | About | Resume (**the "Get in touch" CTA was removed site-wide 2026-06-20** — redundant with the footer "Say hello"; the `.nav-cta` CSS rule is now unused/harmless). **"Specular Glass" treatment (2026-06-20, chosen via a 5-concept multi-agent pass + 3-judge panel):** the flat `rgba(20,20,20,0.85)` glass became a layered top-lit fill (`linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(255,255,255,0.025), transparent 55%)` over `rgba(18,18,20,0.72)`) + `backdrop-filter: blur(28px) saturate(1.4)` (the `saturate` is the 2026 glass tell) + a box-shadow stack (inset specular lip + inset hairline + lift + contact) + a bright-top gradient hairline ring via `nav::before` (mask-composite `exclude`/`xor`) + an opaque `@supports not (backdrop-filter)` fallback. **Active "you are here" tick:** `.nav-links a.nav-active::after { width:100% }` — a persistent 1px white underline under the current page (reuses the existing hover-underline `::after`; CSS-only, no JS, static per-page — the view-transition crossfade handles page-to-page). **Neutral on purpose** (white/grey/glass only — no NOVA blue, no hero gradient; chrome stays neutral). **Mobile:** nav underlines hidden in the full-screen overlay (`.nav-links a::after{display:none}`), and the glass `box-shadow` + `nav::before` ring are reset under `body.menu-open` so the open overlay is clean. **Skipped** (per the panel): a full-width-bar→pill scroll-morph (animates width/top on the fixed nav → the fixed-element-jump bug class) and a hover-travelling highlight pill (2026 cliché + competes with the hero magnetic tilt). **Scroll-condense was added then REMOVED (2026-06-20, per user "too annoying"):** a desktop, width-only "pill narrows 760→700px on scroll" effect (a `.is-scrolled` class + a per-page rAF-throttled scroll listener) shipped briefly (v=187 added, v=188 made width-only after the user said "only shrink the width, not the height") then was **fully reverted (v=189)** — the `@media (min-width:769px)` condense CSS, the `prefers-reduced-motion` override, and the 8 inline scroll scripts were all deleted. **Do NOT re-add scroll-condense unless asked.** The nav is static again (Specular Glass + active underline stay). **Gotcha (IMPORTANT):** the header glass targets the **bare `nav` selector**, which ALSO matches the case-study `<nav class="side-nav">` — the Specular-Glass `box-shadow` + `nav::before` ring leaked a glass box around the side-nav (shipped briefly in the v=186 push, **fixed v=188**: `.side-nav` now resets `box-shadow:none` + `.side-nav::before{display:none}`, on top of its existing background/blur/border/padding resets). **Any future addition to the bare `nav` rule MUST also be reset on `.side-nav`** (or scope the header to `nav:not(.side-nav)`). style.css **v=185→v=189** across all 8 pages.
- **Desktop fold compression — TRIED AND REVERTED 2026-07-26 (per user, "I do not like it").** The Mobbin landing benchmark's top move was to reclaim ~208px of hero padding on desktop (`.hero` 200/160 → 140/72, h1 margin-bottom 48→32, `.status-tag` margin-top 48→28, `.work` padding-top 80→56, gated `min-width:769px`) so a slice of work row one crops into the fold, the way Framer/Pentagram/Anthropic do. It measurably worked — card top 932→706, plate visible above the fold 0→193px @1440×900, mobile provably untouched — **and the user rejected the look.** The hero's full-height breathing room is the deliberate choice; do NOT re-propose compressing the hero to pull work above the fold. (The companion `.project-grid .project-card:nth-child(-n+2) { animation: none }` exemption was removed with it — it only existed because cards at the fold render at the scroll-reveal start keyframe. If any future change DOES place row one at the fold, that exemption becomes necessary again or the cards render faded.)
- **HERO REOPENED 2026-09-02 (per user "for hero section. do you have any thoughts to make it creative and less generic?" then "do 1 and 2"). This SUPERSEDES the "hero is declared done" and "hero copy frozen" notes ONLY in the two respects below; copy, palette and every cursor behaviour are unchanged.** (1) THE LIGHT-SOURCE GEOMETRY WAS BUILT, PUSHED LIVE (ea1feff), AND REVERTED THE SAME NIGHT (user: "revert the hero section. too ugly", clarified "i mean gradient"): the original six-pool mesh from style.css is the hero gradient again. THE HERO GRADIENT IS CLOSED FOR GOOD: a palette change (g3, 2026-08-04), an autonomous fluid field (2026-08-16) and now a geometry change have all been rejected; do not propose any further change to the gradient's colours, form, or motion. What was built, for the record only. Diagnosis given and accepted: everything distinctive about the hero was kinetic (Lightfall, drag, beam, magnetic glyphs) and invisible in the static first frame, which was a centered stack over a blurred four-hue mesh, i.e. the Lovable ramp. Fix = same six pools, same `--g2-*` variables, same irisFlow/blueFlow/g2Lift animations, same blur, NEW GEOMETRY, applied as an inline `.hero-orbs--g2 { background: ... }` override in index.html's <style> (home only; 404.html keeps the old mesh on purpose): a compact yellow sun (26%x22% ellipse at yellow-x / warm-y minus 4%), the red corona (72%x40% at warm), a tight pink bridge (96%x18% at pink-y minus 3%, kept above the horizon), the azure/cyan glints, then a `linear-gradient` HORIZON BAND at `--g2-horizon` (57% of the orbs box on desktop, 45.5% on phones, both MEASURED: the two headline lines split at exactly those percentages; each carries `+ var(--g2-lift)` so the horizon lags with the field on scroll) that fades to transparent before 100% so the mobile clipped-edge line can never return, over the original blue base ellipse. Line 1 sits on the warm, line 2 on the blue, the horizon between them (blurred ~4% of the box, so line 2's caps touch the transition by design). Layer order matters: the band is painted UNDER the glints so blueFlow still shows and ABOVE the pink so the ground stays clean. Verified 1440/375, scrolled and unscrolled, zero errors. (2) THE SITE'S ONE ITALIC: `.hero h1 .line:nth-child(2) .line-inner { font-style: italic }` (inline block), backed by `fonts/NeueMontreal-Italic.woff2` (27 KB, from the local NeueMontreal-Italic.otf, weight 400 italic, italicAngle -11) declared in style.css and preloaded on index.html. Single family, single weight, one flourish; line 2 measures 976px at 1440 (was 984 roman) and 309px at 375 (unchanged, fits the 327px column). CONSEQUENCES: `og-gen.html`/`images/og-image.jpg` show the OLD lockup (roman line 2, mesh field) and should be regenerated if the share card is meant to match; 404.html's hero still uses the mesh and roman type (left alone per the home-only instruction). Ideas offered and NOT taken this round, on record: an asymmetric left-aligned hero composition, a time-of-day warm/blue bias, and a real UI fragment (the Countersign gate) rising into the hero's lower third.
- **Hero light lags the scroll (added 2026-07-26):** as the hero scrolls away the whole light field drifts DOWN by up to **30%**, so it lingers behind the page instead of travelling with it — the gradient reads as an environment the page moves through rather than paint stuck to the box. **The technique is the important part:** irisFlow already owns `--g2-*-y`, `transform` (its scale-breathe) and `translate` (Lightfall's cursor drift), so there was no free channel. Instead of fighting it, a SEPARATE `@property --g2-lift` is folded into every blob's Y via `calc(var(--g2-warm-y) + var(--g2-lift))`, and a second animation `g2Lift` drives only that, on a `scroll(root block)` timeline over the first `100vh`. Two animations coexist on one element because they animate **different properties** — irisFlow keeps the document timeline (`animation-timeline: auto, scroll(root block)`). **At rest `--g2-lift` is 0%, so the hand-tuned warm/blue seam under headline line 1 is completely untouched** — the drift only exists while scrolling away. Gated to `min-width: 769px` + `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` inside `@supports (animation-timeline: scroll())`; mobile is excluded on purpose because its gradient is separately tuned for the hero/work seam (`.hero::after`). Shipped at 30% after 14% was judged too subliminal to earn its place; the single dial is the `--g2-lift` value in `@keyframes g2Lift`.
- **Hero:** Animated mesh gradient (@property CSS). **Light-sweep reveal + specular type + magnetic tilt — the scramble was REPLACED 2026-07-27 (user: "too boring… more 2026"; the decode-text trope read 2020-2023 and the heading went inert after load).** The entire width-pinning machinery (canvas measureText pools, per-span pinning, the old "Scramble width-pinned" fix) is GONE from index.html — no glyph substitution means that whole bug class (line breathing, 3-line snap, AIM pileups) is structurally impossible now. **(1) Entrance:** each glyph transitions from `opacity:0 / translateY(14px) / blur(9px)`, delayed by its **horizontal position** (`--d` = leftmost 0ms → rightmost 450ms; each glyph 700ms), so BOTH lines wake left-to-right as one wave (line 2 is centered so its first glyph starts ~55ms — position-based, not index-based). Spaces stay bare text nodes so wrapping behaves as plain text. h1 class sequence: `.sweep-in` (hidden state) → `.lit` (added after a **double rAF** — same commit-the-start-state bug class as the rule wipe) → `.settled` (+80ms after the wave). **(2) Specular presence — AMBIENT + cursor (reworked same day, 2026-07-27):** the sheen is driven by the **gradient's own motion**, not only the pointer — the first cut was cursor-only, which made the one distinctive effect an easter egg (a visitor who lands and scrolls never saw it). A ~16Hz rAF loop reads `--g2-warm-x` off `.hero-orbs` via `getComputedStyle` (irisFlow animates it as a registered `@property`, so the computed value IS the live light position — reading it can never drift out of sync with the keyframes) and lights glyphs near that x with a broad gaussian (σ=230px, ceiling `AMBIENT = 0.55`; **0.38 measured near-invisible** — brightness 1.04/alpha 0.13, the documented "too subliminal to earn its place" failure mode). The cursor layers on top via **screen blend** (`I = p + a(1−p)`, cursor peaks at 1, σx=90/σy=170) — `window.__heroSpecular(cx,cy,rect)` is still called from Script A's `apply()` but now only RECORDS the pointer (`mouseleave` passes `null`); **the ambient survives mouseleave** — that is the point. Per glyph: inline `filter: brightness(1+0.10·I)` + two-layer white `text-shadow`, writes skipped when ΔI<0.015; the 60ms tick is enough because `.settled` spans carry `transition: filter/text-shadow 0.45s` and CSS interpolates the gaps. The loop is **IntersectionObserver-gated to the hero** (same discipline as the footer dissolve; verified frozen off-screen / resumed on return). **Cascade trap:** the settled rule must be `.hero h1.lit.settled …` (4 classes, later in file) so its `filter: none` beats `.sweep-in.lit`'s `blur(0)` — blur(0)→brightness() is a MISMATCHED filter-function list and interpolates **discretely** (the glow fade-out snaps); `none` counts as the identity list and eases smoothly. **(3)** `.hero h1 .line` lost its `overflow:hidden` (pre-scramble relic; it would clip the entrance blur halo and the specular bloom — `padding-bottom: 6px` kept so layout doesn't shift). **(4) Retimed follow-ups:** `.hero-sub` delay 2.2s→**1.05s**, `.status-tag` 2.6s→**1.35s** (headline settles ~1.15s; the old delays were tuned to the scramble's longer runway). **(5) Mobile ≤768px:** `.hero h1 .line-inner span { filter: none !important }` (fade+rise only — blur is the costly channel on phones); the specular arms only under `(hover:hover) and (pointer:fine)` so touch never gets it (it DOES arm in a width-resized desktop browser — capability-gated, not width-gated, same as Lightfall; testing artifact, not a bug). Reduced motion: h1 jumps straight to `.lit.settled`, spans are never hidden (the hidden state lives inside `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`). **Verification notes:** the same-origin-iframe probe trick still works for catching the entrance mid-flight (entrance verified: first glyph 0.57→1 while mid/last still 0, blur 9→4.7→1.1→0); and **rAF-deferred effects (the specular event path) do not run while the browser pane isn't actively rendering frames** — a dispatch+sleep probe reads stale values and looks like a bug; wait on `requestAnimationFrame` chains instead (same trap family as the not-fronted IntersectionObserver). style.css **v=215→v=217**. **Subtitle carries the builds-in-code claim (added 2026-07-25, improvement-research P4):** "…and I prototype in real code, this site included." — "prototype in real code" is the literal phrase in Anthropic/Cursor/Perplexity design-role JDs (reviewers grep for it) and "this site included" makes the page itself the proof; keep both phrases intact if the subtitle is ever reworded (per the [[feedback_yc_audience]] judge-by-the-reader rule). **Cursor-follow gradient drift — "Lightfall" (added 2026-06-20, desktop only, chosen via a multi-agent design pass over 5 concepts):** on desktop the `.hero-orbs` mesh leans toward the cursor, folded into the SAME mousemove handler as the magnetic tilt (Script A in index.html) so the type + its light read as one motion. It drives the independent CSS **`translate`** property (NOT `transform`), so irisFlow's `transform` scale-breathe keeps running untouched; single rAF-throttled listener, `rect` recomputed per frame (scroll-safe), eased by an inline `transition: translate 0.55s cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1)`. Amplitude knobs in the script: `ORB_X` (horizontal multiplier on nx∈[-0.5,0.5]) + `ORB_Y` (vertical); shipped at **ORB_X=180 (±90px) / ORB_Y=26 (±13px)** after the user asked for it stronger (iterated ±26→±60→±90; ±26 was invisible under the `blur(56px)`). Vertical is kept small on purpose so the hand-tuned warm/blue seam under line 1 barely moves. At ±90px the script also widens the orbs' off-screen bleed to **`left/right: -12%`** (overriding the CSS -5%) so the larger drift never exposes a gradient edge — verified clean at both extremes. **Gated to desktop + motion-safe:** wired only under `(hover:hover) and (pointer:fine)` and NOT `prefers-reduced-motion`, so mobile/touch get nothing (insets + translate are never written below the gate, so the mobile `.hero::after` seam-fade is untouched) and reduced-motion users get a fully static hero — this also **fixed a pre-existing gap** where the magnetic tilt ran even under reduced-motion; now both are gated together. All in index.html's hero `<script>` — no style.css change, no version bump. **The old "No cursor glow" rule still holds:** Lightfall *moves* the existing light field, it never *paints* a glow disc/spotlight (the exact distinction the prior rejection turned on); do NOT add a painted cursor glow. Hire-state pill `.status-tag` below the subtitle reads "Open to new opportunities" with a `.status-dot` (green #4ade80, soft `pulse` animation that breathes opacity 0.7↔1.0 over 3s ease-in-out — deliberately *not* a scale+opacity blink; calm "alive" signal, not nervous). When Chang lands a role, swap the pill copy (e.g. "Open to interesting conversations") or remove `.status-tag` entirely. **Hero gradient variant system (2026-06-15):** the gradient lives on `.hero-orbs` as swappable variant classes. `.hero-orbs--g1` = the original animated violet/coral mesh (rgba(100,70,220)/coral/purple/peach, `meshFlow` 12s), **saved and restorable**. `.hero-orbs--g2` = the **ACTIVE** gradient (HTML: `<div class="hero-orbs hero-orbs--g2">`): a vivid full-neon iridescent spectrum from a reference image — **warm at the top** (yellow #FFD600 → red → pink) flowing into a strong **blue** (rgba(22,120,255)) behind the heading; **only the 4 bright hues, no teal/green/violet edge tones**. It's "alive" via @property-animated blob positions (`irisFlow` 15s) that drift + a scale-breathe (mirrors g1's motion). To revert to the old look, swap the class back to `hero-orbs--g1`. The `.hero-sub` and `.status-tag` were brightened for legibility over g2 (subtitle → rgba(255,255,255,0.82); pill → 0.92 white text + dark glass backdrop `rgba(10,10,12,0.45)` blur(8px) + 0.22 border). **Decision history — do not re-litigate:** iterated muted → vivid → "not that vivid" → full-neon WARM-top → and on **2026-06-15 pushed warmer still to WARM-DOMINANT** ("as warm as the footer": warm fills the body + behind the heading, blue eased back/down behind the subtitle). User **rejected the extreme** (warm covering ALL the text with blue only as a thin bottom-edge band — "too much") and the earlier over-soft/washed versions. **Motion was reworked livelier the same day** (`irisFlow` now 13s): blob travel widened to ~±12% and the **yellow now animates too** (it was pinned), because the first pass "did not feel alive". **Then reverted to BALANCED and refined (current shipped state, 2026-06-15):** the warm-to-blue cut now lands right **under the FIRST heading line** — "AI-first product designer." glows on warm, while "Building what's next." + the subtitle + pill sit crisp on blue (the `irisFlow` centers that produce this: warm-y ~25, pink-y ~48, blue-y ~78; the two headline lines split at ~box-Y 57%). A subtle heading text-shadow is the lever if the first line ever needs more crispness over the warm. **Mobile (≤768px) fixes (2026-06-15):** the short mobile hero compresses the g2 gradient and lands the blue blob low, so it met the dark work section with a visible **cut / dividing line**. Fixed two ways: (a) `.hero-orbs { bottom: 8% }` (was `-12%`, which shoved the blue to the very bottom edge where `overflow:hidden` clipped a ~4%-opacity sliver into a faint line) pulls the blue up — also makes it more visible; **and** (b) a `.hero::after` overlay (`linear-gradient(to bottom, transparent, #0a0a0a 78%)`, `height: 42%`, `z-index: 1` — above orbs/0, below text/2) fades the hero's bottom to the **EXACT page bg** so the gradient dissolves seamlessly into the work section with **no clipped residual**. (A `mask-image` on the orbs was tried first and abandoned: a mask leaves a clipped-residual line because it makes the orbs transparent rather than painting the bg; the overlay paints `#0a0a0a` so the hero edge and work section are the same color — seamless.) Both mobile-only; **desktop is untouched** (`::after` is `none`, orbs use base `bottom:20%`). Also **mobile `.hero h1` is `clamp(26px, 8.5vw, 32px)`** (was a flat `32px`): keeps ~32px on standard phones (≥375px) but scales down on narrow screens (30.6px@360, 27.2px@320) so the first line "AI-first product designer." keeps its period on **one line** instead of orphaning it (each char is an inline-block, so the line can break right before the "."). **Lovable-resemblance challenge — RESOLVED: KEEP g2 (2026-08-04, user's call; do NOT re-propose changing the gradient).** An outside reader told Chang the site looks like lovable.dev/home. Verified side-by-side: the resemblance is real and specific — g2's yellow→red→pink→blue ramp is Lovable's brand gradient stop-for-stop (their hero runs the same four hues inverted), and the footer glow is even closer; layout is NOT the overlap (dark + centered headline is half the portfolio world). A two-hue amber/blue "g3" variant was built in the worktree (hero `.hero-orbs--g3` reusing g2's `--g2-*` @property/irisFlow machinery so specular/Lightfall/lag worked untouched; red→rgba(255,160,30,.85), pink-slot→deep amber rgba(255,120,45,.72) — a sky-blue bridge there was tried first and MOVED THE SEAM ABOVE LINE 1, the pink slot must stay warm-family; footer pink→sky rgba(96,170,255,.58), red→amber-orange rgba(255,150,40,.60)), verified desktop+mobile, then **fully reverted per user ("I do not want to change")**. The user's standing defense, factually sound: Instagram's 2016 brand gradient (yellow→orange→magenta→purple/blue) is the same family and closer to g2 than Lovable's own ramp — the palette is a decade-old iridescent-spectrum trend nobody owns. The g3 recipe above is the recovery path if this is ever revisited; og-image + favicon also carry the ramp and would need to follow. **The accepted differentiation move instead — "blueFlow" electric-blue sublayers (2026-08-04, per user "make the blue more AI and more dynamic", shipped v=227):** the palette is untouched; two pools now live INSIDE the blue region — an azure core `rgba(10,132,255,.50)` (ellipse 58%×34%) and a cyan glint `rgba(96,214,255,.32)` (40%×24%), layered between the pink pool and the broad blue base — driven by their own `blueFlow` animation (own `@property` vars `--g2-azure-x/y`, `--g2-cyan-x/y`) at **9s against irisFlow's 13s, so the composed field only re-aligns every ~39s** and the blue reads as weather rather than a loop; Lovable's gradient is a static wash, so the motion IS the differentiation. Both pools are position-clamped to **y 66–90%** so the warm top and the line-1 seam never see them, and both fold in `--g2-lift` so the scroll lag moves the whole field as one. **The desktop `@supports` lag block is now THREE index-aligned longhand lists (`irisFlow, blueFlow, g2Lift`) — any future animation added to the hero orbs must extend every list in step or the shorthand-reset bug class returns.** Dials: cyan alpha 0.32→0.45 for an overt glint, 9s→7s for restless. **Footer deliberately unchanged** (one signature motion per surface — the footer's is the pixel dissolve; the reflection reading calmer than its source is intentional physics). Verified: three frames seconds apart show the blue mass reorganizing while warm/pink hold identical; mobile seam-fade clean; specular untouched (reads `--g2-warm-x` only). **Open thread — hero vividness (diagnosed, NOT applied):** the hero looks less vivid than the footer because it's blurred radial blobs (`filter: blur(56px)`) while the footer is a crisp un-blurred *linear* gradient — same palette, but the blur averages the hero's colors down. The lever is **reducing the hero blur (~28–32px punches it up to match the footer)**; user was mid-decision on the level when the session paused.

(3) **Ignition beam** — one scripted full-intensity specular pass L→R across the settled headline (starts 180ms after `.settled`, 750ms, σ=150px, travel minX−260→maxX+260, easeInOutSine), computed INSIDE the same intensity pipeline as ambient+cursor (screen-blended) so it can never drift from hover behavior; **phase derives from an absolute `performance.now()` start, so a hidden tab or early scroll-away skips the beam rather than replaying it stale**; frames run unthrottled only while the beam is live. (4) **Wake** — per-glyph inline `transition-duration`: **0.12s when brightening, 1.1s when dimming**, so the beam and any cursor movement paint a slowly-decaying trail; the stylesheet's 0.45s on `.lit.settled` is now only the pre-first-write default. (5) **Magnetic glyphs** — per-glyph `translate` toward the cursor (PULL 3.2px, σ=110px, lerp 0.18/frame, runs every rAF frame — pure math + transform writes, no style reads; the intensity pass keeps its 60ms tick). Forces read the **CACHED base centers** so displacement never feeds back into itself; **the force ramps down inside 24px of the pointer** (direction-singularity guard — without it the glyph under the cursor jitters); `transform` is deliberately NOT in the `.settled` transition list so these writes land instantly (a CSS transition there would smear the spring). **Block tilt halved 6°→3°** so the two position responses don't fight. (6) Sub+pill `fadeUp` easing `ease` → `var(--ease-out)` — closes the "easing family changes mid-sequence" open item from the animation audit. Verified live: beam plateau ~720ms then slow decay; ambient steady state EXACTLY 1.055 brightness (the 0.55 ceiling); magnetic ±2.9px, directionally correct on both sides of the cursor, zero residual transforms after mouseleave; warm/blue seam still splits between the two lines at 100.8px; mobile 375 unchanged (fade+rise only, no filters/transforms/specular, 31.9px, both lines single-line, zero overflow). **New probe gotcha: the same-origin-iframe entrance probe freezes mid-decay when the iframe stops compositing** — a steady 1.076 brightness read was the FROZEN IFRAME, not an engine bug (the main page settles at exactly 1.055); use iframe probes for entrance choreography only, main-page probes for steady states. Trust thesis is substantiated one scroll down (Homewise HITL gates, NOVA, Countersign, Luna). og-gen.html lockup updated + **og-image regenerated (`?v=4→?v=5` on all 10 pages that carry it, incl. both decks)**. (2) **Drag-the-light** — mousedown on the hero grips the field: Lightfall amplification ×1.8 X / ×2.5 Y (X extreme 0.6·180·1.8=194px stays inside the −12% bleed ≈207px; **the seam is ALLOWED to move during an active drag — displacement is the point, release eases home**), nx/ny clamped ±0.6, grip ease 0.25s / release 0.9s / restored 0.55s via timeout, window-level mousemove keeps a held grip tracking outside the hero, mouseleave early-returns while dragging (mouseup owns the reset; released-outside runs the reset itself), links opt out via `closest('a')`, preventDefault kills text selection, `__heroDrag` widens the engine's cursor pool σ90→140, cursor grab/grabbing via a motion+pointer-gated media block in style.css. Verified numerically: drag translate = exactly nx×324, release settles to nx×180 (same pointer), transitions swap 0.25s→0.9s→0.55s. (3) **Pill ignition** — engine adds `.dot-ignite` to `.status-dot` when the beam phase passes 1, **gated on `ignSeen` so a visitor who scrolled away and missed the beam gets no orphan pulse**; `dotIgnite 0.9s` compound animation restates `pulse` (3s, 0.9s delay) so the breathe resumes. (4) **Both review-workflow fixes applied** (14-agent adversarial pass, 3 confirmed / 8 refuted): `cache()` subtracts the engine's own `mx/my` so a resize mid-hover can't bake magnetic displacement into the base centers, and the hero IO's deactivate branch **clears `cur`** (Firefox fires no boundary events when content scrolls under a stationary pointer — a phantom cursor would keep glyphs bent+lit on return). I am designer"). The failure mode: an actor-less noun-phrase headline on a personal site can parse as an appositive describing the person — the judge's first-person version ("I design AI products people trust.") avoided exactly this and was dropped only for layout, but the lesson generalizes: **never ship a line-1 fragment whose subject could be misread as Chang himself, and treat the owner's read as the veto no panel score outranks.** Line 1 is back to "AI-first product designer." permanently. **Minutes later the user killed line 2 as well ("you can not say 'i build what i design' cuz i did not take care of the backend") — an HONESTY veto, consistent with the site's documented prototype-not-product framing: Chang builds front-end prototypes in real code, the dev teams build the backends, so "I build what I design" overclaims full-stack ownership and invites the exact interview probe the Homewise copy is engineered to survive. The judge-panel scores above stand as record only — the hero copy question is CLOSED unless the user reopens it, and any future copy proposal must clear BOTH vetoes: no actor-less fragments that can describe Chang, no claims wider than front-end prototyping. **Presence + release batch, same day (2026-08-13, per user "ok" — the LAST hero additions; two research agents ran first and both verdicts are recorded here so they are never re-derived): (a) craftwork.design sweep (120 curated entries, 14 sites visited live): the hero is ALREADY past the curated bar on motion — nothing in the set approaches the per-glyph light physics; the gap is PRESENCE and PROOF, and the most recurrent missing pattern is a live local clock. (b) YC-tier scroll research (13 homepages fetched raw, markers grepped, Linear's Hero/useScrollMask source read): top-tier heroes are LOAD-TIME moments — none put scroll-triggered animation in the hero itself (scroll budget goes below the fold: one-shot viewport reveals, a nav scrolled-state; full GSAP choreography only on agency builds, and even Anthropic's hero h1 is static), and ZERO of the 12 fetched sites ship the native `animation-timeline`/`view()`/`scroll()` API this site already uses in four places — mechanically ahead of the tier. Built from those verdicts: (1) `.hero-presence` — "San Francisco · 4:32 PM" line under the pill (13px, white 0.55, fadeUp 1.5s closing the entrance sequence; `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `timeZone: 'America/Los_Angeles'`, 10s tick, runs on ALL devices outside the pointer/motion gate since it is text-only; JS-fails leaves the static "San Francisco" markup text, still true). (2) Light release on exit (the Linear useScrollMask analog — the tier's ONE verified in-hero scroll-linked effect): `rel = max(0, 1 − scrollY/(heroH·0.7))` computed per frame in the engine, multiplies the final specular intensity AND the magnetic force k, so on scroll-out the type hands its light back to the field and the grip relaxes, completing just before the gradient's own `--g2-lift` lag maxes at 100vh. The writes are scrubbed; the RENDERING is eased by the wake's asymmetric durations — draining rides the 1.1s DECAY (reads as light draining, not a hard scrub), returning rides the 0.12s RISE. Verified numerically: maxB 1.054 at top, 1.027 at 35% scroll (theory 1.028), 1.000 past 70%, 1.055 on return; presence format-checked and ticking. style.css v=232→v=233 across all 9 pages. Verified: 1.055/1.004 top → 0.952/0.925 at 35% scroll (theory 0.953/0.925) → 0.85 flat past 70% → full restore. style.css v=233→v=234 across all 9 pages (presence CSS removal). The user then asked for BOLD hero directions ("you run so many agents and these are the changes you can only suggest?") — a bold-directions research workflow (3 lenses + feasibility architect + judge) produced a 4-item menu; **the user picked #1: "The Living Gradient" (2026-08-13, per user "let us do #1"), and a WORKING PROTOTYPE now lives in `hero-fluid.js` (worktree, committed, NOT pushed).** All three research lenses independently ranked it first; the elite-portfolio lens's key finding: bold in your OWN medium reads as taste, imported spectacle reads as applying for the wrong job — and every verified hired-at-target-tier portfolio (rauno.me, emilkowal.ski, paco.me, benji.org, jhey.dev) runs a RESTRAINED text hero, so bold is a choice, not a requirement. **Architecture (the load-bearing decisions): (1) the WebGL2 shader reproduces the six g2 pools PROCEDURALLY, transcribed 1:1 from `.hero-orbs--g2` (geometry/ramps/alphas as shader constants — if that CSS block ever changes, the render shader in hero-fluid.js MUST follow; same mirror rule as api/chat.js↔matchData), with per-frame pool positions read LIVE off the hidden-but-still-animating orbs element (`.hero--gl .hero-orbs { opacity: 0 }` — opacity does not stop @property animations), so composition drift can never desync from the CSS and the glyph specular (which reads `--g2-warm-x` off the same element) stays coupled for free. (2) DYE IS NEVER ADVECTED — a Navier-Stokes velocity sim (advect → splat → vorticity confinement ε14 → 18 Jacobi pressure iters → gradient subtract, 192×~108 half-float grid) advects a DISPLACEMENT field that offsets the render's UV lookup (dissipation 0.962/frame): stirring distorts the light, the composition is the attractor it always relaxes back to (verified: displacement 0.010→0.003 over 2.5s). Free dye advection was explicitly rejected by the feasibility pass — yellow+blue mixes to mud and destroys the seam. (3) Integration surface: reads `window.__heroDrag` (drag = wider/stronger splats), zero changes to Script A/type engine; canvas matches the ORBS box (incl. Script A's -12% bleed) at half-res backing with CSS `blur(26px)` standing in for the orbs' blur(32px) (the blur ALSO edge-feathers the canvas exactly like the CSS layer's clip). (4) Fallback chain — mobile/touch/reduced-motion/no-WebGL2/no-EXT_color_buffer_float/failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat/context-lost/JS-throw/`?gl=0` — ALL land on the untouched CSS hero; the class flips only after the first frame renders (no flash). (5) Ambient seeded weather: faint splats every ~2-3s from a sessionStorage-seeded RNG (the generative fold-in — per-visit unique phase). Verified first run: 120fps on a 120Hz display, physics decay correct, composition/seam reads as the same identity vs `?gl=0`. **The "too dark" first render (user-flagged) was a BUG, not a tuning gap — fixed same day: the render shader's back-to-front mix chain produces PREMULTIPLIED color by construction, and the output multiplied by alpha AGAIN, darkening the whole field by its own alpha (worst at low-alpha corners). Output is `vec4(rgb, a)`, never `vec4(rgb*a, a)`.
