- **Footer:** Big "Say hello" watermark (scroll reveal). **Footer structure (restructured 2026-06-15):** the giant "Say hello" watermark lives in its **own `.say-hello` section** (`padding-top: 160px`, holds `.footer-big`), **NOT inside `<footer>`** — `<footer>` now holds only the `.footer-bottom` links bar. Both, plus `.footer-glow`, are wrapped in a **`.page-end` container** (`position: relative; overflow: hidden`) that **owns the glow** so it spans behind BOTH "Say hello" and the links bar and is clipped exactly at the page bottom. **Why two problems forced this:** (1) when "Say hello" + the links bar shared `<footer>` (links pulled up via `.footer-bottom { margin-top: -80px }`), the watermark **overlapped the LinkedIn link at narrow desktop widths** (the `-80px` was removed); (2) a first fix that put the glow only inside `.say-hello` left a **black gap at the page bottom** (glow stopped ~22px short of the edge) — the `.page-end` wrapper clips the glow at the bottom edge instead, so warm reaches the bottom with no gap. **JS wiring:** the glow-sizing scroll effect targets `.page-end` (`footerSection`); the letter-spotlight hover targets `.say-hello` (`footerEl`), **gated to the word's own bounding box** (first/last letter `getBoundingClientRect` + a 16px pad, added 2026-06-17): the handler only measured *horizontal* distance to each letter and listened on the whole `.say-hello`, so a cursor anywhere in the section's 160px top padding lit letters from far above the text while showing no hand cursor (the `<a>` is `pointer-events:none`, only the glyphs are `auto`) — the gate now resets the letters whenever the cursor leaves the word's box, so the lit area matches the hand-cursor area; applied to 5 pages (index/coffee/homewise/ticker AND pollen/nova — the original "all 4 pages" record here was wrong), while **about/play silently kept the oldest UNGATED variant** (no bbox gate, lit from the padding band) until the 2026-07-28 sweep rollout below. The "Say hello" reveal is the same IntersectionObserver + hide-on-scroll-up pattern on ALL pages as of 2026-07-27 (index's `.footer-big-text` reveal was rewired to the exact coffee/homewise/ticker IO pattern, variable named `footerVisible` to avoid colliding with other index globals). **The index "Like / what / you / see?" scroll-CTA was deleted 2026-07-27 and RESTORED 2026-07-28 at the user's explicit request — the "do NOT re-add" note is void, the section stays.** The 2026-currency audit had called it a 2019-2021 awwwards ghost-words trope with engagement-bait copy (flagged by two lenses); the user overruled that, so treat the section as a settled decision and do not re-propose removing it. Restored verbatim from the pre-deletion commit: `.scroll-cta`/`.scroll-word` CSS (0.15 opacity when `.visible`, `clamp(48px,8vw,80px)`, `min-height:40vh`), the mobile `display:none` block, the markup, and the per-word scroll JS whose trigger line is `vh*0.92 - i*(vh*0.05)` (each word keyed to its OWN viewport position, so the left-to-right stagger works at any viewport height — verified 1000→1100→1110). **One deliberate difference from the pre-deletion version: the words no longer gate the "Say hello" reveal.** The old JS toggled the watermark on `allVisible && inView`, which made index the one page with a special-case footer reveal; the restore keeps the standard IO pattern and only re-adds the word reveal, so the two are decoupled. **`.work` bottom padding went back 200px → 80px** in the same change: the 200px was added *because* the CTA was removed, and with the section back the two together would double the gap. Mobile is untouched either way (`.work` has a ≤768px `padding-bottom:80px` override and the CTA is `display:none` there). If the section is ever removed again, `.work` must go back to 200px or the last card row lands 80px off the footer band. style.css **v=219→v=220** across all 8 pages. **Page-end light pass (2026-07-28, per user, INDEX-ONLY, JS-only — no style.css change/bump):** (1) the CTA's one-shot `.visible` toggle is now the **reduced-motion/mobile fallback only**; on desktop each word's opacity is **scrubbed continuously** from its own viewport position (same `vh*0.92 − i*5vh` trigger geometry so the stagger holds; easeOutCubic over `0.16vh` of travel; inline `transition:'none'` because the stylesheet's 0.3s transition would smear per-frame writes) and is **fully reversible** — scroll back down and the words un-light. As `.page-end` enters, a `glow` factor (the same progress the footer-glow height uses) lifts the words past their 0.15 rest toward **0.4** and adds a **blue** text-shadow `rgba(30,120,255, glow*0.5)` — blue on purpose: the glow's nearest (top) stop is blue, so the words read as lit by the approaching field, keeping the "move the light that is already there, never paint a new one" rule. (2) **INDEX's "Say hello" letter spotlight became ambient sweep + cursor** (the headline-specular grammar: hover-only was an easter egg): a gaussian pool (`BASE 0.35`, `AMB 0.28`, `σ=220px` — idle range ~0.37–0.53, dimmed twice from the shipped-first 0.5/0.6 per user "too bright"; the deliberate contrast is idle peak ≈ HALF the cursor's 1.0 so hovering is a real step up. Floor warning: much below BASE 0.3 the word stops reading as a clickable mailto and becomes pure texture) drifts across the word on a 7s sine; the cursor layers on top via screen blend (`σ=150px`, mousemove only RECORDS the pointer, gated to the word's bbox) and the ambient survives mouseleave; one rAF loop paints, IntersectionObserver-gated to `.say-hello`, `sweepStart` bails while `.footer-big-text` is `display:none` (mobile), and reduced-motion gets a fully static word (no sweep, no spotlight — same gating as Lightfall/tilt). **Sweep rolled out to ALL 8 pages 2026-07-28 (per user):** the block is byte-identical everywhere (md5 `f09a7d71edc3d4dfd1bafe3728928902`, measured from the `// Letter light on "Say hello"` comment through `.observe(footerEl);` + its closing `}`). It is a per-page pasted block exactly like the chat widget, so **any tuning change must be re-copied to all 8 pages** (audit: `for f in *.html; do echo "$f $(grep -c sweepFrame $f)"; done` — every page ≥1). The rollout retired THREE coexisting variants: the gated hover spotlight (coffee/homewise/ticker, 1365 chars; pollen/nova, 679 chars) and about/play's 658-char ungated original — which still carried the light-from-the-padding-band bug the 2026-06-17 gate was built to fix; that fix had never reached those two pages. Dials: `BASE/AMB/SIG_A/PERIOD` consts and the CTA's `0.25` glow-boost/`0.5` shadow-alpha literals. **Probe gotcha (cost real debugging time):** the root has `scroll-behavior: smooth`, so a probe's plain `scrollTo()` ANIMATES — and while the browser pane isn't rendering frames the animation freezes mid-flight, which reads as "the effect is broken" and can complete LATER during an unrelated screenshot, teleporting the page. Probes must use `scrollTo({top, behavior:'instant'})` (same trap family as rAF-not-while-hidden). **Mobile (≤768px) — carries the full glow + dissolve as of 2026-06-18** (it used to hide the whole footer block — `.say-hello`/`.footer-big`/`.footer-glow`/`.px-dissolve` all `display:none` — leaving a bare links row the user found "too simple"; they wanted desktop-level richness): now `.footer-glow` + `.px-dissolve` are **shown**, `.say-hello` stays as the glow band (`padding-top: 180px` on mobile — bumped from 130px 2026-06-18 to give the dissolve room to breathe without the empty band becoming a void) but **only the giant text (`.footer-big-text`) and the © line (`.footer-copy`) are hidden** — `.footer-big` stays in flow at 0 height so it still serves as the dissolve's `data-stop` marker (no JS change). The links bar centers over the glow. **The dissolve animates on mobile too** (same as desktop). **Decision history (2026-06-18, do NOT re-introduce static):** a static-on-mobile variant was tried and reverted the same day — the engine's `reduce` flag briefly OR'd in `matchMedia('(max-width: 768px)')` to skip the rAF loop, but we landed on **animated everywhere** for one consistent footer signature + the craft moment on mobile (where much portfolio viewing happens). The battery/accessibility worry is already covered: the dissolve is **IntersectionObserver-gated** (runs only while the footer is on-screen) and **still falls back to a static frame under `prefers-reduced-motion`**, so motion/battery-sensitive users opt out at the OS level without flattening it for everyone. The © line "© 2026 Chang Mou · Built with Claude Code" shows on **desktop only** (user dropped it from mobile 2026-06-18). All 8 pages share the identical `.page-end` block. Left line: **"© 2026 Chang Mou · Designed in the fog" as of 2026-09-02, and it has now survived a round trip: the caramel was re-attached to it ("Designed in the fog with salted caramel", per user) and then removed again within the hour (per user "change the footer to Designed in the fog"). So the flavour is retired from the footer for good. Keep the line lowercase after the middot and without a terminal period.** Note the mid-session claim that the caramel joke "could not be salvaged" once "Built with Claude Code" moved to the hero was WRONG on its own terms — "designed in the fog with ___" is the same place-then-flavour category swap and the joke did travel — but the user removed it anyway, so the reasoning is moot and the short line stands. Full history: "© 2026 Chang Mou · Designed in the fog" as of 2026-09-02 (per user "change the footer tag to something else", asked in the same breath as adding the hero maker's mark; applied to all 10 pages).** This restores the site's ORIGINAL line, which lived in the hero until 2026-05-14 and then in the footer until the Claude Code credit replaced it. **THE REASONING MATTERS, because it explains why the caramel could not simply be salvaged: "salted caramel" was funny only as the second item of "Built with Claude Code and ___" — a list that changes category halfway through. Moving "Built with Claude Code" up to the hero removes the joke's setup, and the flavour word alone is not a joke, just a food word.** So the footer needed genuinely different copy rather than a trim. "Designed in the fog" also complements rather than repeats the new hero mark: the hero says HOW it was made, the footer says WHERE. Alternatives offered and not taken: keeping a caramel variant ("Runs on salted caramel", which reactivates the "runs on coffee" cliché the original deliberately avoided) and a bare "Salted caramel". Historical: the line was "© 2026 Chang Mou · Built with Claude Code and salted caramel" as of 2026-09-02 (per user, applied to all 10 pages that carry the footer line; the flavour is the user's own addition. It shipped capitalised as "Salted Caramel" for one deploy and was LOWERCASED with a terminal period the same hour after the user asked whether it read as a coffee or an ice cream: capitalised it reads as a product or brand name, lowercase it reads as the flavour, which is the joke. Keep it lowercase and WITHOUT a terminal period (the period shipped for one preview round and the user removed it: the line is a credit, and the footer's other text carries none), and do NOT expand it to "latte" or "ice cream" - the deadpan two words are the point) — before that "© 2026 Chang Mou · Built with Claude Code" (was "Designed in the fog" — moved here from the hero in 2026-05-14 so the hero could carry the hire-state signal). Right side: LinkedIn / Email / Resume. **Footer text brightened 2026-06-15** (`.footer-copy` → rgba(255,255,255,0.8), `.footer-links a` → 0.92, both + `text-shadow: 0 1px 4px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)`) so it stays legible over the vivid glow. **Footer glow is the hero's REFLECTION (2026-06-15):** `.footer-glow` is now a smooth **vertical linear gradient** (NOT radial blobs — radials showed a visible blob shape/edge the user rejected) that mirrors g2 — **blue at the inside (top) → pink → red → warm/amber at the bottom**, the reverse of the hero's warm-top→blue, so warm bookends the page top & bottom and blue faces inward (the "mirror" idea from the reference image). It's shapeless + vivid (~0.6–0.68 alpha). The **top** fades from `transparent` (0%) to blend into the dark page above; the **bottom ends on amber** (`rgba(255,190,35,0.64)` at both 88% AND 100%), **NOT transparent** — the page ends at the bottom edge, so a transparent bottom stop just revealed the black background. That was the **"black gap below the footer" (fixed 2026-06-15)**; keep the bottom stop warm. Shared CSS → applies to the footer on **every page**.
- **Lit plates — the card light follows the cursor (added 2026-07-26):** the two-radial plate light scene was a *baked* inline gradient; it is now a real light source. Four cards carry `.project-img--lit` + three inline vars (`--plate-light` / `--plate-deep` / `--plate-base`) and the gradient moved into style.css, where both pools translate together by `--plate-dx/--plate-dy` so the whole field **leans** toward the pointer. The screenshot's contact shadow casts the opposite way via `--plate-sx`. This is the **Lightfall grammar one level down** — move the light that is already there, never paint a new one — so it does NOT violate the standing "no cursor glow disc" rule. **Three things that will bite a future edit:** (1) **the ellipse size must stay pinned at `70% 70%`** — that is exactly what the old `radial-gradient(ellipse at 30% 30%, …)` resolved to via farthest-corner on a 16:9 plate, so the at-rest render is byte-identical (verified: computed value is `70% 70% at 30% 30%`); drop the pin and a moving origin would also *resize* the pool, since farthest-corner is measured from the origin. (2) **`--plate-sx` must NOT be in `.project-card`'s transition list** — it feeds the img's `filter`, and `.project-img img` already carries `filter 0.5s ease`, so easing the property *and* the filter it produces compounds into a ~900ms chase that never lands (measured: shadow settled at 8.3px instead of 10px, and −1.4px instead of 0 after mouseleave). `--plate-dx/dy` DO get the transition (they drive `background-image`, which has no transition of its own). (3) **Only 4 of 8 cards are lit, on purpose** — NOVA + Mercor keep flat `#FFFFFF` (their PNGs carry a baked shadow that halos against anything darker, the documented exception), and StoryBloom + New Craft Society are full-bleed covers with no visible plate. Amplitude is one dial, `PLATE_AMP` in index.html, shipped at **12%**: measured against the composited gradient, 12% swings the plate centre ~20 RGB units, 18% only reaches 25.5, and by 24% the pools slide off-plate so the *edges* move less, not more. Driven by the existing per-card mousemove listener (the one that feeds the `::before` `--x/--y` glow) — no second listener — and gated to `(hover:hover) and (pointer:fine)` + not `prefers-reduced-motion`, matching how Lightfall is gated; when off, the vars sit at their `@property` `initial-value: 0%` and the plates render exactly at rest. style.css **v=209→v=210** (note: the older "v=194" line further down this file was already stale).
- **Hover glow takes the card's own hue (added 2026-07-26):** the `.project-card:hover` `box-shadow` was a hardcoded neutral white haze on all 8 cards; it now spills the card's OWN light past the border, so the plate's lit scene continues past its edge instead of stopping at it. Each card's colour identity moved onto the `<a class="project-card">` itself (`--plate-light` / `--plate-deep` / `--plate-base`), and `.project-img--lit` reads them by inheritance — **one source of truth per card**; do not push them back down onto the `.project-img` div, or the glow loses its input (custom properties inherit down, not up). **The hue had to be REBUILT, not tinted:** every plate hue is a pale pastel, and at these alphas over `#0a0a0a` a pastel and pure white land within ~2 RGB units — the tint would be mathematically present and visually absent. So the glow is `oklch(from var(--plate-light) 0.78 min(calc(c * 5), 0.18) h / 0.10)` — hue only, with lightness and chroma forced. **Two properties of that expression are load-bearing:** (a) `#FFFFFF` has chroma 0, so `calc(c * 5)` stays 0 and **NOVA + Mercor glow neutral with no special-casing** — exactly right for the two plates that must stay flat white; (b) it is `min()`, NOT `clamp()` — a clamp with a floor would drag those chroma-0 whites up to the floor and put a colour cast on precisely the cards that must not have one. The cap exists because unclamped `c * 5` ran 0.105 (StoryBloom) to 0.249 (New Craft Society), so one card glowed 2.4× more vividly than another for no reason a viewer could read; capped, the spread is 0.105–0.18. Gated by `@supports (color: oklch(from white l c h))` inside the existing `@media (hover: hover)`, so browsers without relative colour syntax keep the old white glow. style.css **v=210→v=212**.
- **Thumbnail parallax inside the plate (added 2026-07-26):** the floating screenshot drifts ±8px against its plate as the card crosses the viewport, so the screenshot-on-a-lit-plate material gains real depth. **Two traps, both load-bearing:** (1) **the timeline must be NAMED, not `view()`.** `view()` binds to the nearest ancestor *scroll container*, and both `.project-card` and `.project-img` are `overflow: hidden` — which makes them scroll containers — so an anonymous `view()` on the `<img>` binds to the plate, which never scrolls, and sits dead at progress 0. Fix: `view-timeline-name: --card-view` on `.project-grid .project-card` (whose own nearest scrollport IS the viewport), referenced from the img as `animation-timeline: --card-view`; name lookup walks up the ancestor chain. Same family as the documented body-`overflow-x` trap — watch what silently became a scroll container. (2) **it drives `translate`, NOT `transform`** — hover owns `transform: scale(1.04) translateY(-3px)` on that same element and `.project-img img` transitions `transform` at 0.8s, so a shared property would rubber-band the parallax through the hover transition. Verified coexisting: `translate: -1.71px` alongside `transform: matrix(1.04,…,-3.12)`. Exactly the Lightfall-vs-irisFlow split. **Excluded from the two full-bleed covers** via `.project-img--cover` on StoryBloom + New Craft Society: measured, New Craft's cover fills its plate at **exactly 0px top and bottom gap**, so 8px of travel would swing a bare 8px strip into view (StoryBloom is already cropped past its edges at -10/-4). The six contained screenshots keep ≥20px clearance at full drift (PollenNav tightest at 20px, Mercor loosest at 122px). Inside the existing `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` + `@supports (animation-timeline: view())` block, and note the same longhand rule as `card-rise` — `animation-duration: auto`, never the shorthand.
- **SELECTED WORK hairline draws in (added 2026-07-26):** `.section-rule` wipes left-to-right (`scaleX(0)→1`, `transform-origin:left`, `--dur-slow`/`--ease-out`) the first time `.section-meta` enters view. Home-page only — `.section-rule` exists once in the whole codebase. **It is a one-shot IntersectionObserver transition, NOT an `animation-timeline: view()` scrub like `card-rise`, and that is the whole design decision:** `.section-meta` sits at document y≈855, which is ABOVE the fold on any viewport taller than ~860px, so a scrubbed reveal renders a *partially drawn* line on first paint and then just leaves it there — measured **17% drawn at 1440×900, 48% at 1000px tall, 79% at 1120px**. A shorter range does not rescue it (41.6% at 900px). Time-based means it always plays in full, whether it starts on screen or gets scrolled to. Same failure family as the documented fold-compression note about cards rendering at their start keyframe. **Three traps, all hit and fixed during the build:** (1) **the `transition` must live on `.visible`, not on `.rule-wipe`** — with both on `.rule-wipe`, adding that class animates the line from full width DOWN to zero over 400ms and the observer then retargets it to 1 mid-flight, so it wobbles near full width and the wipe never plays; declaring it only on the outbound state makes the collapse instant and the draw animated. (2) **observe the PARENT `.section-meta`, never the rule itself** — collapsed to `scaleX(0)` the rule has zero rendered area, so its `intersectionRatio` is permanently 0 and any non-zero `threshold` can never fire; it is unobservable precisely because it is hidden. (3) **the collapsed state is scoped to `.rule-wipe`, which JS adds at runtime**, so a JS failure leaves the line full-width rather than invisible; the CSS is additionally inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` and the JS guards on `reduce`. Verified: below-fold load → 0 then draws 0→0.51→0.83→1 on entry and stays drawn on scroll-back-up (one-shot, observer disconnected); above-fold load → plays and settles at 1. (4) **the observer needs `rootMargin: '0px 0px -25% 0px'` AND a `document.fonts.ready` gate.** Without the rootMargin the reveal fires at LOAD on any screen taller than ~860px (the header is at y≈855, already on screen), so the animation burns while the visitor is still reading the hero and is over before they scroll down — it "does not work" from the visitor's point of view even though it ran. Without the fonts gate the rootMargin is defeated anyway: until the webfont swaps, the hero is a different height and `.section-meta` sits ABOVE the trigger line, so the observer fires on that transient layout. Gating on `document.fonts.ready` (the same thing the hero scramble waits for) makes it read a settled layout. Verified at 1440×998: holds at scaleX 0 at scrollY 0, then draws 0→0.58→0.90→0.98→1 over ~400ms on scroll. **Two testing notes:** IntersectionObserver does NOT deliver callbacks when the browser-pane tab is not fronted — several "the observer never fires" readings during this build were that, not the code; and **`index.html` itself is never cache-busted** (the `?v=` only busts style.css), so a browser can serve stale HTML with none of the page's JS while the CSS is current — check `[...document.scripts].some(s => s.textContent.includes('<marker>'))` before believing a JS effect is broken. style.css **v=212→v=214**.
- **ui-skills polish pass (2026-07-26, first routed use of the UI Skills router — sources: `jakubkrehel/make-interfaces-feel-better` [MIFB], `emilkowalski/emil-design-eng` [Emil]; `leonxlnx/minimalist-skill` loaded and rejected as a light-mode generative protocol; `pbakaus/critique` discarded as harness-bound):** five changes, style.css **v=214→v=215**. (1) **Lightbox now animates (Emil):** it was `display:none→flex` with a `transition: opacity 0.2s` that NEVER ran (newly-rendered elements don't transition) — a full-screen jump-cut. Fixed with `@starting-style` + `transition-behavior: allow-discrete` (holds `display` through the exit fade), img settles 0.97→1 (motion-gated; modals scale from center per Emil's exception), **exit 160ms faster than enter 240ms**. **Longhands, NOT the `transition` shorthand, are load-bearing:** an unrecognized `allow-discrete` inside the shorthand would invalidate the whole declaration in older browsers; as a standalone `transition-behavior` line they drop just it and keep today's snap (verified working: mid-flight opacity 0.20 on open, display held at flex while fading 0.156 on close). (2) **`text-wrap: balance` on h1–h4, `pretty` on p/figcaption** (MIFB #10) — site-wide, zero prior usage; no-ops on the char-split home hero. (3) **Carousel dots: painted 24×4 bar moved to `::before` on a 24×20 transparent button** (MIFB #16) — 4px of vertical hit target was the hardest tap on the site; opacity states cascade to the bar so active/hover/`:active` rules needed no change; dots row stays 84px. 40×40 was NOT reachable without respacing the 11-dot rail — deliberate partial fix. (4) **Play cards get the home grid's `card-rise`** (Emil, cohesion — one motion vocabulary across sibling pages): same longhands/range on `.play-grid .play-card`; verified `.play-column`/`.play-grid` set no overflow so anonymous `view()` binds to the viewport (the `--card-view` trap does NOT apply here), and Play's first visible row loads past `entry 45%` so it renders settled, not faded. (5) **1px pure-white 0.08 hairline `outline` (offset −1) on `.play-card img` / `.deck-card img` / `.lightbox img`** (MIFB #11 — pure white, never tinted; outline not border so it draws on top and follows radius). **Deliberately NOT on home-grid imgs** — NOVA/Mercor are transparent PNGs where an outline would draw the box the alpha exists to avoid (verified home `outlineStyle: none`). Also: `.exp-date` got `tabular-nums` (adapted use — static dates, benefit is the About right-rail digit alignment, not layout shift) and the dead `.card-num` block was deleted (styled, zero HTML references). **Findings rejected in the same pass, do not re-apply:** Emil's sub-300ms rule vs the documented acknowledgment/scenery split; MIFB's concentric-radius formula vs the shipped 8/4 play-card radii (formula wants 24px outer — bubbly, off-brand); minimalist-skill's palette/serif core. the grid's dimensional discipline used to exist ONLY at 1440px — measured at 1024, Homewise's description ran 3 lines while PollenNav's ran 2, so their chip rows sat 24px apart and PollenNav carried **74px** of dead space below its chips vs Homewise's 21px (also broken at 1200 and 900). Cause: `.project-card` was `display:block`, so the grid's row-stretch slack landed as dead space *below* the chips with nothing to push them down. Fix is a 4-part height chain, all home-grid-scoped (`.project-card`/`.project-info` appear ONLY in index.html): `.project-card { display:flex; flex-direction:column }` + `.project-img { flex-shrink:0 }` (never compress the 16:9 plate) + `.project-info { flex:1; align-items:stretch }` (was `center`, which would vertically centre instead of filling) + `.card-meta { margin-top:auto; padding-top:14px }`. **Both `.card-meta` properties are required** — `margin-top:auto` does the pinning but collapses to 0 when there is no slack, so `padding-top` carries the original 14px minimum gap; do not merge them back into `margin-top:14px`. Result: `gapBelow` is a uniform 21px at 1440/1200/1024/900 (was 21/45/50/74). Where chip rows still start at different y (Homewise vs PollenNav at 1024), that is the chip row itself *wrapping* to two lines — bottoms stay aligned, which is correct. **Tradeoff accepted:** total card height is unchanged, but on a card with a shorter description the whitespace now sits *between description and chips* instead of below the chips. Side benefit: the two-line description ceiling is no longer a hard copy constraint (Mercor + New Craft had both been re-cut against it).
- **Sticky-stacked work rows — BUILT AND REVERTED 2026-07-26 (per user).** The 8 cards were wrapped in four `.work-row` divs that pinned under the nav at staggered tops (100/114/128/142px) so each row slid over the last, the covered row scaling to 0.955. Mobile was protected with `.work-row { display: contents }` (measured identical: 327px cards, first card y=588) and reduced-motion fell back to the flat grid. It worked, and the user rejected the look — the top of the viewport ends up permanently occupied by strips of previous rows, and because the cards lead with an image those strips are image fragments rather than a clean title edge. **Do not re-propose sticky/stacking the work grid.** Two findings worth keeping: (1) **`animation-timeline: view()` freezes on a pinned element** — a sticky element stops moving in the scrollport, so its view progress stalls at exactly the moment a stack effect should run; driving the scale needed a small rAF handler instead. (2) **Never fade a stacked card with `opacity`** (the user's call, and correct): transparency lets the row beneath ghost through, reading as a rendering bug rather than depth — scale is the only safe lever. Also: the recede progress must key off actual *overlap* (`(row.bottom - nextTop) / row.height`), not proximity to the next row, or a row starts fading while it is still the top card.
- **Progressive defocus on card exit — BUILT AND DELETED 2026-07-26 (per user).** Cards blurred (`blur(5px) saturate(0.6)`, no opacity) as they left the top of the viewport, via a second `view()` timeline animation (`card-defocus`, `animation-range: exit 0% exit 60%`) sharing `.project-grid .project-card` with the existing `card-rise`. It worked and cost nothing (pure CSS, no pinning, no added page height) — the user simply did not want it. **Do not re-add blur/defocus to the work cards.** One finding worth keeping if two timeline animations are ever stacked on one element again: **a timeline animation still FILLS outside its range**, so the two must own disjoint properties — `card-rise` owning opacity+transform while `card-defocus` owned only `filter` was required, or the exit animation clobbers the entry fade.
- **Project cards:** #1a1a1a background, 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.08) border, border glow on hover (box-shadow), image zoom + 3px lift on hover (`scale(1.04) translateY(-3px)`). Title 18px, year 16px right-aligned, description 16px muted with 8px gap. Description reserves 2-line min-height so the tag row below aligns horizontally across cards even when one description fits in a single line. **Thumbnail material language (2026-06-11):** floating screenshots cast a contact shadow on their plates (`.project-img > img { filter: drop-shadow(0 16px 28px rgba(0,0,0,0.16)) }`, deepens on hover — the direct-child selector keeps award badges/overlays exempt), and every flat plate fill uses PollenNav's two-radial light treatment in the card's own hue (`radial-gradient(ellipse at 30% 30%, LIGHT…), radial-gradient(ellipse at 70% 70%, DEEP…), BASE` inline on `.project-img`) — highlight upper-left, deeper pool lower-right, so the grid reads as six consistent lit scenes. New cards should follow both. StoryBloom is exempt from the plate treatment (full-bleed cover hides its plate). **NOVA is also an exception (2026-06-18): its plate is a flat `#FFFFFF`, NOT the two-radial treatment.** `images/nova-card.png` is a transparent product shot of the AR glasses with a soft drop shadow baked as if rendered on white, so the plate must be pure white — anything darker (incl. the radial treatment's lower-right pool, which lands right where the shadow sits) shows a light halo around the shadow. Keep NOVA flat white; do NOT "restore" the radial treatment.
- **Card meta chips:** `.card-meta` container holds 2–3 `.card-tag` chips below each project description. Tags are 11px uppercase, 1px stroke border (rgba 0.12), 4px radius, mute color (rgba 0.55). Pattern: `[Domain] · [Form factor] · [Status]` (e.g. `B2B SaaS · Dashboard · Shipped`, `Health · Mobile App · Award Winner`). Hidden on mobile (≤768px) since stacked cards already show full descriptions. **Project-type chip (`.card-tag--type`, added 2026-06-17):** a filled chip (bg + border `rgba(255,255,255,0.16)`, text `0.92`) that **leads** each home-card meta row, labeling the work `Professional` / `Personal` / `Academic` / `Freelance` (Freelance added 2026-07-31 for New Craft Society, per user). Set apart from the stroke descriptor chips by **fill weight, NOT color** — deliberate: the user asked whether to color it and the answer was no, because the site rule is *chrome stays neutral, color is reserved* (e.g. NOVA's `#0094FF`) and the colorful thumbnails already carry the grid's color (per-type color-coding was also rejected). Labels are authoritative from each case study's own meta Type field: Homewise + Litespace Coffee Chat = Professional, PollenNav = Personal, NOVA = Academic (its page says "Sponsored Academic", shortened to "Academic" on the card). StoryBloom + Countersign have no in-repo case study → labeled **Personal** by inference (Countersign = solo repo; StoryBloom = self-initiated 2026 product); revisit if either is really Professional/Academic.
- **Product Hunt badge on the Litespace Coffee Chat card (added 2026-09-02, per user "give litespace product hunt #1 litespace badge. find that product hunt #1 badge online").** THE CLAIM IS VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY, not taken on trust: Litespace was **#1 Product of the Day on 2 February 2023** with 275 upvotes and 211 comments, and **#5 Product of the Week**, per producthunt.com/products/litespace and its launch page `?launch=litespace-1`. The asset is **Product Hunt's own official embed badge**, `api.producthunt.com/widgets/embed-image/v1/top-post-badge.svg?post_id=litespace-1&theme=light&period=daily` (250x54 SVG, "PRODUCT HUNT / #1 Product of the Day" with the gold medal), **saved locally to `images/producthunt-1-litespace.svg` rather than hotlinked**, per the site's self-hosted-assets convention. **ENDPOINT GOTCHA worth keeping: `post_id` accepts the LAUNCH SLUG (`litespace-1`), not only a numeric id — the numeric id is not exposed anywhere on the product page, and every attempt to scrape one failed.** The other Product Hunt art on that page is `ph-static.imgix.net/badges/color/dailybadge1.svg`, a purple hexagon medal, which was rejected because it carries no wordmark and would read as an unidentified icon beside the iF / Red Dot / NY / European wordmarks. Rendered 120x26 in the standard `.card-awards` row (the row's 26px height scales the badge to 48%), top-left of the lavender plate; the Coffee card had no `.card-awards` div before, so one was added along with `position: relative` on its `.project-img`. **HONESTY FLAG RAISED WITH THE USER: the #1 belongs to the Litespace PLATFORM's February 2023 launch, while Coffee Chat shipped in September 2023, seven months later — so on a card titled "Litespace Coffee Chat" a reader could take the badge as the feature having won it.** The badge says "Litespace" nowhere, which cuts both ways.
- **Card award badges:** `.card-awards` is an absolutely-positioned (top:12px, left:12px) flex row inside `.project-img`. Default badge height is 26px (`.card-awards img { height: 26px }`). PollenNav uses this default for its row of 4 horizontal-wordmark badges (iF, Red Dot, NY, European). **Override for lone portrait badges:** when a card has a single portrait-orientation badge (e.g. StoryBloom's NY Silver 2026 medallion), set `style="height: 30px"` inline on that img — the lone medallion reads too small against a single thumbnail at 26px because portrait badges have ~2.5× less surface area than landscape wordmarks at equal height. 30px brings the visible area into the same order of magnitude as the surrounding card content without going as bold as 32–38px (which made the badge feel larger than the matching NY 2025 sibling on PollenNav). Parent `.project-img` must have `position: relative` for absolute anchoring to work. **StoryBloom badges updated 2026-07-22 (per user):** `images/reddot-winner-2026.svg` (Red Dot Winner 2026 stacked lockup, kept as vector; note the SVG has NO width/height attrs — fine in `<img>`, but a standalone/document render needs them injected) added ordered FIRST, before the NY medallion (order swapped per user). **Both StoryBloom badges use the `.card-awards` defaults (26px desktop / 16px mobile media query) as of 2026-07-22 (per user "same size as pollennav")** — the earlier inline `height: 30px` lone-medallion override was removed; inline heights beat the mobile media query and made the badges oversized on phones, so avoid inline badge heights on multi-badge rows. The StoryBloom cover was also replaced the same day with a user-recomposed 1920×1080 export (phones smaller, top-left clear for the badge row — the old cover's phones collided with the badges; rasterized from the user's Figma SVG via headless Chrome at 2×, downscaled to 1920 PNG lossless, 548KB, `?v=4` cache-bust on the img src). Full-bleed `object-fit: cover` treatment retained (a briefly-tried 65%-plate/Homewise-style cutout treatment was discarded when the new cover landed).
- **Chatbot:** Dark background, white icon, white rotating conic-gradient border (0.5 opacity). **The widget is a self-contained ~164-line block** (`<!-- Chat Widget -->` button + `.chat-panel` + its init script) pasted near the end of `<body>`, just before the pixel-dissolve script — it is NOT shared from style.css or a common JS file, so **every new page has to be given its own copy** (same class of omission as the GA snippet and the fontshare preconnect). **`nova.html` shipped without it and went ~2.5 months unnoticed** (added 2026-05-10, caught by the user 2026-07-28); `git log -S 'class="chat-toggle"' --all -- nova.html` confirmed it was never there rather than removed, and every other shared block on the page (GA, preconnect, favicon, back-to-top, `.page-end` footer, px-dissolve) was present. Fixed 2026-07-28 by copying the block verbatim from homewise.html. **The block is byte-identical on coffee / homewise / ticker / nova (md5 `dc03dc69dd0ab0dbf82e27e26950b2b3`, measured from `<!-- Chat Widget -->` to 3 lines past `chatMic.style.display = 'none'`) — pollen.html's differs and is the odd one out, so copy from one of the four, not from pollen.** Quickest audit for a missing widget: `for f in *.html; do echo "$f $(grep -c 'class="chat-toggle"' $f)"; done`. Note `/api/chat` 404s on the local python preview, so the panel opens but every message errors locally; test against production.
- **Layout:** Vertical single-column on about, single-column on play, 2-col project grid on home
- **Mobile:** Hamburger menu → full-screen overlay. No footer watermark on mobile.
- **About photos:** 4x2 collage grid, square aspect ratio (this is the **mobile** state of the photo deck — see below).
- **About photo deck** (`.deck` / `.deck-track` / `.deck-card` in `about.html` + inlined IIFE, added 2026-05-29): infinite editorial carousel of the 8 personal photos with three composable behaviors — **always-on auto-rotate + drag-to-rotate + an always-on hover "light shimmer" tilt**. Markup triples the 8 cards (24 total) so a full set always bleeds off both edges. Built from a reference (pearl-social-nine.vercel.app) but **adapted**: the reference hijacks the mouse-wheel because its carousel IS the whole viewport (`h-screen`, page non-scrollable); our deck sits mid-page, so wheel-hijack would trap scrolling. **We use drag-to-rotate instead** — a direction-intent guard commits to a horizontal drag (grab + momentum flick, friction 0.92) but releases vertical gestures back to the page (`touch-action: pan-y`), so scrolling is never trapped. Mechanics: continuous drift `SPEED = 48` px/sec via rAF with a sub-pixel accumulator (`acc += SPEED*dt`; `Math.floor` into `scrollLeft` so it never stutters) — **the drift NEVER pauses** (see history); seeded-middle on first paint (`offsetLeft + offsetWidth/2 - clientWidth/2`, in the load handler so no flash); symmetric wrap at `>=2*setWidth` / `<setWidth`. Per-frame magnetic focus (`focus = max(0, 1 - dist/halfSpan)`, `halfSpan = trackWidth*0.28`) written as `--focus` and driving `scale(0.82 + 0.22*focus)`, `opacity(0.62 + 0.38*focus)`, **squared**-falloff drop-shadow + warm-coral halo (`rgba(255,150,120,…)`, matches the footer-glow palette — deliberately NOT the reference's gold). **Hover tilt ("light shimmer"):** a global cursor (`mouseX/Y/mouseOver`) is tracked, and every frame `paintCards()` resolves which card the cursor is over and tilts it in 3D toward the pointer (`--tilt-rx/ry`, ±`TILT_MAX=11`°, lerped `0.14`) — recomputed per frame so the tilt tracks the cursor AND the drifting cards (the card shimmers as it passes under a still cursor; deck keeps moving). Requires `perspective: 900px` on `.deck-track` (the cards' DIRECT parent) — `overflow` forces `transform-style:flat`, so a preserve-3d chain from `.deck` can't reach the cards; without direct-parent perspective the tilt flat-skews ("just leans left"). Cards face flat at rest (rotate 0). Bottom-fade `mask-image` dissolves the deck into the page (use `filter: drop-shadow`, not `box-shadow`, so it renders through the mask). Sits at a balanced gap below the hero via `.deck { margin-top: -84px }` (pulls up into the hero's empty bottom padding so heading→deck ≈ heading→header; the shared `.page-hero` is untouched) — the mobile block resets this to `margin-top: 0` because the mobile hero is much shorter and -84px drags the grid into the heading. `.deck-track` uses `overflow-x: scroll; overflow-y: hidden` — the explicit `hidden` is required because `overflow-x:scroll` would otherwise force `overflow-y:auto`, making the deck a vertical scroll-container that traps the page's wheel. **Mobile (≤768px): the JS bails immediately** (`matchMedia('(max-width: 768px)')` → add `is-ready`, `return`) and CSS collapses the same markup to the original static 4×2 grid (`.deck-card:nth-child(n+9){display:none}` hides the 2 duplicate sets) — no drift/drag/tilt. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion` (static scrollable row). Deliberate exception to the "no motion on About" preference (which still holds for Skills/Tools chips) — the deck IS the focal surface here. **History (don't re-add the PAUSE):** a hover-*pause* (deck freezes on hover so you can hold-and-tilt a card, via a `driftFactor` eased 0↔1) was tried twice and removed — even eased, stopping/resuming the drift was the source of every "jump"/jolt the user reported. Decision (2026-05-29, after several back-and-forths): **the drift must never stop.** The hover tilt simply plays over the moving cards; at 48px/s a card crosses the cursor slowly enough to see the tilt without needing a pause. Keep it no-pause. **Hover captions were BUILT AND FULLY REMOVED 2026-08-17 (per user "maybe delete the text"). The deck is a wall of photographs with no words, as before. Do not rebuild this without being asked.** It is worth knowing what was tried, because four versions shipped and each was rejected for a different reason: (1) **bottom-scrim text** in Neue Montreal on a black gradient bar; (2) **handwriting** (`Bradley Hand` rendered to transparent PNG) placed per-photo with `--cx/--cy/--cr` — the user asked for this, then declined using his OWN hand ("do not use my handwritting"), which is what killed it: the argument for handwriting was that it would be the site's one non-transferable element, and as a FONT it keeps the single-typeface rule-break while losing the payoff that justified it; (3) the **hero g2 ramp as the ink** (the user's suggestion) — its stops are tuned to GLOW on `#0a0a0a`, so over a photograph red/pink/deep-blue all land mid-dark and the text sinks; lifting the ramp 45% toward white fixed legibility but stopped reading as the site's gradient; (4) plain **20px Neue Montreal** on each photo's measured darkest region. **Legibility treatments rejected on looks, all worth not retrying: `brightness(0.6)` on the whole photo ("do not make the whole image darken, just the text part"), and a soft black aura baked behind the glyphs from a blurred glyph mask ("looks ugly" — it read as a smudge).** **The one durable technique from the whole thread: placement can be MEASURED rather than eyeballed** — a script reproduces each photo's `object-fit: cover` crop into the 280×373 card and scans it with a hand-rolled summed-area table for the darkest, quietest window that fits the note (`mean luma + 0.6 × std`); `/usr/bin/python3` has PIL but no numpy. Measured luma per photo ran 43 (food) to 110 (jenga, which has no genuinely dark region). **Two CSS traps hit along the way, both general:** a hover-only element's OFF state must live OUTSIDE the `@media (hover: hover)` block AND out-specify anything setting `display` on it (`.deck-card img.deck-cap`, not `.deck-cap`, since `.deck-card img` is (0,1,1)) — otherwise on touch it falls back to visible normal flow and dumps its content into the tile; and **a `matchMedia('(hover: hover)')` probe reads as PASSING while that is broken, so always screenshot the non-hover breakpoint.** Caption copy was written by reading the actual image files, which caught a live falsehood: `talk1.jpeg`'s alt said "Giving a talk" when the photo is shot **from the audience** and the speaker is not Chang. **That fix is KEPT — alt is now "At a design talk"** — and is the only surviving trace of the whole thread. style.css **v=239→v=249** across 10 pages.
- **Play grid overflow bug (fixed 2026-07-26, UI-craft review P2 — long-standing, NOT a regression):** `.play-grid` used `grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr`, and bare `1fr` means `minmax(auto, 1fr)` — a track can never shrink below its widest content. Whisper XR's 11-dot carousel rail has a hard min-content of 456px (11×24px dots + 10×8px gaps + 112px arrow-clearance padding), so the right column floored at ~498px: at 800px viewport the columns measured **274px vs 498px** and the right column ended at x=852 on an 800px screen, clipping ~72px off every right-column card. It was invisible in casual testing because `html { overflow-x: hidden }` swallowed the overflow (the page never showed a scrollbar). Fix = `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) minmax(0, 1fr)` + `flex-wrap: wrap` on `.carousel-dots`. **Keep the `padding: 24px 56px` on `.carousel-dots`** — that 56px is clearance for `.carousel-arrow`, which is `position:absolute; left/right: 0`; zeroing it (as a naive min-content fix would) puts dots under the arrows. Verified 375→1920: columns equal at every width, zero clipped cards, carousels functional, desktop 656px columns unchanged. **Lesson: `overflow-x: hidden` on `html` hides layout overflow — test with `document.documentElement.scrollWidth > innerWidth`, not by looking for a scrollbar.**
- **Pinned horizontal Play — BUILT AND REVERTED 2026-07-26 (per user).** `.play-grid` was wrapped in `.play-pin` > `.play-stage` (sticky, 100vh, overflow hidden), `.play-column` collapsed with `display:contents` (the same trick mobile already uses for `--mo`), and the 20 cards became one 9,008px flex row panned sideways by a rAF handler reading `.play-pin`'s offset. It worked cleanly — stage pinned, strip panned 0→−7,662px, no overflow, carousels/lightbox/video-autoplay all intact, mobile untouched — and the visual was genuinely good (the GPT Image 2 pieces read far better at 420px in a gallery row than in the masonry). **Rejected on scroll cost:** page height went 7,400px → 9,566px, of which **8,564px is captive pinned scroll** (~25–30s of continuous scrolling to pass the section, versus a few seconds to skim the masonry), and only 3 cards are visible at a time instead of ~6. Same tension that killed the sticky-stack: more cinematic, less scannable — and for a page whose job is "here are 20 things I made", scannability wins. **Do not re-propose pinning/horizontal-scrolling the Play grid.** Untried dials if it ever comes back: pan faster than scroll (travel ratio 0.5×) or narrower cards (340px).
- **Play projects:** 2-column flex masonry via `.play-column` children of `.play-grid`. Each project wrapped in `.play-card` (var(--surface) bg, 1px border, 20px padding, 8px outer radius). Image on top via CSS order, info block below with 20px margin-top on `.play-info`. Info block is `.play-info-top` (flex row: h3 left + `.play-year` muted 16px right, 8px margin-bottom) followed by `<p>` description. Pattern mirrors home page work cards. Inner image radius 4px (concentric with 8px card). Click images to zoom via lightbox. `.play-card--desktop-only` (hidden ≤768px) thins the mobile stack: **Whisper XR** plus **four more cards added 2026-06-21 (per user) — Buffering Ocean, No Signal Summer, Escape Velocity, and TickerPulse** all carry the class, so they show on desktop but are hidden on mobile (HTML-only; the class already exists in style.css — no version bump). Carousel arrows sit below image in the dots row (bottom: 24px within 84px min-height dots area) — symmetric 24px spacing on both sides. **First-click jump fixed 2026-06-21:** carousel slides are `display:none` + `loading="lazy"`, so a non-active slide's image wasn't fetched until first shown; on the first arrow click the track collapsed (323px→0px on Whisper XR) then snapped back when the lazy image painted. Fix = give each slide `<img>` intrinsic `width`/`height` attributes (Whisper `1920×1080`, TickerPulse `1920×1258`) **plus `height:auto` in the inline style** so the aspect-ratio reserves the box before load. The `height:auto` is REQUIRED — without it the `height` attribute acts as a fixed presentational hint and the image renders at full 1080px (caught + fixed mid-change). HTML-only, no CSS/version bump; lazy-loading preserved. Same pattern applies to NOVA's `.final-carousel` if it ever shows the same jump. **Video cards grouped at the bottom 2026-06-22 (per user "all the video related things should under"):** the 4 video-player cards — **Futuristic Starship Cockpit, I Believe in a Thing Called Love, About the Teddy Bear, UN/FOLD** — were moved to the **end of column 2** so they're the last 4 cards on mobile (the masonry flattens col1-then-col2, so "bottom of mobile" = tail of col2). To keep **desktop** balanced after pulling 3 of them out of col1, the two desktop-only image cards **No Signal Summer + Escape Velocity were moved into col1** — they're `display:none` ≤768px, so this rebalances desktop (10/10 cards, ~6% height diff) with **zero mobile effect**. **Midjourney stays in col1** (it has `<video>` clips in its grid's bottom row but reads as AI-art, not a video — user's call; the question was scoped to the 4 true video players). HTML-only reorder of whole `.play-card` blocks, no CSS/version bump. **Curated into themed desktop sections 2026-06-23 (per user, "for desktop" reorders — SUPERSEDES the simple video-grouping above):** the desktop reading order is now **GPT2 art → UI screens → other → film videos**, with the 7 GPT Image 2 cards distributed ~evenly across both columns at the top (4 col1 / 3 col2) so the "AI art" band leads both columns, then the product/UI work, then the rest. **Final layout — col1:** Scope · Buffering Ocean · Octocrab · Golden Flux · Midjourney *(AI-art cluster; Midjourney sits directly under Golden Flux per user)* → Luma · Redbrick Coffee · TickerPulse · Market Street Booklet Kit · Missing Pavilion. **col2:** The Last Human Gestures · No Signal Summer · Escape Velocity · Whisper XR *(Whisper directly under Escape Velocity per user)* · CoPatible · Futuristic Starship Cockpit · Mirror · I Believe · About the Teddy Bear · UN/FOLD. **Key per-user placement rules baked in:** Whisper XR directly under Escape Velocity; **one carousel per column** (Whisper col2 / TickerPulse col1) for height balance — "TickerPulse should not be last" was read as keeping it in col1 repositioned off the bottom, NOT moving it to col2; the swap of Market Street↔Mirror put Mirror under Starship in col2 and Market Street in col1, which **dropped the earlier "Starship above Market Street" rule** (Starship now sits above Mirror — user-approved). **Starship Cockpit is no longer treated as a bottom-grouped "video"** — it's the interactive Figma-Make piece, placed mid-col2; only the 3 true film videos (I Believe / Teddy Bear / UN/FOLD) stay grouped at the very bottom (still last on mobile). Columns balance to **~2% height diff** (10/10 cards). All HTML-only reorders of whole `.play-card` blocks; no CSS/version bump. **Two more swaps 2026-06-23 (per user):** Market Street↔Mirror (above) then **Missing Pavilion↔I Believe** — so col1 now ends `…TickerPulse · Market Street Booklet Kit · I Believe in a Thing Called Love` and col2 ends `…Mirror · Missing Pavilion · About the Teddy Bear · UN/FOLD`. Both desktop columns now end on a film video; balance landed at **~1.3%**. **Independent mobile order — DECOUPLED 2026-06-23 (per user "give mobile its own independent order"; SUPERSEDES every "mobile = col1-then-col2 flatten" note above):** mobile no longer derives from the desktop DOM order. Each `.play-card` carries an inline **`style="--mo:N"`** (mobile-order int) in play.html, and the `@media (max-width:768px)` block renders `.play-card { order: var(--mo, 99); }`. **Desktop never consumes `--mo`**, so the 2-column desktop layout and the mobile sequence are fully independent — change one without touching the other. (This replaced the old `.play-card{order:1}` + `.play-card--m-feature{order:0}` mobile rule; `.play-card--m-feature` is now an **unused class**, left in markup, harmless.) **To reorder mobile:** edit the `--mo` numbers in play.html only. **To reorder desktop:** move `.play-card` blocks between/within columns as before; set the moved card's `--mo` too if its mobile spot should change. The shipped mobile order (themed, videos grouped at bottom): Scope · The Last Human Gestures · Octocrab · Golden Flux *(GPT2 art)* · Midjourney · Luma · Redbrick Coffee · CoPatible · Mirror *(UI)* · Missing Pavilion · Market Street *(other)* · **Futuristic Starship Cockpit · I Believe · About the Teddy Bear · UN/FOLD** *(all 4 videos last — note Starship IS grouped with the films on mobile here, unlike desktop where it sits mid-col2)*. The `--mo` map in play.html also assigns the 5 desktop-only cards logical values (they're `display:none` ≤768px so it's moot, but keeps the sequence sane if ever un-hidden). style.css bumped **v=191→v=192** across all 8 pages. **"Fish Ocean" card added + "CoPatible" deleted 2026-06-24 (per user):** Fish Ocean (a predator-prey ASCII-terminal ecosystem, "Made with Claude Code", live link `fish-ocean-gray.vercel.app`) is a **desktop-only** card placed in **col2 directly after Futuristic Starship Cockpit** (user first said "under TickerPulse", then corrected to after Starship). It's a **click-to-play** video card (NOT an autoplay loop): `<video src="images/fish-ocean.mp4" controls preload="none" poster="images/fish-ocean-poster.jpg" playsinline width="1280" height="804">` + a `.play-link` "Play it here ↗" — mirrors the homewise-live pattern so the **44MB** mp4 never auto-loads (`preload="none"` keeps it at readyState 0 until clicked; it's also excluded from the autoplay observer which targets `video[preload="metadata"]`). **Why click-to-play, not a ~3-4MB autoplay clip like Midjourney's:** the source is a 44s / 3456×2168 / 268MB screen recording; transcoded with `avconvert -p Preset1280x720` (720p = 2× the ~620px card display; 1080p was overkill → 86MB) to 44MB, poster grabbed via `qlmanage -t -s 1280` then `sips`→jpg. The user's copy had an em dash ("old course project — now living") → changed to a **comma** per [[feedback]] no-em-dash rule. **CoPatible was deleted** the same turn (whole `.play-card` block removed; `images/copatible.jpg` left as a harmless orphan). Net effect: Fish Ocean (+1 col2) then CoPatible (−1 col2) leaves **col2 back at 10**, columns **10/10, ~0.8% balance**. Mobile is unaffected by Fish Ocean (desktop-only); CoPatible's removal drops the mobile stack to **14 visible cards**. HTML + 2 new media files, no CSS/version bump. **Golden Flux + Escape Velocity HIDDEN 2026-08-14 (per user "maybe hide" — softened from "delete"):** both get the Buffering Ocean treatment, inline `display:none` on the card div, markup kept for easy restore (Golden Flux was mobile-visible, so the mobile stack drops to 13; Escape Velocity was already desktop-only). **"Robot Operator Console" card added 2026-08-14 (per user):** a Unity humanoid-robot warehouse sim with an industrial control interface (battery, joint load, gait stability, acknowledgeable alarms; overhead/robot-eye/VR views), "Made with Unity and Claude Code". **Desktop-only click-to-play video card in col1 directly ABOVE Redbrick Coffee (moved there from before-Market-Street 2026-08-14 per user)** (`--mo:18`, moot while desktop-only) — placed col1 for balance (the grid was near-perfect before it; the new card costs its own ~484px wherever it goes, col1 lands 6.1% vs col2's 6.3%; first shipped in col2 after Fish Ocean at 10% measured pre-image-load and moved same day). Follows the Fish Ocean pattern exactly: `controls preload="none" poster` (excluded from the autoplay observer), 24s source 3176×1780 31MB → `avconvert Preset1280x720` → `images/robot-console.mp4` 11.4MB 1280×716, poster `robot-console-poster.jpg` via the documented Swift frame-0 recipe. The user's copy had two em dashes, recomposed to colon lists per the no-em-dash rule. **"Running Dog" card added + UN/FOLD moved + Buffering Ocean hidden 2026-06-27 (per user):** (1) **Running Dog** — a SolidWorks tri-gear tilting mechanism (the project's real name; year **2020**, user-confirmed) added in **col2 directly under Missing Pavilion** (`--mo:15`, tie-breaks to sit right after Missing Pavilion on mobile too), a regular image card (shows on mobile). Copy: "A tri-gear mechanism modeled in SolidWorks. Rotating the primary crank transfers motion to two more gears in one linked movement, and a secondary crank on the central bar converts that rotation into vertical motion through a crank pin." Image `images/solidworks-trigear.jpg` (192KB, 1920px) from `~/Downloads/Frame 18 (1).png` — note the user **pasted a white-gap version in chat but the Downloads export already had BLACK gaps** between the 3 composite panels (what the user wanted), so no recolor was needed. **PIL is under `/usr/bin/python3`, NOT the default `python3` (now 3.14, no PIL).** (2) **UN/FOLD moved to col1** (appended after I Believe at col1's bottom) — both desktop columns now end on a film; `--mo:20` unchanged so it stays last on mobile (desktop-only change, zero mobile effect). (3) **Buffering Ocean hidden everywhere** — added inline `display:none` to its already-`--desktop-only` card (so now hidden on desktop too, kept in markup for easy restore). Net desktop balance: **10/10 visible, ~1.7%**. HTML + 1 new image, no CSS/version bump.
- **Play video clips:** Videos live in `images/` (no separate `videos/` folder). Markup pattern is `<video loop muted playsinline preload="metadata" style="…">` — **no `autoplay` attribute**: a small inline IntersectionObserver at the end of play.html calls `.play()` when a clip scrolls near the viewport (200px rootMargin) and `.pause()` when it leaves, so the ~10MB of clips never load on initial page view (2026-06-11 payload pass). `muted + playsinline` are still required for browsers to allow inline programmatic play; iOS stays non-fullscreen. Use the same `aspect-ratio:16/10;object-fit:cover;border-radius:8px` styling as the surrounding images so they sit in the same grid. The lightbox JS in `play.html` only handles `<img>`, so videos won't click-to-zoom — that's fine. Used in the Midjourney card to extend the 3-col still grid into a 3x3 grid where the bottom row is moving clips.
- **Case study patterns (shared):**
  - `.deliverable` — h2 caption + tight description above a full-width product image (Final Design walkthroughs on pollen.html and ticker.html, matches Coffee Chat Final Deliverables h2 style)
  - `.iteration-strip` — paired V1+V2 grid with full-width Final below (PollenNav Explorations)
  - `.voc-quotes` — 3-col grid of user quote cards with theme tag (TickerPulse Research)
  - `.pairing-annotated` — image left + annotation notes right with dashed arrows (Coffee Chat)
  - `.tool-cell` — name + small thumbnail in compare-table cells (Coffee Chat competitive analysis)
  - `.research-image-row` — flex row for 2+ research process images at the **same height but proportional widths**. Each `<figure>` has inline `style="flex: <aspect-ratio>"` (e.g. 1.14 / 1.35 / 1.66) and `flex-basis: 0`, so widths scale with each image's natural aspect ratio while heights stay equal. Each figure holds an `<img>` + a small muted center-aligned `<figcaption>` underneath as a label. Used in NOVA Research (field activities / interviews / affinity map).
  - `.problem-grid--stack` — 1-column variant of `.problem-grid`. Use when stacked full-width `.problem-card`s read better than 3 narrow cards — e.g. when the cards sit directly under a 3-image row and a 3-col grid would imply a 1:1 mapping that doesn't exist. Used in NOVA Research insights.
  - `.eval-matrix` (on `.compare-table`) — evaluation matrix table with `table-layout: fixed`, smaller padding/font, and a fixed 168px first column. Each cell pairs an `.eval-dot eval-dot--good/mid/bad` (8px circle, green/yellow/red — `#22C55E`/`#FACC15`/`#EF4444`) with the value text. Apply `.eval-row--highlight` to a `<tr>` to highlight the chosen approach (subtle blue tint `rgba(0,148,255,0.08)` + 2px `#0094FF` left accent on first cell). Used in NOVA Solution Evaluation Matrix.
  - `.comp-grid` — 2x2 image grid for competitive analysis (gap 16px, each image 16:9, center-aligned figcaption underneath). Stacks to 1 column under 768px. Used in NOVA Competitive analysis (Meta Orion / Snap Spectacles / Meta Ray-Ban / Apple Vision roadmap).
  - `.hud-grid` — 3x2 image+title+description grid for design exploration (e.g. HUD text styles). Title above image, image (16:9), description below. To highlight the chosen option, add `.hud-grid__item--selected` — applies a 3px `#0094FF` border + 1px rgba halo to **just the image** (not the whole card). Used in NOVA Challenge 1.
  - `.hud-example` — annotated example pattern: a numbered legend list (`.hud-example__legend` with `.hud-example__num` blue circles) placed **above** the image, with each `<li>` reading "[circle] **Label:** description." The matching numbered markers appear on the image itself. Used in NOVA Challenge 1 to walk through HUD Text in action.
  - `.phone-flow` — light-gray (`#E8E8E8`) panel matching the Companion app onboarding bg, holds 2–3 mobile screens in a 3-col grid (`.phone-flow__panel`). Each phone is wrapped in `.phone-flow__cell` (figure + dark `figcaption`) so labels sit **inside** the panel below the phone in small dark text. Used in NOVA Challenge 2 (Joint Onboarding / Safe Zone Setup / Safety Controls).
  - `.final-carousel` (on `.carousel` with `[data-carousel]`) — reuses the Whisper XR carousel pattern from `play.html` (`.carousel-track` / `.carousel-slide.active` / `.carousel-dots` / `.carousel-prev|next`) inside case-study Final Design sections. Modifier class only adds the standard 32px top-margin in `.project-content`; the carousel CSS is shared. The init JS (~25 lines: queries `[data-carousel]`, builds dots, wires prev/next click handlers) must be inlined in each page's script — there's no shared JS file. Used in NOVA Final Design (Companion app onboarding / Navigation mode / Freedom mode).
  - `.solution-diagram` — abstract system diagram for showing the product concept without real UI. CSS-only with two inline SVG glyphs (e.g. AR glasses + phone) flanking a dashed `#0094FF` connector with arrows. Each side has a label + small muted sub-label. Sits on a `.surface` panel. Used in NOVA Solution as a hero visual above the descriptive cards. Pattern: two abstract endpoints + bidirectional connector = "system with two parts that talk to each other."
  - `.partner-logos` — small partner-credit strip ("In collaboration with [logo] [logo]"). Logos render in **full original color at 60px CSS height** — no brightness/invert flatten, no opacity dim (changed 2026-05-11 per user; the brand colors carry signal). **Both logos must still be tight-cropped** so equal CSS `height` produces equal visible mark size — when a logo has internal padding (e.g. a tagline below the wordmark), the visible letters render smaller than the partner's at the same 60px box. Use `img.getbbox()` in PIL to verify the tight crop, or swap to a tighter-cropped source file. Used in NOVA Overview to credit CCA + UNICEF Innovation.
  - `.iter-example` — before/after iteration row (NOVA Usability Testing + Challenge 3). One `<h3>` title (optional), then 2-col grid with `<figure>` cells. Each cell: image + figcaption that starts with `<strong class="iter-example__mark iter-example__mark--bad|--good">Before:|After:</strong>`. The `::before` pseudo-element renders a red ✕ pill (`rgba(239,68,68,0.18)` bg + `#EF4444` text) or green ✓ pill (`rgba(34,197,94,0.18)` bg + `#22C55E` text) before the label. Two iter-examples stacked = paired before/after pattern from the framer reference. Also used in Challenge 3 (without mascot vs with mascot AR view). **Coffee Chat Iteration 1 & 2 were converted to this `.iter-example` format 2026-06-21 (per user, "same format as NOVA Challenge 3" with red/green labels)** — replacing the old `.before-after`/`.before-after-label` blocks (now unused CSS). Because the Coffee widgets are **transparent PNGs on a lavender (#D0D1FD) plate**, each cell wraps the image in a `.iter-plate` helper: a square (`aspect-ratio:1/1`) lavender plate with the image **absolutely positioned** (`inset:24px`, `object-fit:contain`) so a tall "before" screenshot can't blow the square out (an in-flow `%`-height image was overriding aspect-ratio and stretching the plate). NOVA's own iter-examples stay bare (full-bleed AR images, no plate).
  - `.mascot-exploration` — full-width figure for displaying the mascot character lineup. Inner `<img>` has `background: #E5E5E5` + padding so transparent-bg mascots render on a light gray "marketing display" panel matching the Companion app onboarding bg. Used in NOVA Challenge 3 below the before/after pair.
  - `.img-placeholder` (with `data-label` attribute) — dev-time placeholder for upcoming images. Dashed-border gray box with the label text from `data-label` shown via `::before`. Modifiers: `.img-placeholder--16-9` (AR view aspect), `.img-placeholder--mascot` (wide 16:7 panel). Swap with `<img>` once the real image lands.
- **Back-to-top button (`.back-to-top`, decoupled from the side-nav 2026-07-28):** the case-study back-to-top used to be hidden by `@media (max-width: 1440px) { .side-nav, .back-to-top { display: none } }` — a rule added way back in `7f936fc` whose comment ("hide side nav on smaller screens where it would overlap content") only ever justified the **side-nav**, which lives in the left margin. The button is `position: fixed` bottom-right and overlaps nothing, so it had been swept into the selector by accident and was invisible on **every common laptop width (1440 / 1366 / 1280)** — it only appeared above 1440px, which is why it looked fine in wide-window screenshots and "disappeared" on a normal laptop. The 1440 rule now hides `.side-nav` alone, and an explicit `.back-to-top { display: none }` was added to the ≤768px block so **mobile behaviour is unchanged**. Verified at 1700 / 1440 / 1024 (visible, opacity 1, hit-testable, `bottom: 280px` clearing the chat toggle at 220px desktop / 96px below 1136px) and 375 (hidden, no horizontal overflow). style.css **v=220→v=221**. **Gotcha:** the toggle JS is `scroll`-event-only with no initial call, so on a mid-page reload the button stays hidden until the first scroll — pre-existing, and it also means a probe must `dispatchEvent(new Event('scroll'))` after `scrollTo` or it reads a false negative.
- **Chat teaser:** Bottom-right tooltip bubble showing "Hey, I'm Chang's AI. Ask me anything." **Fires on FIRST SCROLL as of 2026-07-26** (was a 4s timer — at ~1280px viewports the bubble overlapped the hero headline, the rubric audit's one first-screen flag; scroll-triggered means it appears over the work grid, never the hero). `{ once: true, passive: true }` scroll listener; still hides after 6s, once-per-browser via localStorage, hidden on mobile (≤768px). A visitor who never scrolls never sees it — accepted trade.
- **Play lightbox:** Click any image inside a `.play-card` to expand. Dark 0.95 overlay, 8px radius close button (var(--radius)) with glassmorphism matching nav. Esc or click-outside to close.
- **Footer glow palette (2026-06-15, hero reflection):** vertical linear gradient — transparent → blue rgba(30,122,255,0.68) @30% → pink rgba(255,48,176,0.64) @52% → red rgba(255,40,70,0.60) @70% → amber rgba(255,190,35,0.64) @88% → amber rgba(255,190,35,0.64) @100% (**bottom stays warm, NOT transparent** — a transparent bottom stop caused a black gap at the page's bottom edge; fixed 2026-06-15). Reverse-orientation reflection of hero g2 (see Footer above). Replaced the old "Dusk" palette (violet/coral/purple/peach) when the hero moved to g2. To revert the whole gradient direction back to the old neutral footer, restore that Dusk radial set.
- **Footer pixel dissolve (`.px-dissolve`, 2026-06-15):** an animated "pixel dissolve" on the **footer only** — a `<canvas class="px-dissolve" data-edge="top" data-stop=".footer-big">` inside `.page-end` (right after `.footer-glow`) paints twinkling dark square "gaps" (`#0a0a0a`, 20px cells) over the **static** glow's top edge, dissolving the blue→dark transition into pixels **above "Say hello"** (`data-stop` keeps the band's bottom above `.footer-big`; `data-edge="top"` dissolves upward into the dark). The gradient holds still — only the squares animate (the AWS-Summit-card look the user referenced). One inline engine, **byte-identical on all 8 pages** (md5-matched), drives it via `requestAnimationFrame`, **pauses off-screen via IntersectionObserver**, has a `prefers-reduced-motion` static-frame fallback, is resize-aware, and is **shown + animated on mobile (≤768px) as of 2026-06-18** (the whole footer block used to be `display:none` on mobile; see Footer). A static-on-mobile variant was tried and reverted the same day — kept **animated everywhere** for one consistent signature; the battery/accessibility cases are already covered because the loop is IntersectionObserver-gated and still falls back to a static frame under `prefers-reduced-motion`. Canvas `z-index:1` (above glow 0, below content 2); the band sits above the text so it never occludes it. **Hero was deliberately left SMOOTH (no dissolve):** the hero gradient trails off right where the hero text sits, so an edge-dissolve there reads as "cut off" with a faint gradient clip-edge line — the footer's glow is a *bounded block* with a clean edge, so only it takes the effect cleanly. A hero version (frozen gradient + dissolve + removing the "Open to new opportunities" pill for room) was tried **and reverted 2026-06-15** (cut-off/line problem + it fought the pill for space); the **footer-only dissolve is the shipped decision** — a distinct, deliberate footer "signature" (hero/footer differing in treatment is fine — they're never co-visible and share the warm→blue palette; user OK'd it citing YC-site footers that differ from their hero). `style.css?v=156` across all 8 pages. To remove entirely: delete the `.px-dissolve` canvas + the engine `<script>` from each page and the `.px-dissolve` CSS rules.
- **Favicon (`favicon.svg`, updated 2026-06-15):** a warm→blue **vertical linear gradient** orb (#FFD600 → #FF1E3C → #FF3CB0 → #1E78FF) matching hero g2, replacing the old peach/coral/violet radial. Single file linked by all 8 pages; carries `?v=2` cache-bust on the link (favicons cache hard — new visitors get it immediately, returning visitors after a hard refresh).
- **Cache version (2026-06-15):** `style.css?v=159` set **uniformly across all 8 pages** (previously scattered 46–146, then 147, 150→151 footer `.page-end` restructure, 152→156 footer pixel-dissolve, 157→159 mobile-hero gradient + headline fixes), so every page busts cache. **Note:** the CSS is cached hard per `?v=` — when changing `style.css`, bump the version on the page you're previewing or the browser serves the stale stylesheet (a `?cb=` on the HTML URL alone does NOT bust the linked CSS). The **og-image was regenerated 2026-06-21 to the hero g2 palette** (warm→blue with the hero's true-red band, `?v=4`) — the old violet/coral cohesion gap is now closed. **v=160 (2026-06-17):** home-card project-type chips (`.card-tag--type`). **v=161 (2026-06-17):** Homewise pitch-deck enrichment (`.hw-stats` market band + `.hw-flow` map) + the strengthened type-chip fill — both set uniformly across all 8 pages. **v=162–167 (2026-06-17):** iterations of the Homewise Problem/Solution rework — stat band boxed→boxless→**3 cards**, scannable pain headlines (`.voc-quotes--pain`), `.hw-flow` removed, enriched Solution + `.solution-coda`. (The 2026-06-17 Track clip + live-demo embed were HTML/asset-only, no further bump.) **All 8 pages were at v=167.** **v=168–171 (2026-06-18, this session):** mobile-footer work (glow + pixel-dissolve brought to mobile and made animated everywhere; glow band 130→180px) and the home-grid Coffee Chat ↔ Countersign swap + `.project-card--desktop-only` mobile-hide rule. **All 8 pages were at v=171.** **v=172–178 (2026-06-20):** `.case-cover` consolidation, page crossfade view-transition, About polish pass. **v=184 (2026-06-20, this session):** About rework — Skills/Tools deleted; Experience/Education/Recognition → inline "name · detail · date" rows (subgrid removed); Testimonials moved above Books/Podcasts; `--text-soft` mid-tier grey; `.media-section--end` footer gap. All changes are About-scoped (other pages' rendering unaffected); version set uniformly across all 8 pages. **All 8 pages were at v=184.** **v=185 (2026-06-20, this session):** Books + Podcasts hidden on mobile (`.media-section{display:none}` ≤768px). **All 8 pages were at v=185.** **v=186 (2026-06-20, this session):** nav "Get in touch" CTA removed site-wide + header "Specular Glass" material (top-lit fill, `saturate(1.4)` blur, specular box-shadow stack, `nav::before` gradient ring, opaque fallback) + persistent `.nav-active` underline tick. **All 8 pages were at v=186.** **v=187 (2026-06-20):** scroll-condense added. **v=188 (2026-06-20):** scroll-condense made width-only (no height/position change, per user) + fixed the Specular-Glass `box-shadow`/`nav::before` leak onto the case-study `.side-nav`. **All 8 pages were at v=188.** **v=189 (2026-06-20, this session):** scroll-condense REMOVED entirely (user "too annoying") — CSS block + 8 inline scroll scripts deleted; static Specular Glass + active underline + the `.side-nav` leak fix remain. **All 8 pages were at v=189.** **v=190 (2026-06-20, this session):** nav width 760→700px (static "after-shrink" size kept, with the lighter "before-shrink" glass opacity). **All 8 pages were at v=190.** **v=191 (2026-06-21):** Coffee Chat Iteration 1 & 2 before/after converted to the `.iter-example` red/green format + new `.iter-plate` square-plate helper. **All 8 pages now at v=191.** **v=223 (2026-08-01):** Mobbin vibe pass (card titles 21px, 72px hw-spread figures, About eyebrows + the later-reverted featured quote). **v=224 (2026-08-01):** interaction six-pack + 404.html — see those bullets. **v=222 (2026-07-31):** premium-research quick-win pass (unified label token, type-ramp middle, hero blur 32, hero meta row, live status pill, footer info line; the mono-tier trial that shipped alongside was REJECTED and reverted 2026-08-01) — see the Premium-research bullet. **All 8 pages now at v=222.**
- **Interaction state layer (added 2026-07-26, UI-craft review P3, end of style.css):** before this the 84KB stylesheet had ZERO `:focus-visible` rules, ZERO `:active` rules, and exactly one `outline` declaration (`outline:none` on `.chat-input`) — keyboard users got Chrome's stock #005FCC ring, an off-system blue. Now: (1) a global `:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid rgba(240,240,240,0.9); outline-offset: 3px }` — **white per the chrome-stays-neutral rule, NOT #0094FF**; `outline` (not box-shadow) so it never collides with component shadows and follows border-radius; placed at the END of style.css to win the tie against `.chat-input{outline:none}`; verified with real Tab keypresses (programmatic `.focus()` does NOT trigger `:focus-visible` — test with actual keyboard input). (2) `:active { scale(0.96) }` press feedback on small controls only (carousel arrows/dots, hamburger, lightbox-close, chat buttons) — large cards deliberately excluded, scaling a 660px card on press reads as a glitch. (3) `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on body. (4) Four touch-leaky hover rules (`.play-card:hover`, `.photo-wrap:hover img`, `.carousel-arrow:hover`, `.lightbox-close:hover`) wrapped in `@media (hover: hover)` — untreated, a tap on mobile left the card's border+glow stuck lit while scrolling. All additive; new interactive components should get focus-visible for free (global rule) but need their own `:active` + hover-gating. style.css **v=193→v=194** (v=193 was the same-day tablet-rail + play-grid fixes).
- **Scroll-driven card reveals (added 2026-06-11, end of style.css):** pure-CSS progressive enhancement — `.project-grid .project-card` rises 18px + fades during viewport entry via `animation-timeline: view()`, `animation-range: entry 5% entry 45%`, gated behind `@supports` + `prefers-reduced-motion`; unsupported browsers get the site unchanged. **Two traps, do not regress:** (a) use animation **longhands** with `animation-duration: auto` — the `animation` shorthand's implicit 0s duration instantly finishes a timeline-driven effect (fill-mode then pins the end keyframe; looks like "animation does nothing"); (b) **`body` must NOT get `overflow-x: hidden`** — a non-visible overflow makes `<body>` a scroll container, and every `view()` timeline attaches to it instead of the viewport, silently breaking all scroll-driven animations. Horizontal clipping lives on `html` alone (identical clipping; verified no horizontal overflow). Comments at both spots in style.css explain this. **History + current state:** cross-document view transitions first shipped as a **paired `view-transition-name` card→cover MORPH** on 2026-06-11 and were removed the same day per user (the card thumbnails and the covers are *different* images, so the morph read as a glitchy crossfade-zoom). **Re-added 2026-06-20 as a plain root CROSSFADE (NOT the morph)** — `@view-transition { navigation: auto }` + custom `::view-transition-old/new(root)` animations at the end of style.css (old fades out 0.3s; new fades in 0.34s; **PURE OPACITY, no transform**; keyframes `vt-fade-out`/`vt-fade-in`). **The root snapshot must NOT translate:** a 12px "fade-up" rise was tried first but it lifted the `position:fixed` chrome — the floating nav, the chat button, the case-study side-nav — 12px on every navigation (fixed elements ride the root snapshot, so the nav visibly jumped), so it was changed to a still crossfade (fixed 2026-06-20). Site-wide on same-origin navigation (work card→case study, nav links, back-to-home), progressive enhancement (unsupported browsers navigate instantly), gated off under `prefers-reduced-motion`. **Do NOT re-add the paired-name card→cover morph — that's the rejected version; the user chose the crossfade.** **Also do NOT re-add the 12px root fade-up** — it makes the fixed nav jump (see above). style.css bumped v=172→v=173 (first ship) → v=174 (rise removed).
