- **SEO / AI-readability layer (added 2026-07-26, improvement-research P6):** `robots.txt` (allow all + sitemap pointer — deliberately NO Disallow for ticker.html, or crawlers could never see its noindex), `sitemap.xml` (7 canonical pages: / about play coffee pollen nova homewise; excludes ticker/coffeeslide/og-gen), `llms.txt` (markdown site+projects summary for AI assistants — update it when the project lineup or roles change, same trigger as the chat prompt), self-referential `<link rel="canonical">` on all 9 HTML pages (`.html` variants are canonical since internal links + og:url use them; coffeeslide's canonical is the clean `/coffeeslide` matching its og:url; canonicals resolve the `/nova`-vs-`/nova.html` duplicate routes from vercel.json), JSON-LD (Person on index.html — jobTitle/address/alumniOf/awards/sameAs LinkedIn+X/knowsAbout; CreativeWork on the 4 case studies with each page's own meta description), `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` on **ticker.html** (deliberately-unlinked page was crawlable out of context) and **og-gen.html** (internal tool that ships to prod), and index.html's meta description upgraded to carry name/role/location ("Chang Mou is an AI-first product designer in San Francisco…" — og:/twitter: descriptions kept as the shorter social copy on purpose). New pages need: canonical + sitemap entry + (if a case study) CreativeWork JSON-LD.
- **Vercel project:** chang-mou-portfolio
- **Domain registrar:** Squarespace Domains (domains.squarespace.com)
- **Analytics:** Google Analytics 4 (Measurement ID `G-RRQ4XH93F3`) injected in `<head>` of all six HTML pages. Any new page needs the gtag snippet added.
- **Font loading:** every page carries `<link rel="preconnect" href="https://cdn.fontshare.com" crossorigin>` immediately before the Fontshare stylesheet link (the font binaries live on cdn.fontshare.com, a different origin than the api.fontshare.com CSS — preconnect shortens first-visit FOUT). New pages need this line too. index.html loads weights 400,500,700 (600 was never used in CSS and was trimmed); other pages load 400 only.

Redesigned 2026-06-12: hero-mesh hues filling the ENTIRE frame per user ("do not let black come in") — deep-violet linear base under four wide corner pools, full-bleed inset so blur never shows an edge; reads violet→coral with no dark areas (straight-copied hero alphas over #0a0a0a rendered too dark as a static frame) + centered two-line lockup — "AI-first product designer." (white) / "Building what's next." (muted 0.5). **Card carries no other text** per user ("do not say other things") — the earlier status pill, sub-line, and "Chang Mou" signature were removed; don't re-add them on regen. Referenced via og: and twitter: meta tags on all eight pages. New pages need the same meta block. **Regenerated 2026-06-21 to the hero g2 palette** (the `.mesh` in `og-gen.html` now stacks the hero's exact g2 bands — yellow → **true red `rgba(255,30,58)`** → pink → blue as wide horizontal bands so the red reads as strongly as the live hero. A first pass used a soft pink-red corner pool that washed the red out (user: "my hero's gradient has some red, why this one does not"), fixed same day by mirroring the hero's `.hero-orbs--g2` band colors/alphas. Replaced the violet/coral mesh that no longer matched the site). **To regenerate:** edit the `.mesh` in `og-gen.html`, sync it to `/tmp/portfolio/` (preview server on 8888), then render with headless Chrome — `"/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome" --headless=new --hide-scrollbars --force-device-scale-factor=2 --window-size=1200,630 --virtual-time-budget=6000 --screenshot=/tmp/og.png "http://localhost:8888/og-gen.html"` (the `--virtual-time-budget` lets the Fontshare webfont load) → 2400×1260 PNG → `sips -z 630 1200 /tmp/og.png -s format jpeg -s formatOptions 95 --out images/og-image.jpg`. (The older `node /tmp/og-snap.mjs` puppeteer path is gone — its `/tmp/ogsnap` deps were cleared; Chrome headless replaces it.) **Cache-busting:** the og:image/twitter:image URLs carry a version param (`og-image.jpg?v=4` as of 2026-06-21, all 8 pages) — bump it whenever the image changes, because X/LinkedIn cache cards per-URL for ~a week and X no longer has a force-refresh tool (LinkedIn can be forced via Post Inspector).

## Dev Notes
- Node.js v24.14.0 installed via nodejs.org
- Preview server serves from `/tmp/portfolio/` — sync files there after edits
- Git initialized — commit before major changes
- Cache-busting: current `style.css?v=68` on play.html, v=48 on index.html — bump version after CSS changes. Keep version consistent per page.
- GitHub CLI: `/tmp/gh/gh_2.89.0_macOS_arm64/bin/gh` (not in PATH, use full path)
- Framer reference site: https://aware-acknowledge-592065.framer.app/
- Preview server: launched via `.claude/launch.json`, which now runs **`python3 .claude/serve.py`** - the script lives IN THE REPO and serves the repo directory directly. **NEW WRINKLE (2026-08-06): the preview harness sometimes resolves `launch.json` from the PRIMARY checkout instead of the worktree — and the primary at `/Users/changmou/Desktop/portfolio 2026` is months stale (pre-2026-08-02 config demanding `/tmp/portfolio/serve.py`) and holds uncommitted parallel work (do NOT pull or edit it). **Recovery when the server fails with "can't open /tmp/portfolio/serve.py" (hit again 2026-08-20, fix improved): recreate `/tmp/portfolio/serve.py` as a SHIM whose `ROOT` is the worktree's absolute path** (threaded, dotfile-403, chdir ROOT), then preview_start. **Do NOT rsync the worktree into /tmp/portfolio** — that was the old recovery and it reintroduces exactly the stale-copy confusion that moving serve.py into the repo was meant to kill (screenshots taken before the rsync lands). The shim satisfies the stale primary's config path while still serving the live worktree, so there is one copy of the files and no sync step. It is a /tmp file, so expect to recreate it whenever /tmp is wiped. The durable fix is the user pulling main in the primary checkout someday. **BOTH serve.py copies carry RANGE support as of 2026-08-29 (`206 Partial Content`, single ranges) — it is LOAD-BEARING, not an optimization: without it Chrome cannot honor `preload="metadata"`, so homewise's clips fall back to FULL downloads (~165MB per cold load), the load event hangs for minutes, and the browser pane's tools refuse to run ("page is still loading") or wedge outright, which reads as a broken page. If the shim is ever recreated from memory, recreate it FROM `.claude/serve.py` (swap ROOT to the worktree path) so Range support comes along.**** **Changed 2026-08-02 after `/tmp/portfolio/serve.py` was wiped twice in minutes**, breaking the preview both times; serving the worktree also removes the rsync-after-every-edit step and the whole class of stale-preview bugs it caused. `ThreadingHTTPServer` is still REQUIRED (single-threaded serves video-heavy pages sequentially and they look broken), and the handler 403s dotfile paths so `.git`/`.claude` are not served. The old `/tmp/portfolio` staging copy is obsolete; ignore the sync instructions below. The script `chdir`s to `/tmp/portfolio` before starting `http.server` to avoid getcwd permission errors. Sync files there after edits. **Use `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer`, NOT the single-threaded `TCPServer`/`SimpleHTTPRequestHandler` default (fixed 2026-06-27):** the single-threaded server serves one request at a time, so pages with many large video clips (homewise ≈135MB across 7 clips, the Play page's clips) download sequentially and crawl — the clips render as empty 150px boxes and look "disappeared" (`networkState:2`/`readyState:0` while they queue). Threaded server serves them in parallel and they load fine. This is a **preview-only** issue (Vercel serves in parallel; production is never affected). If clips look missing in preview, suspect the server is single-threaded before suspecting the markup. **`/tmp/portfolio` also gets wiped periodically** (lost `serve.py` AND most of `images/` twice on 2026-06-27) — when the dev server fails to start or images 404, re-sync from the worktree (`rsync -a --exclude=.git --exclude=.claude ./ /tmp/portfolio/`, NO `--delete` or it removes `serve.py` which lives only in /tmp) and recreate `serve.py` (threaded) if missing.
- Do not push to git unless user explicitly asks
- **Portfolio audit checker:** When the user asks for portfolio suggestions or feedback, reference the rubric from `~/.claude/skills/portfolio-audit/SKILL.md` (from https://github.com/hey-stefan/portfolio-audit). Cite which principle each suggestion comes from. When suggestions are personal interpretation rather than direct rubric items, say so explicitly.
- **UI Skills router (added 2026-07-26):** `.claude/skills/ui-skills/SKILL.md` is the routing skill from `npx ui-skills` (ibelick), checked into the repo so it loads every session. Before visual/layout/typography/motion/interaction work, route to the **smallest useful** expert skill: `npx ui-skills categories` → `npx ui-skills list --category <c>` → `npx ui-skills get <slug>`. Prefer 1 skill, never more than 3. Registry is 185 skills / 47 authors / 26 categories as of 2026-07-26. **Two gotchas recorded in the SKILL.md itself:** (a) `get` resolves top-level slugs ONLY — companion files a skill links to (e.g. `emilkowalski/review-animations` → `STANDARDS.md`, which holds its actual easing/duration tables) return "Skill not found", so such skills load only partially and the missing numbers must NOT be invented; (b) several skills carry generic rules that **contradict deliberate decisions in this file** — most sharply "UI animations must be under 300ms" versus the documented acknowledgment/scenery split (hover answers in 160ms, decorative travel like the 800ms image glide and 400ms plate light stays slow on purpose). Sort any skill's findings into "real" vs "conflicts with a documented decision" and defend the latter rather than silently complying.

**Case study section order (May 2026):**
- **RESTRUCTURED 2026-07-27 (audit finding #3) — the three orders below SUPERSEDE the pre-restructure lists.** All three legacy studies went to **10 sections**, matching homewise.html's shape. **Compression and renaming ONLY:** the process-then-payoff arc is untouched (the April-2026 "show the win first" inversion stays reverted) and NO TL;DR/summary layer was added (rejected 2026-07-26 as repetitive). **The rename is the point:** side-nav links like "Exploration 2" / "Challenge 1" advertise process volume rather than what got decided, and they are the single loudest Behance-template tell — a reader clocks the era from the nav rail before reading a word. Every new name was lifted from an h2 already on the page. Do NOT reintroduce numbered section labels on any case study.
- **Coffee Chat mentor-framework pass 2026-07-28 (HTML-only, style.css untouched, no version bump)** — same framework and same workflow shape as the homewise pass. **Measured premise correction: coffee.html is LIGHTER than homewise.html (1114 vs 1432 words), not heavier.** Its problem was never volume, it was beat density and repetition, so the pass cut *beats*, not words (net −30). Shipped: **(1) The three `.problem-card`s were one causal chain split into three parallel boxes** (card 3 causes card 1, card 2 is its consequence) and named no difficulty at all, so a hiring reader concluded the problem was easy and every decision downstream read as taste. They now name the three real hardnesses, each lifted from a sentence already on the page downstream: "Nobody wants to go first" (social pressure, from the Research caption), "A good pairing has to be inferred" (thin signals, from the GPT-3 section), "Scheduling has two sides" (from the user-flow intro + the flow chart's own ✕ tags). **Deliberately NOT homewise's "A user problem / A data problem / One connected system" triad** — a literal clone across two case studies makes the template visible. The GPT-3 sentence and the user-flow intro were trimmed of what the cards now carry. **(2) The four `.goals-grid` cards were DELETED** — they were a table of contents for the three sections that follow, and the fourth ("Cross-Department Matching") was a promise the page never pays off. The section, its label, h2 and the two-week constraint sentence all stay; that sentence is now the section's whole payload. Same defect PollenNav's Challenge cards had. `.goals-grid`/`.goal-card` CSS is now unused on this page (still used nowhere else — safe to leave). **(3) "What I did" bullets 3-4 stopped spoiling two payloads** (the pairing reveal ~148 lines early, and both removals). **(4) The removal license moved out of the Reflection** — the page makes four removals and the sentence licensing them all sat after every one, so it now extends the two-week constraint at its source ("…and anything that added friction had to go"); Timeline field became "2 weeks · Sep 2023" so the best scoping fact is on the first screen. **(5) Overview leads with the bet** ("Coffee Chat is the 0-to-1 feature of Litespace, …" — was behind a sentence defining SaaS), and the prose `.highlight` moved off the market gap onto the scoped statement, since it is the page's only skim affordance in prose. **(6) The "pressure free" thesis, asserted 4× and argued 0×, is now attached to the two decisions that deliver it**: default-to-Going ("so nobody has to be the one who says yes") and no-reason-on-decline ("Asking for one would put the pressure back"), plus silence-as-a-supported-exit promoted out of an SVG branch label into the sentence beside it. Prose, **not** a stated-principles list — that shape was rejected on homewise. **(7) REAL BUG FIXED: the 2026-07-27 restructure renamed the side-nav but never the on-page `.section-label` eyebrows**, so two consecutive sections both read "Exploration", the third read "Iteration", and clicking "The user flow" in the rail landed on a section headed "Exploration". All three now match the rail verbatim (anchor ids `exploration-1/-2`, `iteration-1` deliberately unchanged). **Lesson: when renaming sections, grep BOTH `side-nav-link` and `section-label` — they are separate strings and the 2026-07-27 pass only did one.** Also gave the last two Final Design deliverables the headings they were missing entirely. **Killed by the judge, do not re-propose:** importing the deck's $4.5M stat onto the page, minting an 11th section for the insight, collapsing/reordering the five Final Design deliverables, deleting either before/after pair, and adding interview evidence that does not exist.
- Coffee Chat (13 → 10): Overview *(absorbs the old Impact grid, so 63%/200+ land in the first screen — Homewise has no separate Impact section either)* → Problem → Research → **Goals and constraints** *(the two-week pre-rebrand constraint folded in as one line)* → **Meaningful pairings** *(ex-Exploration 1)* → **The user flow** *(ex-Exploration 2)* → **What testing changed** *(both Iterations merged, both before/after pairs intact)* → Final Design → Design System → Reflection.
- PollenNav (12 → 10): Overview → **Recognition** *(the old "Impact" — it holds four award cards, NOT outcome stats, so it was renamed rather than folded into Overview; folding would open the case study on a wall of awards, against the no-award-bragging rule)* → Problem → Research → **Goals and challenges** → **Making the data readable** *(Explorations 1+2 merged, both solve data legibility)* → **Approachable, not clinical** *(ex-Exploration 3, the visual-identity beat, genuinely separate)* → Usability Testing → Final Design → Reflection. **Structural note:** the three Challenge cards mapped 1:1 onto the three Explorations and each exploration intro already restated its own challenge, so Challenges was functioning as a table of contents for the next three sections; it now reads as part of the brief. **KNOWN UNFIXED:** `images/pollen-playbook.jpg` still renders "How might we equip people like Maya…" *inside the image* — the HTML text was removed but the phrase survives in pixels, and it needs a re-export from Chang. Lesson: verify rendered output, not just markup, when removing copy.
- TickerPulse keeps its original order (Background → Problem → Research → Solution → Final Design → Reflection). Its Solution block is a narrative bridge to the finals; moving Final Design would orphan it.
- UNICEF NOVA (12 → 10 on 2026-07-27): Overview *(absorbs the old Impact grid)* → Problem → Solution → Research *(absorbs Ideation as its closing beat)* → **HUD text** *(ex-Challenge 1)* → **Independence vs tracking** *(ex-Challenge 2)* → **The mascot** *(ex-Challenge 3)* → Usability Testing → Final Design → Reflection. **Two real fixes beyond renaming:** (a) **duplicate stats removed** — Impact and Usability Testing both carried an 80% card with near-identical wording ("more comfortable" vs "feel more comfortable") and both counted the same 20 parents; Impact now leads under Overview, Usability's duplicate grid is deleted, and its one unique fact ("5 major problems solved") was folded into that section's prose. Verified 80% now appears exactly once. (b) **Ideation's "How might we…" was a text HMW** (unlike PollenNav's, which is baked into an image) and is now a declarative stating the same four requirements. The pre-restructure order was: Overview → Impact → Problem → Solution → Research → Ideation → Challenge 1/2/3 → Usability Testing → Final Design → Reflection. Mirrors the framer reference: Solution sits right after Problem so the system is named early, then Research justifies it. Uses `.problem-grid--2col` modifier (CSS: `grid-template-columns: repeat(2, 1fr)`) for the two-card "For children / For parents" Solution layout. Each Challenge ends with a parent-quote in `.table-caption` style. **Trimmed 2026-06-18 (portfolio-audit pacing pass — surface the AR work sooner):** the **Design Goals section was DELETED** (the "Four success metrics" `.goals-grid` 4-card block + its `#design-goals` side-nav link; it was the weakest-earning section, restating the independence-vs-reassurance balance Problem/Solution already establish, so Ideation's "Design opportunity" now hands straight to Challenge 1 — `.goals-grid`/`.goal-card` CSS is now unused, left in style.css). **Usability Testing was reduced to a validation summary:** its two `.iter-example` before/after blocks (Simplifying AR dialogue, Enhancing safety setup) were **removed** (they re-covered Challenge 1/2 ground), and the impact-grid's "2 Major iterations: simplifying AR dialogue, rebuilding safety setup" stat became **"5 — Major problems solved from usability feedback, from navigation cues to the hardware concept"** (an earlier 5-item `.project-list` of those problems was added then removed per user; the heading "Iterative feedback to refine the experience" + intro were KEPT). **That sentence was rewritten 2026-08-04 because the promise had gone unpaid.** Removing the list was fine while the Impact grid still carried the `5 — Major problems solved` card, but the **2026-07-27 dedup pass deleted that card** (only 80%/20+/50+ survived, in Overview), leaving a sentence that promised an enumeration the page delivered nowhere. The intro now ends "…and it pushed the hardware concept toward thinner, more child-friendly glasses" and the "five major problems" phrasing is gone. **The five (user-supplied 2026-08-04) are: navigation cues clarified · safety alerts, SOS and danger warnings · companion-app onboarding, safe zones and parent-child contact · reduced visual clutter · hardware refined toward thinner, more child-friendly glasses. Four of the five restate Challenge 1 / Challenge 2 / the Final Design deliverables, which is exactly why the list was cut — only the hardware refinement is new information, so it is the only one named.** Do NOT restore the full list. **Lesson, and the second recorded instance of it: a later pass can invalidate an earlier pass's premise.** 2026-06-18 removed the list *because* the stat card carried the number; 2026-07-27 deleted the stat card without knowing that; neither pass could see it had left a dangling promise. Same family as the homewise "audit recommendation's rationale invalidated by a later edit in the same session" note. Challenge 3's own `.iter-example` (mascot before/after) is untouched.
- **Reflection convention, unified 2026-08-04 across all four case studies (per user; HTML-only, no version bump).** Three rules now hold everywhere. **(1) The h2 is a CLAIM, never "What I learned."** The 2026-07-27/28 passes converted section h2s to claims but missed Coffee Chat and PollenNav, which still trailed off into the generic label. **(2) The body reflects the WHOLE case study and closes on what Chang learned AS A DESIGNER** — not one section's decision. **This rule exists because the first attempt got it wrong and the user corrected it.** The pattern was derived from Homewise and NOVA as "insight → that is why [specific decision] → takeaway", which produced a Coffee reflection aimed at the design system and a PollenNav one aimed at a single exploration — both single sections near the end of their pages, i.e. NARROWER, not broader. What actually makes Homewise and NOVA work is that they generalize the case study's **central** tension into a transferable craft lesson. **(3) Length follows from covering the arc**, roughly 75-125 words; padding a narrow reflection is the wrong fix. Final state: NOVA 124w "The most protective version was the one no child would wear" · Coffee 98w **"When the barrier is social, the design work is subtraction"** (walks the four removals — explained match, no message thread, default-to-Going, no reason on decline — then generalizes, with the mid-rebrand tokens-and-components lesson as a subordinate second beat) · PollenNav 87w **"The hard part was not accuracy, it was making invisible data feel tangible"** (20 interviews → "not another symptom tracker" → the V2 density bars that read as precise and still failed → legibility and feeling are the product) · Homewise 74w. **GOTCHA hit on both files: promoting a body sentence to the h2 REQUIRES trimming it from the body**, or the rename manufactures the heading/body redundancy the 2026-07-28 lesson warns about. **`ticker.html` still says "What I learned" and is deliberately left** (unlinked + noindex). **Coffee's removals thread WAS used here with explicit user approval** — note this does NOT reverse the 2026-07-28 decision, which moved the removal-*licensing* sentence ("anything that added friction had to go") out to Goals-and-constraints; generalizing the removals into a designer lesson is a different move, and the licensing sentence stays where it is. **Consequence: Coffee's Reflection h2 changed twice on 2026-08-04, so any note quoting "Design for the identity that is coming" as the heading is stale — that line is now the Design System section's territory only, and `coffeeslide.html` still carries BOTH the old Design System heading and a generic "What I learned" Reflection slide, so the deck is divergent from the page in two places.**
- Earlier (April 2026) we tried promoting Final Design to right after Research as a "show the win first" move. Reverted in May 2026 — the classic process-then-payoff arc reads stronger for these two case studies. **A "summary block after the hero" skim layer for NOVA + PollenNav (2026-07-26 improvement-research P8) was also REJECTED by the user ("feels repetitive" — the heroes already carry the intro beats). Do not re-suggest either variant; the case studies keep their single process-then-payoff narrative with no TL;DR layer.**

## User preferences noted

- No em dashes in descriptive copy (prefers periods/splitting sentences)
- **Card → case-study hero-sub continuity (added 2026-07-27, per user):** every case study's `.project-hero-sub` must OPEN with its home card description's first clause **verbatim**, then continue with something the card does NOT say. Shipped state: Homewise "AI command center for homeowners." → mechanism; PollenNav "Street level pollen guidance for safer city travel." → the city-averages contrast; Coffee Chat "AI powered coffee chat for intentional, pressure free pairings across hybrid teams." (opening only, nothing added); NOVA "AR navigation system for children that balances independence and parental reassurance." → the CCA/UNICEF context. **The user chose this over full duplication** (the alternative offered): the shared opening confirms the click, the divergent tail avoids making the reader re-read a sentence they finished one second ago. **Two consequences to preserve:** (a) they can never be byte-identical because card copy drops the terminal period (rule below) while hero subs are full sentences; (b) Coffee Chat's hero sub deliberately **drops the 63%/200+ stats** — the card carries them as the hook and the Impact grid restates them 200px down, so repeating them in the sub was pure duplication. Also note the site's house style leaves compounds unhyphenated ("AI powered", "Street level", "pressure free") — the coffee hero sub was un-hyphenated to match its card rather than hyphenating the curated card copy. If a card description is ever rewritten, its hero sub opening must be updated to match.
- Home work-card descriptions drop the TERMINAL period (caption convention, unified 2026-07-23 per user): internal periods where grammar needs them, but the description never ends with one (e.g. "…manages repairs from one workspace" not "…workspace."). All 8 cards audited to comply; keep new cards consistent.
- Do not brag about awards in copy; let the Recognition/Impact sections do that visually
- **About-page Background bio (`about.html`) rules, learned 2026-06-13 — apply to any bio rewrite:** (a) **No organization names in the bio** — orgs live in Experience/Education below; don't repeat them up top. (b) **Don't overclaim "agentic"** as the identity — only 2 of 7 projects are truly agentic (Homewise, Countersign); StoryBloom is generative, TickerPulse/Coffee Chat are AI-powered tools, PollenNav/NOVA aren't AI. Lead with broad "AI products" and keep the agentic angle at the *project* level (cards/case studies), not the bio headline. (c) **Figma and code (Claude Code) are separate systems** — never phrase as a Figma-to-code pipeline ("take the work all the way into code" is WRONG); Chang mocks up in Figma on some projects and builds directly in code on others (e.g. Homewise). Keep Figma positive (the role market wants Figma + Cursor, not anti-Figma).
- Keep "Play" as page name (not "Side") — matches the craft/experimentation signal the checker rewards
- Do not push to git unless explicitly asked
- Check sync to `/tmp/portfolio/` before screenshots (some resets happen)
- No category tags on play cards (denied this approach earlier)
- No accordion on play cards (tighten text instead of hiding it)
- Copy targets a YC/startup hiring audience — keep resonant buzzwords like "first-principles thinking." Don't suggest cutting them as "designer-cliché." Judge phrases by the intended reader, not by general good-writing principles.
- Avoid decorative numbering (`01 — SELECTED WORK`, `01/02/03/04` card chips) on sections that don't have a real numbered series — implies an ordering that doesn't exist. Section labels can use the editorial hairline-rule treatment (`.section-meta` with `.section-label-text` + `.section-rule`) without the number.
- Don't put a 3-column image row directly above a 3-card grid when the images and cards aren't 1:1 related — readers will infer a mapping that doesn't exist. Either label each image (figcaptions) and switch the cards to a different shape (e.g. `.problem-grid--stack` for full-width stacked cards), or use a different image count.
- **No reading-measure cap on About prose.** A `max-width: 62ch` on `.about-block p` + `.testimonial-quote` (UI-craft review P5, 2026-07-26) was applied and REVERTED same day per user — "too much white space". The user prefers full-container-width text on About over the editorial 45–75cpl measure; the typographic-ideal argument was heard and overruled. Do not re-suggest. (The other P5 parts stayed: `.hero-sub` line-height 1.75→1.6 and `.project-info p` 1.4→1.5 with its paired `min-height` bump.)
- No motion on About → Skills + Tools chip rows. Tried a Bubbl-inspired stagger drop-in + hover wobble on 2026-05-11, reverted. The section is scan-and-leave, so constant micro-motion competes with readability instead of adding signal. Keep chips static; if motion is wanted, put it on hero or play-page surfaces instead.
- No grain/noise texture over the dark gradients. Tried an SVG-turbulence film-grain overlay on the hero mesh + footer glow on 2026-06-11, removed same day — gray noise over the near-black background reads as glitch/static ("the color difference is too obvious"), not as craft texture. If texture is ever wanted, it would need soft-light blending scoped to the gradient glow only, but the default answer is no.
- No overlay furniture on work-card thumbnails. Hover arrow chips (↗/→ glass circles) and on-image status pills (glass "Shipped"/"Speculative Design" bottom-left) were prototyped 2026-06-11 and reverted same day — added chrome on the thumbnails reads as clutter. The accepted direction for card upgrades is **image/light physics** (contact shadows, lift, plate light scenes — all kept), not added elements. Award badges top-left are the one sanctioned overlay.
- Work cards link to the live experience (or case study), never to a GitHub repo — for a design-hiring audience the card's single click should land in the work itself. When the code is worth surfacing, put the repo URL in the chat prompt (`api/chat.js` PORTFOLIO PROJECTS list) instead. Decided 2026-06-12 when weighing Homewise's repo vs live link (chose live/case study over repo). Homewise's card now points at its case study `homewise.html` (which itself ends in a live-demo CTA), once the clips landed; Countersign's card → live demo; both repos surface only in the chat prompt + CLAUDE.md.
- Date format conventions for `.exp-date` in Experience / Recognition / Education on about.html: single year (`2026`) = one-time award or event; comma list (`2025, 2026`) = multiple discrete wins of the same award in different years (e.g., NY Product Design Awards Silver — PollenNav 2025 + StoryBloom 2026); en-dash range (`2024 – 2025`) = continuous tenure or one award covering an academic year (e.g., Bernard Osher Scholarship). Don't mix — using a range for two discrete wins implies a continuous award; using a comma list for a continuous period implies two separate awards.
